## Made during step 3a

D288 to D299 were decided on 5 Nov 2026 while building step 3a, the database and its privacy rules; D299 came from an outside review. Questions for the owner are in `PLAN.md`.

### D288. How the database is laid out

Decided 5 Nov 2026 in step 3a. Builds ARCHITECTURE.md 3.1 to 3.6.

- **Where.** A Supabase project in London (D17, D19), changed only by the migrations in `supabase/migrations` (D297). The tables are in Supabase's `public` part, which the app will reach from step 3c with the privacy rules on every row. The checks the rules use are in a part of their own, `app`, which the app's web address never shows.
- **A table for every kind of record the phone keeps:** accounts, balance updates, series, payment changes, ticks, debts, budgets, spends, savings goals, pay profiles, usual weeks (`pay_weeks`), pay adjustments, settings (D289) and practice changes (D294), and the links a person keeps to themselves (D291). Then people, plans, plan members and invites (ARCHITECTURE.md 3.2), and the history: edits and notices (ARCHITECTURE.md 3.5, D296).
- **Each column is a field of the engine's own record,** so a plan can come back exactly as it was left (brief Part 10). Money is whole pence, as a whole number a phone can count exactly (never floating point); rates, percentages and hours are exact text, such as "1.1612"; dates are dates. Times the engine compares, such as when a tick was made (D33), are kept exactly as the phone wrote them, such as "2026-10-30T09:14:05Z", because the engine compares them as written and a database's own way of writing a time would change their order.
- **The sync fields** (ARCHITECTURE.md 3.1) are on every plan record. The database fills in who made it and when, who last changed it and when ("Changed by Tom", brief 4.14), its version and the plan's change number, whatever a phone sends. A phone sends the id, the plan, the record's place among its kind (`list_order`, so lists come back in the order they were made, D168), the edit that saved it and whether it's removed.
- **Each tick and each change keeps its payment number** (PLAN.md item 4), so an undo in step 3c can check it's still the same payment.
- **The plan's change numbers** are kept apart from the plan's own row, so a tick isn't a change to the plan. There's one count for the plan's shared records and one for each person's private records (D299), so a phone asks for everything after the numbers it has in each. Saves on one count wait for each other, one at a time, so two phones never get the same number (ARCHITECTURE.md 7).
- **Nothing is ever deleted by a person.** Removing a record marks it removed, which is a change like any other, so the same privacy rules apply (ARCHITECTURE.md 3.1).
- Budgets keep the time the phone made them (`made_at`) for the same-day order (D12, D107), apart from the database's own `created_at`.

Why: ARCHITECTURE.md 3 sets out the records; these are the choices it left open, each made so the plan on the server is exactly the plan on the phone.

### D289. Each person's main pay and setup, private to them

Decided 5 Nov 2026 in step 3a. Adds to D14, D155 and ARCHITECTURE.md 3.2.

- The plan's nation for bank holidays is on the plan, shared by everyone in it.
- Each person's main pay (D14), and how far their first-time setup has got (step 2e), are in their own `settings` row for the plan, always private to them. ARCHITECTURE.md 3.2 put the main pay on the plan member record, which both people can read.

Why: Jess's main pay can be on her private account, so naming it where Tom can read it would tell him something about that account.

### D290. Privacy is filled in by the database

Decided 5 Nov 2026 in step 3a. Builds ARCHITECTURE.md 3.6 ("The database fills this in itself from the account, so the app can't forget").

- A record takes its privacy from what it's on: a balance update, a budget or a payment from its account; a change or a tick from its payment; a spend from its budget; a usual week from its pay profile; overtime from its pay. Whatever a phone sends is replaced.
- An account, a debt or a pay profile is shared or private to the person it belongs to, as they choose; a goal kept in a private account is private to the same person (D65). Someone can make something private only to themselves, never to their partner.
- Until shared plans come in step 3f, something private stays private ("sharing-comes-later"), and a record can't move to another account. Money between a private account and a shared one is always two linked halves (D16), never one payment.
- Every check runs as the person making the change, under the privacy rules, so naming something they can't see is refused exactly as naming something that doesn't exist. Nobody can find out what's private by trying.

Why: one forgotten field would put Jess's private payment where Tom can read it (ARCHITECTURE.md 7). Filled in by the database, it can't be forgotten.

### D291. A shared record never names a private one

Decided 5 Nov 2026 in step 3a. Builds D65 and PLAN.md item 5. Changes ARCHITECTURE.md 3.3's `mirror_of`.

- A record everyone in the plan can read never names a record only one person can read: the database refuses it ("link-to-private").
- So Jess's payment from the joint account to her private credit card is a shared payment with no debt named on it, and the link to her card is kept in `private_links`, which only she reads. Tom's copy of the payment shows its name, date and amount, and nothing about her card (D65). The same goes for a payment into a savings goal of hers, and for a shared tick made inside her card's Update from statement (D62).
- A debt or goal link is kept for the payment's family, so a split keeps it, as the engine does (D49).
- The shared half of a private transfer, "From Jess", doesn't name its private half. The private half, on Jess's account, names the shared half instead (`mirrored_by`), so only Jess's phone can join them up (D94).
- A shared payment can't use a private pay profile or usual week either. Whether Jess's job details should be private when her pay goes into the joint account is a question for the owner in `PLAN.md`.

Test: test 6 in `supabase/tests`.

Why: PLAN.md item 5 asks for the partner's copy to leave the link out. A rule that refuses every such link, rather than hiding one column, can't miss one.

### D292. "Can view only" changes nothing shared

Decided 5 Nov 2026 in step 3a. Builds D51 and ARCHITECTURE.md 3.6.

- Someone who can view only can't add, change, remove or tick anything shared, or change the plan's bank holidays. The database refuses, not only the screen (brief 8.7 test 3).
- Nobody can add themselves to a plan or change their own role: joining, roles and leaving go through the server's own steps in 3f and 3g.
- Their own private records are still theirs to change, as ARCHITECTURE.md 3.6 says, so they can keep their own balance up to date. This is a question for the owner in `PLAN.md`.

Why: brief 8.7 test 3, D51, and ARCHITECTURE.md 3.6's rule.

### D293. Locked to one person, and what goes with it

Decided 5 Nov 2026 in step 3a. Adds to D16 and D94.

- A record locked to someone, such as "From Jess", can be changed only by them. Its one-off changes and its ticks are locked with it, because both halves of a transfer change together (D94).
- Only the person whose money it is can lock a record, to themselves, on a shared account. A locked record carries no note, debt, goal, money set aside or pay details, and its changes no name or note (ARCHITECTURE.md 3.6).

Why: ARCHITECTURE.md 3.6 says the server refuses a shared half that carries anything but the date, amount, schedule and estimate flag, and a tick or change on one half alone would break D94.

### D294. Practice changes have a table, but nothing sends them yet

Decided 5 Nov 2026 in step 3a. A question for the owner in `PLAN.md`.

- The owner listed practice changes among the records to give a table, so there is one, `practice_changes`, always private to the person who made them.
- ARCHITECTURE.md 3.8 and D264 say practice changes stay on the phone and are never sent anywhere. Nothing in the app sends them in step 3a, and step 3c won't until the owner says which way it should be.

Why: the owner's instruction for this step and an earlier decision disagree, and only the owner can choose.

### D295. Server-only records come with the steps that use them

Decided 5 Nov 2026 in step 3a. Adds to ARCHITECTURE.md 3.7.

- ARCHITECTURE.md 3.7's records kept only on the server get their tables in the step that uses them, so their details are decided with their screens: signed-in devices and recovery contacts in 3e, deletion requests in 3h, reminders and support in Phase 4, and subscriptions, prices, organisations and referrals in Phase 5.
- Every new table starts with everything refused, and the first database test fails on any table without its rules.
- Live updates (Supabase Realtime) refuse every private channel until step 3c gives them their rules.

Why: tables made now would be guesses, and the test keeps every later one as safe as these.

### D296. Edits and notices are written only by the server

Decided 5 Nov 2026 in step 3a. Adds to ARCHITECTURE.md 3.5.

The history of edits and the notices have their tables and privacy rules now: each person reads the plan's shared edits and their own, and only their own notices. Nobody can write them until the server's save step, `save_edit`, comes in step 3c.

Why: an edit's "before" copies can hold private records, so who writes them, and with what privacy, belongs with the step that saves edits.

### D297. How database changes reach the real database

Decided 5 Nov 2026 in step 3a. Adds to D175 and ARCHITECTURE.md 1.2.

- On every push and pull request, GitHub starts a fresh local Supabase on its own machine with every migration and the test helpers in `supabase/seed.sql`, runs the pgTAP tests in `supabase/tests`, and checks the database's own functions for mistakes ("Database and its privacy rules").
- Only from `main`, and only once those checks have passed on that exact commit, the **Database** workflow sends the migrations to the real project in London, as the Deploy workflow does for the live app. It needs three secrets (README.md, "The database"); until they're added, it says so and changes nothing.
- Only the migrations go to the real project, never the seed, the tests or their made-up people.
- The Supabase command line is version 2.122.0 (ARCHITECTURE.md 1.2), from npm, and the database is Postgres 17, Supabase's own for new projects.

Why: the owner's instruction: the sandbox can't reach Supabase, so all database work goes through GitHub, and only `main` changes the real project, after its checks.

### D298. The database tests

Decided 5 Nov 2026 in step 3a. Builds brief Part 9 and 8.7 tests 2 and 3.

- Nine files in `supabase/tests/database`, 208 checks, on one shared plan: Jess's, with Tom in it as "Can edit" or "Can view only", Jess's private account with every kind of record on it, Tom's private account, their joint account, and Alex, who has a sign-in but isn't in the plan.
- Every table is tried, not a list: someone not signed in can't read, add, change or remove anything in any of them; no row Tom can read, in any table, is or names one of Jess's private records; and every table has its rules.
- Each list a check uses is first checked to hold every kind of record, so a check can't pass by testing nothing.
- Each rule was broken on purpose, one at a time, to see the tests fail: private rows readable by everyone (16 checks failed), viewers allowed to change things (9), an ended session still working (5), a locked row changeable (3), a shared row allowed to name a private one (2), a person who has left still reading (2), privacy not filled in from the account (10), and each of D299's six rules (between 1 and 4 each).

Why: a test that has never been seen to fail could be passing for the wrong reason. The first runs found one real problem and two weak tests, all fixed before anything reached GitHub: a check that let Tom add a balance, a payment or a budget to Jess's private account, saved as shared; a test helper that read a table's column instead of its own input, so the private-account checks covered fewer records than they said; and a session test that couldn't tell an ended session from the person's other phones.

### D299. What the outside review of the database changed

Decided 5 Nov 2026 in step 3a, from an outside review that tried every request Tom could make. It read nothing of Jess's private records, but found six weaker places, five now refused. Adds to D288 to D293.

- **A record names only records in its own plan.** Tom, with "Can view only", could make a plan of his own and tick Jess's shared rent from there. Jess then couldn't tick it herself, because one payment has one tick, and couldn't see why. Every record's account, payment, budget, family, debt, goal, usual week and edit must now be in its own plan, and one tick and one change per payment is counted for payments and pay-later bills apart.
- **A payment joins only its own family** (D49). An editor could add a payment to the family of "From Jess", or of Jess's private pay, which would change her payments. Now a payment starts a family of its own, or joins one in the plan on the same account, with the same privacy and lock, after the payment it was split from ("family-mixed").
- **No gap in the shared change numbers.** Every save took the plan's next number, so Tom could see the numbers jump while Jess changed private records, and so tell when she was busy with them. Now the shared records have their own count and each person's private records theirs (D288), so nothing shows when someone was using the app (brief 4.14).
- **A shared record never names a private edit.** The edit a record names must be in the plan and readable by everyone who can read the record (D291). The server's save step in 3c keeps the shared part of a change apart from the private part.
- **Nobody can say an account is someone else's.** Who an account, debt, goal or pay profile belongs to ("From Jess", D16) can be set only by that person.
- **Accepted:** someone who already knows the id of one of Jess's private records can find out that it exists, by trying to make a record with the same id. Ids are made at random on the phone (UUID version 7, with 74 random bits), so they can't be guessed, and nothing in the record shows.

Test: `supabase/tests/database/08-plans-families-and-counts.test.sql`.

Why: the review showed each with a request that worked when it shouldn't have.

## From the owner's instructions for step 3b

D300 and D304 are the owner's instructions for step 3b, on 6 Nov 2026, and D301 to D303 answer step 3a's three questions. They were built in step 3b. Points marked "decided in step 3b" weren't in the owner's words.

### D300. GitHub's checks cost less: once per commit, and the end-to-end tests once a pull request is ready

The owner's instruction, 6 Nov 2026. GitHub's free minutes ran out, and extra minutes are capped at $10 a month. Adds to D175 and D297, and replaces ARCHITECTURE.md 1.2's "the slower end-to-end tests run only on pull requests if minutes run short".

- Each commit runs the checks once: the Check workflow runs on pull requests and on `main`, no longer on the push as well.
- A newer push to a pull request stops the older run. Runs on `main` are never stopped: each commit on `main` has a group of its own.
- On a pull request, every push runs lint, type checks, the unit tests and the database tests. Decided in step 3b: in one job, "Lint, type checks, tests and the database", with the local database starting while lint and the tests run, because GitHub rounds each job up to a whole minute (about 2 minutes a push, not 4).
- The end-to-end tests (light and dark, 320px and 360px) run only once the pull request is ready. Decided in step 3b: GitHub's draft pull requests. Claude Code opens each step's pull request as a draft and marks it "Ready for review" when the step is done; that starts the end-to-end tests at once (`ready.yml`), and every push after it runs everything. Chosen over a label or a button because it needs no setting up, is part of every pull request already, and the owner's tick list stays the same. The preview link and the bank holiday download wait for ready too.
- `main` always runs everything before the app and the database are updated.
- The test browser is kept in GitHub's own cache between runs, and downloaded only when Playwright's version changes, so a slow download can't time a job out again (it took 9 minutes on 6 Nov and the job ran out of time). Each end-to-end job has 35 minutes, where the longest takes about 22. Decided in step 3b: installing the parts the browser needs from Ubuntu is given 3 minutes at most and never stops a job, because GitHub's machines already have them: on 6 Nov Ubuntu's download hung for 10 minutes and stopped two parts, while the sign-in tests ran straight after it and passed.
- Decided in step 3b: the sign-in tests run in part 3, against a local Supabase started at the beginning of that job, rather than in a job of their own, which would pay for setting up a fourth machine.
- The Database workflow says `--linked` to `supabase db push` and its dry run, and the command line stays 2.122.0.

Measured on GitHub's own records: a pull request's checks took about 42 billed minutes a push before (2 for lint and tests, 2 for the database, about 38 for the end-to-end tests), twice a push once the pull request was open. Step 2h ran them 27 times, about 800 minutes; step 3a 6 times plus 3 tries on `main`, about 300. Measured on step 3b's own pull request: a draft push costs 2 minutes; marking it ready costs about 50 (the end-to-end tests 47, of which part 3, with the sign-in tests, is 22; the preview 2; the bank holidays 1); and `main` costs about 52. So a step like 3a, with 6 pushes, now takes about 115 minutes (about 60% less), and a step like 2h about 125 (about 85% less). Getting past two-thirds on every step is a question for the owner in PLAN.md.

Why: the owner's instruction.

Practice changes never leave the phone. The `practice_changes` table is taken out of the database.

Test: `supabase/tests/database/00-every-table.test.sql` checks there's no such table.

### D303. Job details stay private, even when the pay lands in a shared account

Agreed 6 Nov 2026, the owner's answer to step 3a's question 5. Replaces the question in D291.

- Job details stay private to their person, even when their pay goes into a shared account. The partner sees the pay, and any change from overtime or a missed shift, as amounts only: never the tax code, pension, usual week, hours or rate.
- Only the person can add overtime or a missed shift to their own pay.
- The database rules change now, so a shared record never has to name a private one (D291).
- Decided in step 3b:
  - A job (pay profile), and so its usual weeks, is always private to the person it belongs to. The database fills that in, whatever a phone sends.
  - A payment can say whose pay it is (`paid_to`). Only that person can say so, and a split keeps it ("this and all later ones" from a partner stays the person's pay). On a private account it can only be that account's person.
  - Pay in a shared account finds its job and usual weeks through a private link (`private_links`, as a private debt does, D291), which only its person reads. One link per payment series, made only by the person whose pay it is.
  - "I did overtime" and "I missed a shift" are two records: the change to the payday (`pay_adjustments`: which payday and by how much), which everyone who reads the pay reads, locked to its person so only they can change or remove it; and its details (`pay_adjustment_details`: what happened, the day, the hours, the rate, any change to the extras and the tax year), which only its person reads. The same for pay on a private account, so there's one way to keep them.
  - A partner with "Can edit" can still change the shared pay itself, or mark it as arrived, as with anything shared.

Test: `supabase/tests/database/09-job-details.test.sql`, 25 checks. Each new rule was broken on purpose to see it fail.

Why: the owner's answer. Step 3c's phone joins the private links and details back on, as for D291 (PLAN.md items 24 and 28).

### D304. The web addresses: prepenny.co.uk, with prepenny.com sending people there

The owner's instruction, 6 Nov 2026, while step 3b was being built. Adds to D56.

- The owner bought prepenny.co.uk and prepenny.com through Cloudflare, in the account the app uses.
- prepenny.co.uk is the main address: the app, and the address sign-in emails come from.
- prepenny.com, and the www. of both, send people to prepenny.co.uk.
- Decided in step 3b: sign-in emails come from hello@prepenny.co.uk, as "PrePenny". The owner can have replies forwarded to their own email for free with Cloudflare's Email Routing (README.md, "Signing in").
- Decided in step 3b: the address is added to the app in Cloudflare's dashboard rather than in `wrangler.jsonc`, so the Deploy workflow's key needs no more permissions than it has. The old address, prepenny.prepenny.workers.dev, keeps working. A browser keeps a plan for each address on its own, so a plan made at the old one stays there.

Why: the owner's instruction.

## Made during step 3b

D305 to D310 were decided on 6 Nov 2026 while building step 3b, signing up, signing in and signing out.

### D305. How sign-in is set up on Supabase

Decided 6 Nov 2026 in step 3b. Builds D19, D20 and D56.

- Every sign-in setting of the real project is in `supabase/sign-in-settings.json`, with the email in `supabase/templates/sign-in-code.html`. The Database workflow sends them from `main` to Supabase's settings service after the database changes, with the Supabase key it already has. Secrets come only from GitHub's secrets: `RESEND_API_KEY`, `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` and `GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET`. Until each is added, the workflow says what's missing and leaves that part off.
- A code lasts 10 minutes and has 6 digits. A new code for the same address can be asked for once a minute. Supabase's own limits stay as they are: 30 emails an hour in all, and 30 sign-in requests and 30 code checks from one connection in 5 minutes. The local Supabase allows more emails and requests, because every sign-in test asks from one machine within minutes; its once-a-minute limit stays, and a test checks the message when it's hit.
- Someone new gets the "confirmation" email, someone signing in again the "magic link" one: both are the same PrePenny email, with the code first and the link as a backup.
- The app is told the project's address and its publishable key by two GitHub variables, `SUPABASE_URL` and `SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`. Both are public: they're in the app every phone downloads, and the database's rules decide what each person can do. Until they're set, the app is built with signing in off and says "Signing in isn't switched on yet. Your plan stays on this phone."
- Signed out after 90 days without opening PrePenny needs Supabase's paid plan (D19). The workflow asks for it every time; Supabase refuses it on the free plan, and the workflow notes that and carries on, so it switches on by itself the first time the workflow runs after the move. Until then, a phone stays signed in until the person signs out. Each time PrePenny opens on a phone that's signed in, the sign-in is renewed in the background, which is what the 90 days count from.

Why: so the owner sets things up once, nothing in Supabase is changed by hand, and the settings can be read and checked in one file.

### D306. Signing up and signing in

Decided 6 Nov 2026 in step 3b. Builds brief 4.13, D20 and ARCHITECTURE.md 4.1.

- One page, `/sign-in/`, outside the app's own frame, so it works for someone with no plan on the phone. From More it's "Sign up", from the welcome's "Already have an account? Sign in" it's "Sign in". Both work the same way: a new email address gets an account, a known one signs in. Asking "is there an account?" first would tell anyone which addresses have one.
- First the email address or "Continue with Google". Then "We've sent a 6-digit code to sam@example.com. It lasts 10 minutes.", the box for the code and "Sign up" or "Sign in", "Or tap the link in the email, on this phone.", "Send a new code" and "Use a different email address".
- The email's link opens PrePenny's own sign-in page, with its one-time key after a "#", so it's never sent to PrePenny's site or written in its logs, and it works in any browser. It works once.
- Supabase answers a wrong code and a code that has run out the same way. PrePenny knows when it sent the code, so it says "That code doesn't match. Check the 6 numbers in the email from PrePenny, or tap Send a new code." before 10 minutes and "This code has run out. Tap Send a new code and we'll email you another." after.
- Supabase's limits, in words: "You can ask for a new code in 47 seconds.", "Too many codes have been sent in the last hour. Try again in an hour, or continue with Google." and "Too many tries from this phone. Wait 5 minutes, then try again."
- The email address and when the code was sent are kept for the tab only, so the code step comes back if the phone reloads PrePenny while the person reads their email.
- Once signed in, the plan on the phone becomes the person's (brief 4.13 "Try first"): the phone notes their sign-in id on it. It stays on the phone: nothing of the plan is sent in this step. A plan made after signing in becomes theirs when PrePenny next opens.
- A plan that's already someone else's stays theirs. That can only happen if someone signs in on a phone whose last person never signed out, as when a sign-in ends by itself. Signing in is then refused with "This phone has a plan that belongs to another account. Sign in with that account and sign out first, then sign in with yours." When a sign-in ends by itself, the plan stays on the phone, because in step 3b it isn't kept anywhere else yet. Steps 3c and 3e decide what the phone does then (PLAN.md item 29).
- After signing in from More, the person is back on More, which says "Signed in as sam@example.com" and "For now your plan is on this phone only." From the welcome, they go to their plan, or to the welcome again with "Signed in as sam@example.com." if they have none.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/sign-in.spec.ts` tests 1, 2, 4 and 5, and `apps/web/test/unit/account.test.ts`.

Why: D20 puts the code first, because a link opened from email on an iPhone opens Safari, not the app on the home screen. The rest follows VOICE.md: every mistake says what to do.

### D307. Google's "G" on its button

Decided 6 Nov 2026 in step 3b. An exception to DESIGN.md's rules on icons and colours.

"Continue with Google" has Google's "G" in Google's own four colours before the words, as Google's rules for its sign-in button ask. It's the only mark in Google's colours, and it isn't a money state. The button is otherwise PrePenny's own outlined button, with a line under it: "Opens Google to choose your account, then comes back here."

Why: Google asks for its mark, and people look for it.

### D308. Signing out

Decided 6 Nov 2026 in step 3b. Builds brief 4.13 and PLAN.md item 20.

- Two taps: "Sign out" in Your account on More, then "Sign out" in the sheet it opens, which says "Signing out removes your plan, and everything else PrePenny keeps, from this phone." and, while the person has a plan of their own, "Your plan isn't kept anywhere else yet, so it can't be brought back." Its other button is "Stay signed in".
- It ends this phone's sign-in on Supabase too when there's a signal ("this device" only; "Sign out everywhere" is step 3e). With no signal, the phone forgets the sign-in all the same.
- Then it removes everything PrePenny keeps on the phone: the phone's database (every plan, the example included, What if's practice changes and which plan was showing) and the browser's small stores (the sign-in itself, Light or Dark, how Coming up is grouped, "How to read this", and pay from the calculator). Only the app's own files stay, which hold nothing of anyone's, so PrePenny still opens with no signal. The page is then loaded afresh, so nothing is left in memory either, and the welcome says "You're signed out. Nothing of yours is left on this phone."

Test: `apps/web/e2e/sign-in.spec.ts` test 3, and `apps/web/test/unit/account.test.ts`.

Why: the brief: a shared or borrowed phone keeps nothing behind. Light or Dark goes too, because it says something about the person who chose it.

### D309. Nothing is loaded or sent until someone signs in

Decided 6 Nov 2026 in step 3b. Builds PLAN.md item 20.

- Supabase's own code is loaded only on the sign-in page when it's used, or when PrePenny opens on a phone that's signed in. A phone that never signs in loads none of it and sends nothing anywhere, as before (`e2e/checks.spec.ts`).
- At sign-in, only the email address, the code, the link's one-time key or Google's answer go to Supabase: nothing about money (test 1 checks every request).
- Nothing writes an email address, a code or a message to the console, and there are no error reports yet. When error reports come (Sentry, D56), they send only the kind of error (PLAN.md item 20).
- The security policy allows connections to PrePenny's own site and exactly one other address: the Supabase project the app was built with (`scripts/write-headers.ts`). Listing each of Next.js's small page scripts in it is still to do (PLAN.md item 10).

Why: PLAN.md item 20 and CLAUDE.md's privacy rules.

### D310. Smaller choices in step 3b

Decided 6 Nov 2026 in step 3b.

- Your account sits on More after Your plan and before Colours. More's last line says "Help is still being built.", since the account part is here.
- The code box takes numbers only, from the phone's number keys, and the phone can fill it in from the email itself (`one-time-code`). Spaces are ignored.
- Each sign-in step moves focus to its heading, so a screen reader starts there (WCAG 2.4.3). Mistakes in what was typed sit under their box in red, and other problems under the buttons, in red too (D276).
- Signing in on a Cloudflare preview link works with the code. The email's link opens the live app, because Supabase has one site address.

Why: each follows DESIGN.md, VOICE.md and D213 where they didn't say exactly.

### D311. "Can view only" can still add and change their own pay, and overtime on it

The owner's answer, 6 Nov 2026. Adds to D51, D292, D302 and D303.

- Someone with "Can view only" can still add and change their own pay, and overtime and missed shifts on it, even when the pay goes into a shared account.
- Only their own pay: they still can't touch anyone else's pay, or any other shared record. The database refuses, not only the screen.
- Decided in step 3b:
  - Their own pay is money coming in that says it's theirs (`paid_to`, which only they can set, D303), with no money set aside into another account and no savings goal, because either would change something else shared.
  - With it go its one-off changes (the real amount, a moved date, skipping a payday), a change for this and all later ones, removing it, and its ticks ("Mark as arrived"). D51 keeps every other tick from someone who can view only. Ticking their own pay is part of looking after it, like typing its real amount; the owner can say otherwise.
  - Overtime and missed shifts on it: the change to the payday, locked to them (D303), and its private details.

Why: the owner's answer.

### D312. The email's link asks for a tap first: "Finish signing in"

The owner's instruction, 6 Nov 2026. Adds to D306.

- The email's link opens PrePenny's sign-in page on "Finish signing in": "You opened the sign-in link from your email. Tap the button to sign in on this phone.", with "Finish signing in" and "Sign in another way". Nothing is sent to Supabase until the tap.
- Decided in step 3b: the link's one-time key leaves the page's address as soon as it opens and is kept only while the page is open, so it isn't left in the browser's history. A link used already, or run out, says "This link has run out or has been used already. Type your email address to get a new code."

### D313. Nobody can plant a password on someone's account

The owner's instruction, 6 Nov 2026: prove that someone who signs up with my address and a password can't use that password once I sign in with a code.

- Found in step 3b: on the version of Supabase's sign-in service PrePenny runs (2.197.0), they can. The stranger's sign-up keeps their password; when the address's owner signs in with a code, the address is confirmed and the stranger's password starts to work.
- Decided in step 3b: the database keeps no password on an account until its email address is confirmed, and removes any password at the moment it's confirmed (`app.no_planted_password()`, a trigger on Supabase's own `auth.users`). Whoever set it hadn't shown they own the address. PrePenny signs people in with codes and Google only (D20), so nobody loses anything. When "add a password" comes (brief 4.13), it's set by someone signed in, on a confirmed account, and kept.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/sign-in.spec.ts` ("nobody can plant a password"), against Supabase's own sign-in service, and `supabase/tests/database/11-no-planted-passwords.test.sql`, 5 checks. Without the trigger, both fail.

Why: the owner's instruction. Supabase's note that it clears such passwords didn't hold on the version PrePenny runs.

## From the outside review of step 3b

### D314. What the outside review of step 3b changed

Decided 6 Nov 2026 in step 3b, from an outside review of the whole step. Adds to D305 to D310.

- **Whose plan it is stays on it.** Saving the plan, as it was loaded before the person signed in, took the mark off. Now every save keeps it.
- **"Sign out" is always there.** With no signal, or a sign-in Supabase can't renew for the moment, More showed "Sign up or sign in". Now it shows who's signed in and "Sign out", from what the phone keeps, and checks with Supabase next time.
- **Signing out is quick.** It waited for Supabase as long as the signal let it. Now it waits 3 seconds at most, and not at all with no signal, then removes everything.
- **"Continue with Google" asks first.** Before Google is set up (README.md, part C), it sent people to Supabase's bare error page. Now it asks Supabase's public settings first and says "Google sign-in isn't switched on yet. Use your email address instead."
- **Other tabs.** PrePenny open in another tab when someone signs out starts afresh too, so it can't show or save the plan from memory.
- **Smaller:** a sign-in that finds someone else's plan on the phone also ends when PrePenny opens; "Use a different email address" looks like a link and forgets the code sent; the lines read out while signing in stay where screen readers listen; coming back from Google with Back works; Google's one-time code is answered from the phone, not sent to PrePenny's site; the email says "Nobody can sign in without it", since the link works too; a build with no Supabase address has signing in off, and the tests build with the local one (`pnpm build:local`); and the test browser is kept as soon as it's downloaded, even when a test then fails.
- **A correction to D309 and README.md.** Google shares the person's name and the address of their profile picture as well as their email address: Supabase asks Google for "email" and "profile" and keeps them with the sign-in. PrePenny doesn't use or show them.
- **Accepted for now:** shared pay that someone else added can't later become a person's own pay (`paid_to` is set when the pay is added, D303). Step 3c, which sends pay to the server, decides how the phone sets it (PLAN.md item 28).

Why: each was shown by the review with a case that went wrong.

## From the owner's second round of answers on step 3b

D321 to D323 come from the owner's message of 7 Nov 2026, which also confirmed D315 and D317 to D320 in writing: signing in is switched on now (the owner sets up README.md, "Signing in"), with the sign-out warning staying until step 3c (D315).

### D321. Supabase's custom domain add-on goes on just before launch

Agreed 7 Nov 2026. Replaces the second half of D316.

Supabase's custom domain add-on (about US$10 a month, on the paid plan) is added just before launch, so Google's page says a PrePenny address such as auth.prepenny.co.uk instead of the project's supabase.co one. It's on the launch checklist, next to the move to the paid plan. Until then, Google's page keeps saying the project's own address.

Why: the owner's answer. It's no longer a question for launch: it's a task for just before it.

### D322. prepenny.co.uk is written into the app's hosting settings

The owner's instruction, 7 Nov 2026. Replaces the last point of D304.

prepenny.co.uk is listed in `apps/web/wrangler.jsonc` as the app's custom domain, so every `wrangler deploy` of the live app adds it if it's missing and keeps it. It isn't added by hand in Cloudflare's dashboard.

What was checked, in Wrangler 4.151.3's own code:

- With no custom domain in `wrangler.jsonc`, `wrangler deploy` leaves the app's custom domains alone, so one added by hand would have stayed. Doing it by hand was safe, but the owner asked for the deploy to keep it.
- With one listed, Wrangler sends Cloudflare the whole list each time and asks it to match exactly, so any custom domain added by hand and not listed is taken off at the next deploy. README.md says not to add others by hand.
- If Cloudflare refuses, the new version is already online at the workers.dev address and the Deploy run gets a red cross.
- Cloudflare makes the DNS record for the address itself; the key needs no DNS permission.
- Preview links (`wrangler versions upload`) ignore the list, so a pull request never touches prepenny.co.uk.

GitHub's Cloudflare key needs three permissions for this: Account, Workers Scripts, Edit; Zone, Workers Routes, Edit; and Zone, Zone, Read, for the prepenny.co.uk zone or all zones. These are the ones Wrangler asks for when it signs in by itself. The **Edit Cloudflare Workers** template has the first two; whether it has Zone, Zone, Read couldn't be checked from here (Cloudflare's documentation was out of reach), so README.md, "Signing in", part A, has the owner check all three and add any that's missing. Adding a permission keeps the key the same, so nothing changes in GitHub.

Why: the owner's instruction, so every deploy keeps the address.

### D323. Nothing is installed from Ubuntu for the end-to-end tests

The owner's instruction, 7 Nov 2026. Replaces D300's point about Ubuntu's download being given 3 minutes at most.

The end-to-end workflow no longer installs anything from Ubuntu for the test browser. GitHub's machines already have every library it needs: the step only ever said "already the newest version", and on 6 Nov 2026 its download hung for 10 minutes and stopped parts 1 and 3, while the tests ran straight after it and passed. If a library ever goes missing, the browser says which when it starts, and the step can come back then.

Why: the owner's instruction, to remove the step or make it unable to hold up a job. Removing it also saves the minute or so it took.

### D324. A plan on the phone and another in the account: the person chooses which to keep

The owner's answer, 7 Nov 2026.

- When someone signs in on a phone that has a plan of its own, and their account already has a saved plan, one plain screen asks which to keep: "Your saved plan, last changed Tue 3 Nov" or "The plan on this phone, started today".
- After they choose one and tap "Keep this plan", a confirm says the other will be removed and that this can't be undone: "The plan on this phone will be removed. This can't be undone." or "Your saved plan will be removed from your account. This can't be undone.", with "Yes, remove it" and "No, go back".
- Decided in step 3c:
  - The screen is the sign-in page's last step, "Choose which plan to keep", with the two plans as a list with a round button beside each (DESIGN.md, D213) under "Which plan do you want to keep?", neither chosen to start with (D265). "Keep this plan" with nothing chosen says "Choose a plan to keep."
  - "Last changed" is when anything in the saved plan was last saved; "started" is when the first thing was put in the plan on the phone. Each says "today", or the day as everywhere else.
  - Keeping the saved plan removes the phone's plan from the phone and brings the saved one down. Keeping the phone's plan removes the saved plan from the account for good (`remove_plan`), then sends the phone's plan up as the first copy. A plan someone else is in is never removed from under them: the server refuses ("shared-plan").
  - A plan on the phone with nothing in it yet, as when "Make my plan" has just been tapped, isn't asked about: the saved plan comes down.
  - A phone that finds the two plans while PrePenny is open, rather than at sign-in, opens this same step.

Why: the owner's answer.

### D327. Every screen in the middle on a laptop, and a check for it

## Made during step 3c

D330 to D344 were decided on 7 Nov 2026 while building step 3c, your plan on the server.

### D330. save_edit runs as the person, with two small helpers that use the database's own rights

Decided 7 Nov 2026 in step 3c. Builds ARCHITECTURE.md 3.5 and PLAN.md item 25.

- `save_edit` runs as the person who sent it (`security invoker`). Every record it writes goes through the same privacy rules and checks as everything else (steps 3a and 3b, 281 database checks), so it can't open a hole those rules close. It adds what the rules can't: the same edit twice changes nothing, a record changed since refuses the whole edit, the engine's family rules (D332), and the history and the nudge.
- Two things a person can't do themselves are done by small helpers with the database's own rights, each checking who's asking: `app.begin_edit` writes the edit in the history (D296), only for a current member of the plan, while their sign-in lasts, and never under someone else's edit id; `app.nudge` and `app.send_nudge` send the live nudge, only for a member's own plan. `remove_plan` uses the database's rights too, to delete a plan: only its owner, only while signed in, and never while someone else is in it. Every check each makes has a test that tries to get past it (`12-save-edit.test.sql`, `13-save-edit-roles.test.sql`), and each was removed on purpose to see the tests fail.
- `plan_changes` runs as the person, so the privacy rules decide every row, and it also only asks for shared rows and the person's own: removing either guard alone still sends a partner nothing private; removing both makes 5 checks fail.
- The `app` part stays off the Data API: only the four steps the phone calls are in `public` (`save_edit`, `plan_changes`, `start_plan`, `remove_plan`), and a database check fails if anything else a signed-in person can run turns up there.

Why: the owner asked which, and why. Running as the person means one set of rules decides everything, already tested record by record; the helpers are kept to the two jobs a person mustn't be able to do directly.

### D331. What an edit is on the server

Decided 7 Nov 2026 in step 3c. Builds ARCHITECTURE.md 3.5.

- One change on the phone is one edit: its records, saved whole or not at all. Each record sent is the whole record, null where empty, with the version the phone last saw; a new record has none, and a removed one is marked removed (ARCHITECTURE.md 3.1).
- An edit's id comes from the phone. Sent again, the same edit is "already" saved and changes nothing; the phone gets each record's version as it is now.
- A record not at the version the phone saw refuses the whole edit with "changed-since" and the records' ids (never what's in them). Nothing of it is saved, not even in the history, so it can be sent again once the phone has looked (step 3d). A record the person can read but not change ("Can view only", or locked to someone else) is refused as "not-allowed"; one they can't see, as "not-found".
- Anything else the database refuses, such as an amount below zero in a budget, comes back as "bad-record" alone, and only that reaches the database's logs: Postgres's own message would show what the record holds (CLAUDE.md).
- An edit starts private to its person, and becomes shared the moment it saves a shared record, so a partner never sees that someone changed only private things (D299), and a shared record never names a private edit (D291). It keeps no before-copies or summary yet: Activity and older undos are step 3g.

Test: `supabase/tests/database/12-save-edit.test.sql` and `13-save-edit-roles.test.sql`.

Why: the owner's five tests, and ARCHITECTURE.md 3.5.

### D332. The engine's family rules on the server

Decided 7 Nov 2026 in step 3c. Builds PLAN.md item 27.

After every edit, for each payment family it touched, the server refuses ("family-broken"): two series of a family holding the same payment number; a family whose first series isn't the one it's named after, starting at payment 0; a series split from another that starts before it; a series before the family's last that never ends, or has a different last amount; and the two halves of a private transfer that don't match, or a shared half that isn't mirrored by exactly one private half of its person's (D49, D89, D94).

Test: `supabase/tests/database/12-save-edit.test.sql`.

Why: ARCHITECTURE.md 2 asks the server to refuse what the engine never makes.

### D333. How the phone keeps its plan in step with the account

Decided 7 Nov 2026 in step 3c. Builds ARCHITECTURE.md 3.5 and 3.8.

- The phone keeps three things beside the plan: a copy of the server's rows as it last saw them, the queue of changes not sent yet, and, for a plan kept in an account, who it's for and the change numbers it has brought down (D299).
- A change is saved on the phone and joins the queue in the same save, so one is never kept without the other. The queue is sent one change at a time, in order, each as one edit. A change the server refuses stays, and nothing after it is sent: step 3d decides what happens then.
- The plan on the phone is always the server's rows with the queue on top, so a change brought down never hides one made here.
- A phone looks for what's new when PrePenny opens, when it comes back on screen, when the signal returns, every minute while it's showing, straight after sending, and when a live nudge arrives (D338).
- The first copy goes up in edits of at most 400 records, every series of a plan in the same one, so a family is never half saved.
- The example is never sent (D182). A phone that has never signed in sends nothing, as before (D309).

Why: ARCHITECTURE.md 3.5, and so a plan with no signal keeps working and loses nothing.

### D334. The plan as rows on the server

Decided 7 Nov 2026 in step 3c. Builds D288 to D291, D303, and PLAN.md items 5, 24 and 28.

- Each engine record is one row, every field a column, so the plan comes back exactly as it was left: tested on Sam's example, Sam with every part of the engine on, and the other five people's plans, which come back identical, with the same figures on Home, Coming up and Debts, and nothing more to send.
- Everything a person makes is private to them, until shared plans come in step 3f: accounts, debts and goals, and so everything on them. A new account, debt or goal on a partner's phone is private to that partner too.
- A one-off change, a tick, a person's settings, an overtime's details and a private link have no id in the engine, so each row's id is worked out from what it's for (a SHA-256 of its plan, kind and parts): two phones ticking the same payment name the same row, so it's one tick (D83) and a clash, never two.
- Whose pay a payment is (`paid_to`, D303) is set once, when the pay is added: on a private account, its person's; on a shared one, the person adding it, for Pay or Benefits. A split keeps it. Shared pay someone else added can't later be claimed: the person removes it and adds it again as theirs (D314's question).
- A shared payment's private debt or goal, and shared pay's job and usual weeks, go in its person's private links, and come back on their phone only (items 5, 24 and 28). A shared tick inside a private debt's Update from statement keeps its link the same way. A shared tick inside another account's private Update balance doesn't name it, because it counts only for that account (D83).
- A debt's or goal's `belongsTo` in the engine is carried by its privacy; the app never sets it.

Test: `apps/web/test/unit/sync-rows.test.ts` (37 checks).

Why: brief Part 10 ("exactly as they left it") and the privacy decisions of steps 3a and 3b.

### D343. Two end-to-end tests at a time saved nothing, so it's one at a time again

Decided 7 Nov 2026 in step 3c, from the measurements D320 asked for. Replaces D329's first point.

- Measured on this step's pull request, with the same tests, as GitHub bills them (each part rounded up to a whole minute):
  - One at a time, run twice: 11 + 15 + 21 = 47 minutes for the end-to-end tests, then 8 + 15 + 24 = 47 minutes.
  - Two at a time, run twice: 9 + 14 + 18 = 41 minutes, then 13 + 15 + 22 = 50 minutes.
  - For comparison, step 3b on `main`, one at a time, with 28 fewer tests: 8 + 14 + 14 = 36 minutes. The quick checks take 2 minutes on every push, whatever the setting.
- No saving could be measured: GitHub's machines vary from run to run by more than the difference. On the slow machine of the second run two at a time, the speed test, run on its own, saw Home open again in 2.2 seconds with the phone slowed 4 times; on another run it was 0.6 seconds, the same as step 3b's app measured side by side on the building computer.
- Two at a time wasn't fully reliable either. On its first run, two older checks needed a second try, both in part 3's first minutes, while the local Supabase was starting beside them (D344). Its second run needed none. One at a time, the first run needed no second try, and the second needed one, for a reason that has nothing to do with the setting (D344). Every test passed in the end on every run, and on a full run two at a time on the building computer.
- GitHub's machines for a private repository have 2 processors, and one test keeps them mostly busy: the browser, the app's background worker that works out the plan, the local web server, and in part 3 the local Supabase. With another test beside it, each test takes about twice as long. That halves the room each test has before its time limit.
- So each part runs one test at a time again (`--workers=1`, written out so it doesn't depend on the machine), with the speed test among the others, as before.

Why: D320 keeps two at a time only if it saves minutes and every test passes several runs in a row. It did neither.

Decided 7 Nov 2026 in step 3c, found on GitHub. Each failed once and passed on its second try.

- **The accessibility checks wait for the page to stop moving.** On one of GitHub's slower machines, axe checked the overtime sheet while it was still fading in, and measured its intro line at 4.33 to 1, below the 4.5 to 1 it needs. Once the sheet is open, the line is 7.92 to 1 (`--ink-muted`, #4a5060, on the card, #fffdf8). The grey axe measured is that colour at about three-quarters strength, part of the way through the sheet's 0.18-second fade. Every axe check in the end-to-end tests now goes through `e2e/axe.ts`, which first waits for anything moving on the page to finish. An endless animation, such as a skeleton's pulse, isn't waited for.

It names nothing of PrePenny's and holds no data. Every other line still counts.

## From the owner's review of step 3c

D345 comes from the owner's reviewer, and D346 to D350 are the owner's answers to the questions step 3c left in `PLAN.md`, all on 7 Nov 2026.

### D345. Every change a person makes goes through save_edit

From the owner's reviewer, 7 Nov 2026. Builds D296 and D330.

- Signed-in people had insert and update on the record tables and on the plan's own row, so a change could still be made straight through the Data API (REST or GraphQL). The privacy rules held, but such a change skipped `save_edit`: no edit in the history, no family rules, no nudge. A partner who can edit could change shared records and leave no history, which matters for Activity in step 3g.
- Now the first check on every record table and on the plan's own row refuses a person's insert or update with "use-save-edit", unless `save_edit` is saving it. `start_plan` still makes a plan: it marks the one plan it's making, for that one insert.
- Decided in step 3c: `save_edit` marks its writes with a setting that lasts to the end of the request, so it now takes the mark off as soon as its records are written. Otherwise one GraphQL request with two steps, `save_edit` then a change of its own, could pass the second off as part of the first.
- A person is a request with a person's own rights, the Data API's roles. The database's own helpers, the migrations and the tests' set-up aren't refused. Nothing a person can reach can set either mark.
- The check runs as the person writing and reads its marks itself, so nobody is given a right to any new function.
- The older tests of the privacy rules now write inside an edit, as `save_edit` does (`tests.in_an_edit`), so the rules are still tested as they apply there. Test 08's check that a shared record never names a private edit is now made as the database writing for Jess, since a person's change always names the edit `save_edit` is saving.
- A plan can't be renamed for now: `save_edit` changes only the plan's nation, and no screen renames a plan yet. Renaming will go through `save_edit` when a screen needs it.

Test: `14-only-through-save-edit.test.sql` (31 checks): a direct insert and a direct update on every record table, and on the plan's nation, are refused for the owner, someone who can edit and someone who can only view, and the same changes through `save_edit` still work; `start_plan` makes a plan and nothing else; neither mark lasts beyond its step; the database itself isn't refused. `00-every-table.test.sql` checks the guard comes first on every table. Each rule was broken on purpose to see the tests fail, apart from `start_plan` taking its mark off, which can only ever let in the plan it has just made.

Why: the reviewer's finding. Every change needs its history (Activity, step 3g), its family rules and its nudge.

## From the owner's instructions for the step 3c fix

D351 to D354 come from the owner's message of 8 Nov 2026, after step 3c went live: their phone, signed in during step 3b with a plan part-way through setup, went straight to setup, never brought down the plan their laptop had saved, never asked which plan to keep, and had no way to sign out. Points marked "decided in the fix" weren't in the owner's words.

### D351. Every screen of a signed-in phone looks at the account, the welcome and setup included

The owner's instruction, 8 Nov 2026. Builds D324 and D333.

- **What was wrong.** In step 3c, only the app's own screens (Home, Coming up, Debts and More) looked at the account. A phone whose plan isn't set up to the end is sent from `/app/` to setup straight away, as soon as it has read the plan from the phone, which is quicker than checking the sign-in with Supabase. So the look at the account never started, and the welcome and setup never looked at all. Setup did try to send each step's change, but on finding a saved plan in the account it asked for "which one to keep" through a hook only the app's screens had, so nothing happened. A fresh phone worked because the sign-in page looks at the account itself, straight after signing in; the owner's phone was signed in already, so it never went through that page again.
- **Now.** Every screen looks: the welcome, setup and the app, when PrePenny opens, when it comes back on screen, when the signal returns and every minute (`components/KeepInStep.tsx`).
  - The phone has a plan with something in it, finished or not, and the account has another: it goes to "Choose which plan to keep" (D324).
  - The phone has no plan, or one with nothing in it yet: the saved plan comes down, and the phone shows Home (or setup, where the saved plan isn't set up yet).
  - Only the phone has a plan, with something in it: it goes up as the first copy.
- Decided in the fix:
  - A plan with nothing in it yet, as when "Make my plan" has just been tapped, isn't sent up: it waits until something is put in it. Otherwise another phone with a real plan would be asked to choose against an empty one.
  - A phone whose plan is kept in the account looks once each time PrePenny opens, then keeps it in step as before. One that hasn't sent its plan yet, or has none, looks every time.
  - Once the saved plan has come down in place of the one showing, the screen loads afresh, and a screen that still had the old plan open can never save it back (`PlanGone` in `data/db.ts`).
  - On "Choose which plan to keep", the line under "The plan on this phone" says "The one on this phone now, as you left it.", or "The one on this phone now. It isn't finished yet." when its setup isn't finished, so it's clear which is the half-made one. The old line, "The one you made on this phone before signing in.", wasn't true for a plan made after signing in.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/your-plan.spec.ts` tests 7 (the owner's own case, which failed in exactly the owner's way before the fix), 8 and 9, and `apps/web/test/unit/db.test.ts` ("A plan removed from this phone").

Why: the owner's instruction, and D324 asks for "which one to keep" whenever the phone finds two plans, not only at sign-in.

### D352. An account holds one plan of the person's own

The owner's instruction, 8 Nov 2026: make two plans of one's own impossible, and decide what happens to an account that already has two.

- **How two could happen.** Two phones that each found the account empty at the same moment could both send their own plan up, and nothing in the database stopped a second plan. Each phone then kept its own plan in step and never saw the other's changes. From the code, the owner's phone could only have sent its plan up if a setup change was made on it after step 3c went live and before the laptop's plan reached the account; the laptop would then have asked which plan to keep when it opened, which it didn't, so the owner's account most likely has the laptop's plan only. Nothing here reads the live database.
- **Now the database refuses a second plan** of a person's own ("has-a-plan"), whoever asks. Two phones asking at the same moment are taken one at a time: the second waits for the first, then is refused. Being in someone else's plan doesn't count. The phone that's refused looks at the account again and asks which plan to keep.
- Decided in the fix, for an account that already has two:
  - Nothing is removed by itself. The next phone signed in to it asks "Choose which plan to keep": the plan on this phone against the other saved plan, with the same words and confirm as D324. Keeping the saved plan removes this phone's plan from the account too; keeping this phone's removes the other. Either way, one plan is left.
  - A phone with no plan of its own brings down the newest of the two first, then asks about the other.
  - Another phone that was keeping the removed plan in step takes the kept plan in its place the next time it looks. Anything on it not sent yet goes with it, as the confirm said ("This can't be undone."). This also covers D324's "Keep the plan on this phone", which left the other phone keeping a plan that was no longer in the account.
- Step 3g, which can change who owns a plan (D53), must keep this rule.

Test: `supabase/tests/database/15-one-plan-each.test.sql` (14 checks; turning the rule off makes 5 fail), and `apps/web/e2e/your-plan.spec.ts` tests 3, 11 (an account with two) and 12 (two at the same moment, 5 times, through Supabase's own Data API). Two requests at once were also tried by hand on two database connections: the second waited 2 seconds for the first, then was refused.

Why: the owner's instruction. Nothing is removed without the person choosing, because only they know which plan is the right one.

### D353. "Sign out" on the welcome, every setup step and "Choose which plan to keep"

The owner's instruction, 8 Nov 2026.

- Next to "Signed in as sam@example.com.", "Sign out" opens the same sheet as on More (`components/SignOutSheet.tsx`, which More uses too), with the same warning when something hasn't been sent.
- Decided in the fix:
  - It's on every setup step too, under Next and Back, and on "Choose which plan to keep", since none of them can reach More. Setup and the welcome send a person with two plans back to the choice until they choose, so signing out is the other way out.
  - The words sit centred under the buttons, "Sign out" as a link-style button with its 44px tap area.
  - A plan with nothing in it has nothing to lose, so the sheet doesn't warn about it.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/your-plan.spec.ts` test 10. Screenshots are in `docs/screens/3c-fix`.

Why: the owner's instruction.

### D354. The engine's speed test is timed in up to 5 rounds

The owner's instruction, 8 Nov 2026. Adds to D72 and D134.

- On a busy GitHub machine the speed test once measured 12.25 milliseconds against its 12 millisecond limit.
- Now it's timed in rounds: each round warms up, then takes the middle of 20 runs, as before. It stops at the first round under 12 milliseconds, and fails if none of 5 rounds is. The limit and the plan timed are the same, so an engine that's slower for real is slower in every round and still fails. Each round's time is shown in the test's output.
- Tried on purpose: with the building computer's 4 processors kept busy by 8 other programs, rounds 1 to 3 took 17.69, 22.46 and 12.23 milliseconds and round 4 took 5.41, so it passed. With the limit lowered to 3 milliseconds, all 5 rounds were over it and the test failed.

Why: the owner's instruction: sturdy on a busy machine, without loosening what it checks.

## From the outside review of the step 3c fix

### D355. What the outside review of the step 3c fix changed

Decided 8 Nov 2026 in the step 3c fix, from an outside review of the whole fix. Adds to D351 and D352.

- **A refused change isn't the plan leaving the account.** The fix first took any "not found" while sending a change to mean the plan had been removed elsewhere, and then stopped bringing changes down. Now only the plan itself not being found, when the phone asks for what's new, means that. A refused change waits in the queue, as step 3c decided (step 3d).
- **Never two plans on one phone.** "Make my plan" tapped in the moment the saved plan was on its way could have left both on the phone. Now the swap checks, in the same save, that the phone's plan is still the one it looked at, and looks again if not; and a new plan is never started beside one that has just come down.
- **A first copy cut off part-way is sent again.** Found in step 3c's own code: a first copy that didn't all arrive, as with the signal lost part-way, was taken as saved, never sent again, and More and the sign-out sheet said nothing was missing. Now the phone marks it as still going up, sends the rest from where it stopped at the next look, and until then More says "Your plan hasn't been saved in your account yet." and signing out warns.
- **"Keep the plan on this phone" can't say nothing was removed when something was.** Found in step 3c's own code: once the saved plan has been removed, the choice is made, even if this phone's plan can't go up straight away; it goes up at the next look, as any plan not saved yet does.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/your-plan.spec.ts` test 13 (without the mark, More said "Your plan is saved in your account." with nothing in it, and the test failed), and `apps/web/test/unit/db.test.ts` ("A new plan beside one that has just come down").

Why: each was shown by the review with a case that went wrong.

## From the owner's decision for the shorter setup

D356 is the owner's decision of 8 Nov 2026, and D357 the owner's answer to the question the step 3c fix left in `PLAN.md`. Points marked "decided in the shorter setup" weren't in the owner's words.

### D356. Setup is 6 screens in 3 parts

The owner's decision, 8 Nov 2026. Replaces brief 4.1's order of setup steps, D221's "Step 2 of 8" and Next and Back between 8 steps, D226 (savings as a setup step) and the step numbers in D224, D225 and D227. Everything else those decisions say stays.

- **Why.** The owner finds 8 setup steps tiring: even with "Skip this step", people still read every screen. Brief Part 10 counts PrePenny as done when three people set it up on their own phones in under 5 minutes.
- **The welcome** says, before setup starts: "Set it up once, about 5 minutes. After that, just keep it up to date." It fits VOICE.md as the owner wrote it, so it's kept word for word.
- **6 screens in 3 parts:**
  - Part 1, Money in: 1. Your account (what your bank shows today). 2. How you're paid.
  - Part 2, Money out: 3. Home and bills: rent or mortgage at the top, then tick your bills, on one screen. 4. Debts. 5. Spending money.
  - Part 3, Your plan: 6. Here's your plan.
- **The top of each screen** shows its part instead of "Step 3 of 8": "Part 2 of 3: Money out", with a simple three-part bar.
- **Savings leaves setup.** "Here's your plan" ends with "From now on: update your balance when you check your bank, and tick payments as they go out." Below it, a card says "Saving for something? Add a goal", which opens the savings goal form. Savings stays easy to find after setup.
- **Nothing about the plan's accuracy changes:** everything the old 8 steps asked for is still asked for, apart from savings, which the plan screen offers. "Skip this step" stays where it was: on every screen but the first and the last.
- Decided in the shorter setup:
  - **Home and bills.** "I don't pay rent", a button that went on to the bills, is now an answer to "Which do you pay": Rent, Mortgage, "I don't pay rent or a mortgage", or "I'll add it later". The last two hide the rent boxes. "I'll add it later" leaves rent out on its own while the bills are added, as Skip on the old rent step did, so someone who pays rent but hasn't the amount to hand isn't made to say they don't pay it; it's asked again on the way back. Next checks the rent and every ticked bill together, saves nothing until all of them are right, and puts focus on the first mistake, the rent's before the bills' (D203). Then the rent is added, then each bill, each as its own thing with Remove, rent listed first (D221). Rent left empty says "Type the amount, for example 550. Or choose I'll add it later." "I don't pay rent or a mortgage" stays chosen when coming back, unless rent was added after all.
  - **The bar.** Three parts side by side, each filled in navy as far as the screen showing: Part 1 is half filled on screen 1 and full on screen 2, Part 2 fills a third on each of screens 3 to 5, and Part 3 is full on the plan. The empty part is the card edge colour, so it shows on the paper. The bar is only a picture: the words above it say the same, and screen readers hear only the words (brief Part 6: colour is never the only signal).
  - **"Saving for something?"** is a line of words on the card, not a heading, because VOICE.md avoids questions as headlines. Under it: "Set a target and see the date you'll get there. You can also add one later from More, under Savings goals.", which is how savings stays easy to find after setup: More has had Savings goals since step 2f.
  - **"Add a goal"** opens the same savings goal sheet as More's Savings goals, which asks everything setup's old savings step asked (D226). As the old step did, it starts with the money kept in a savings account if there is one, the second account if not, or "A new savings account" when there's only one account; More's sheet keeps its own start. Each goal is one thing setup added, made a moment after the last (D229), listed on the card with Remove, and "Holiday added." is said. The button then says "Add another goal". The plan's figures, such as "First savings goal reached", are worked out again straight away.
  - The welcome's line shows for everyone, setup finished or not, so nothing on the welcome moves while the page loads and no tap lands on the wrong button. After setup it still says what to do: keep it up to date.
  - **Taps.** A tap is a press of a button, a choice or a tick box, or a box typed into, counted once. Picking a date in a phone's own date picker takes a few more taps, the same before and after. Counted from "Make my plan" to "Go to my plan", with the same answers each time, the old setup on `main` before this change and the new one, the same at 320px and 360px (corrected by D363, which found every date box counted twice before):

  | Setting up as | Before: 8 steps | Now: 6 screens |
  | --- | --- | --- |
  | Someone who skips every step they can | 12 taps | 10 taps |
  | Sam (debts, no rent or bills) | 78 taps | 77 taps |
  | Priya (every screen, and a savings goal) | 67 taps | 67 taps |
  | Dee (two incomes, rent, bills) | 49 taps | 47 taps |
  | Kofi (three incomes, rent, bills, a debt) | 72 taps | 70 taps |
  | Jess and Tom (three accounts) | 70 taps | 68 taps |
  | Ali (student loan, rent each term) | 39 taps | 37 taps |

  Most taps are typing in the person's own numbers, which are all still asked, so the taps fall by 1 or 2: Next once for rent and bills together, and no Skip for savings. Someone adding a savings goal taps "Add a goal" and "Add goal" instead of Next, so Priya's taps stay the same. What changes most is what there is to read: 6 screens instead of 8 for everyone.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/setup.spec.ts` tests 1 to 9, with test 7 counting the screens and taps of Priya's whole setup; each of the six people set up and using the app in `apps/web/e2e/people.spec.ts`; `apps/web/e2e/your-plan.spec.ts` test 14 for setting up while signed in; `apps/web/test/unit/setup-progress.test.ts` for the parts. Screenshots of the welcome and all 6 screens are in `docs/screens/setup-6`.

Why: the owner's decision.

## Made during the shorter setup

D358 to D360 were decided on 8 Nov 2026 while building the shorter setup. D360 came from checking its tests.

### D358. Progress from the old 8 steps carries on at the matching screen

Decided 8 Nov 2026 in the shorter setup, from the owner's instruction that anyone part-way through the old 8 steps, on their phone or in their saved account, carries on at the matching new screen with nothing lost. Adds to D221 and D351.

- Progress saved now says it's the new layout (`layout: 2`). Progress without it is from the old 8 steps, and is read like this wherever it was saved, on the phone or in the account:

  | Old step | Opens |
  | --- | --- |
  | The welcome | The welcome, with "Carry on making my plan" |
  | 1. Your account | 1. Your account |
  | 2. How you're paid | 2. How you're paid |
  | 3. Rent or mortgage | 3. Home and bills |
  | 4. Bills | 3. Home and bills |
  | 5. Debts | 4. Debts |
  | 6. Spending money | 5. Spending money |
  | 7. Savings | 6. Here's your plan, where "Add a goal" is |
  | 8. Here's your plan | 6. Here's your plan |
  | Done | Done |

- Nothing is lost: every record stays as it is, every answer ("I don't pay rent", "I have no debts", extra hours) is kept, and every thing added keeps its Remove, now listed on the screen that shows its kind: rent and bills on Home and bills, goals on the plan's "Saving for something?" card.
- The plan's records don't change, so nothing is sent to the account just for reading old progress. The next change saves the new layout, on the phone and in the account.
- A step number that can't be read opens the first screen, and damaged items are left out, so no one is left on a blank page.
- One case can't be read exactly: someone setting up on two phones at once, with one still running the PrePenny from before this update until it's reloaded ("PrePenny has been updated. Tap to reload.", D202). That phone reads the new screen numbers as its old steps, and can save its old numbers beside the new layout. Then setup may open a screen early or late, and an old 7 or 8 opens the plan. Nothing is lost either way: every record and every Remove is kept, and Back and Next reach every screen.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/setup.spec.ts` test 8 (on the phone, from every old step, at 320px and 360px in light and dark, with an old "I don't pay rent" answer, and Remove after on a bill and on a goal from the old savings step) and `apps/web/e2e/your-plan.spec.ts` test 15 (in the account, from every old step, on another phone), and `apps/web/test/unit/setup-progress.test.ts`.

Why: the owner's instruction.

### D359. Smaller choices in the shorter setup

Decided 8 Nov 2026 in the shorter setup.

- The screenshots of the old 8 steps stay in `docs/screens/2e` as they were, as a record of step 2e. The new ones are in `docs/screens/setup-6`.
- `docs/TESTING-WITH-PEOPLE.md` times setup from "Make my plan" to "Go to my plan" on "Part 3 of 3: Your plan", notes the screen's name where someone gets stuck, and uses the live address, https://prepenny.co.uk.
- A screen of setup is still a "step" inside the code and in "Skip this step", the words the owner kept.
- Test 13 of `apps/web/e2e/your-plan.spec.ts` (step 3c fix) now waits for the sign-in page to read the phone, "The plan you made on this phone becomes yours…", before typing an email, as the other sign-up tests do. On a busy machine its tap on "Send me a code" could come first, and nothing was sent. Nothing it checks changed.

### D360. Signing out waits for a send under way

Decided 8 Nov 2026, while checking the shorter setup's tests. Adds to D308, D325 and D353.

- **What was wrong.** Signing out straight after a change, such as Back on a setup screen and then "Sign out" on the welcome, could leave one record behind on the phone: the note of which account the plan is kept in, with the account's id and change numbers, though never an amount, a name or a note. The change was still being sent, or the phone was still bringing down what's new, when signing out cleared the phone, and it saved that note after. D308 says signing out leaves nothing behind. Your-plan test 10 caught it about once in 7 runs on this branch, and never in 20 runs on `main`, where the timing happened to be different.
- **Now.** Once the sign-in has ended, nothing new starts, and signing out waits for any send or look already under way to finish, for 3 seconds at most, before it clears the phone. Then nothing can come back.
- It changes nothing someone sees: signing out takes the same moment, and with no signal it still clears the phone at once.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/your-plan.spec.ts` test 10 passed 20 runs in a row afterwards (3 of 20 failed before), and every sign-out test in `your-plan.spec.ts` and `sign-in.spec.ts` passes.

Why: the rule in D308. A record left after signing out says whose plan this phone held.
