## From the owner's reviewer of the shorter setup

D363 and D364 come from the owner's reviewer, 8 Nov 2026, after GitHub's end-to-end run of the shorter setup.

### D363. Setup's tap count counts each box typed into once

From the owner's reviewer, 8 Nov 2026. Corrects the tap counts in D356.

- **What GitHub found.** Setup test 7 counted 67 taps for Priya's whole setup on GitHub, at 320px and at 360px, where the computer this was built on had counted 79. Nothing in setup is skipped or works differently on a small phone: the same test now also checks that Priya's saved plan is exactly the one her answers make, every date included, and setup test 1 does the same at 320px and checks every day's balance, and both pass on GitHub.
- **The cause.** The tap counter in the tests counted a box as tapped each time the browser said the box got focus. Filling in a date box, the browser on the computer this was built on says so twice, and GitHub's newer browser once. So each of Priya's 12 date boxes (pay, rent, 9 bills and the goal's date) counted as 2 taps there and 1 on GitHub. The counter was wrong, not setup.
- **Now.** A box counts once, when typing starts in it, from what's typed (the browser's "input" event, which every browser sends for every kind of box). A tap anywhere else, then typing in the same box again, counts again. Priya's whole setup is 67 taps on both computers, at both widths, and the test checks her 12 dates are each counted once.
- Every count in D356's table was taken again with the corrected counter, on `main` and on the shorter setup, at 320px and 360px. Each is lower by the date boxes it counted twice; what the shorter setup saves is the same: 1 or 2 taps for most people, and 2 screens for everyone.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/setup.spec.ts` tests 3 and 7.

Why: the reviewer's finding. A count that depends on the browser can't show whether setup got shorter.

### D364. What if test 1 waits for Coming up before using it

From the owner's reviewer, 8 Nov 2026, about PLAN.md item 36. Fixed in the shorter setup, not later, because a test that fails at random on `main` can stop the live app and the database updating after a merge (D297).

- **What failed.** What if test 1, after throwing the practice changes away, taps "Back to Coming up" and opens Fri 30 Oct there. Sometimes Fri 30 Oct stayed closed. On the computer this was built on it failed 9 runs in 20 on `main`.
- **The cause.** What if shows a practice copy of Coming up, with the same lowest point line and the same paydays, and the test had opened Fri 30 Oct in it. After the tap on "Back to Coming up", the test waited for the lowest point line, which the What if page already showed, so it sometimes went on while What if was still on screen. It found Fri 30 Oct already open there, so it didn't tap it, and a moment later Coming up came in with Fri 30 Oct closed. Logging every tap showed it: in each failed run no tap reached the page after "Back to Coming up", and Fri 30 Oct was open on What if just before. Nothing in the app was wrong: a person sees Coming up appear and taps it.
- **Now.** After "Back to Coming up", the test waits for Coming up's own heading and for What if's side-by-side to have gone, then reads the paydays. Nothing it checks was loosened, and nothing is retried.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/what-if.spec.ts` test 1 passed 80 runs in a row afterwards, 20 each at 320px and 360px in light and dark, on the computer that failed it 9 times in 20.

Why: the reviewer's instruction. Find the cause, fix it, and don't skip, loosen or retry.

## Made during step 3d

D365 to D374 were decided on 8 Nov 2026 while building step 3d, offline changes and clashes, from the owner's instructions for it and D59. Questions for the owner are in `PLAN.md`.

### D365. How a clash is settled

Decided 8 Nov 2026 in step 3d. Builds D59 and ARCHITECTURE.md 3.5.

- **The queue.** A change made with no signal is saved on the phone at once and waits in the queue, in the order made, as since step 3c. Each change now also keeps the commands that made it, the day it was made on, the payments it was about (as they looked before and after it) and what the phone had brought down when it was made.
- **Before each change is sent,** the phone looks at what's new on the server first. When nobody else has changed what the change is about since it was made, it's sent as it is.
- **When someone has,** the phone makes the change again, with its own commands, on the newest records, as the engine would make it now (ARCHITECTURE.md 3.5). A payment is found again by its family and number (D49), so a change to Rent on Tue 1 Dec still lands on it after the other phone split Rent for this and all later ones. Then:
  - Both changed different things, such as one moved Rent on Tue 1 Dec and the other changed its amount: both are kept, and nobody needs telling.
  - Both changed the same thing, such as Rent's amount on Tue 1 Dec: they clash, and the change made last wins (D59). Judged by the payment as the person sees it (its amount, date, whether it's removed or ticked) and record by record, so "this and all later ones" on one phone and "just this one" on the other is a clash too.
  - The winner is saved. When it's this phone's, the server tells the other person (D367). When it's the other phone's, this phone's change isn't sent, and the person is told, with "Put mine back" (D368).
- **One change is one unit,** as for undo (D87): when it clashes and loses, none of it is sent, and "Put mine back" makes all of it again.
- **A payment the other phone removed** can't be changed here, whichever was made last: making the change again wouldn't bring the payment back. It isn't sent, and the person is told it can't be made. A payment removed on both phones is done already, with nothing to send. When this phone removed a payment the other phone changed, the newest wins as usual.
- **Both phones ticked the same payment:** it's done, with nothing to send and nobody told.
- **A change this phone sent itself** never clashes with a later one from the same phone.
- **"Put mine back"** makes the same commands again, now, on the plan as it is, as a new change with its own Undo. Being newest, it's kept. When the newer change removed the payment, there's no "Put mine back" (making the old change again wouldn't bring the payment back), and the button says "Got it".

Test: `apps/web/test/unit/clash.test.ts` (12 checks on Sam's example plan), and `apps/web/e2e/clashes.spec.ts` tests 2.

Why: D59, ARCHITECTURE.md 3.5's "the phone runs the same command again on top of them", and the owner's instruction that the change that lost is replayed on the newest records where that still makes sense.

### D366. The phone's clock, corrected against the server's

Decided 8 Nov 2026 in step 3d. Builds D59 and ARCHITECTURE.md 3.5.

- Every answer from `save_edit` and `plan_changes` carries the server's own time. The phone keeps the difference from the latest answer that came back within 10 seconds, worked out against the middle of the time the question took, so it's right to within half a second or so.
- Each change is stamped "made at" with the phone's time plus that difference. A change made before the phone had heard the server's time is corrected when it's sent.
- The server saves a "made at" later than its own time as its own time, so even a phone that never corrected its clock can't make its changes look newer than they are.
- The difference is kept in the phone's database, so signing out removes it with everything else (D308).
- Only "made at" is corrected. The times a phone writes inside the plan, such as when a payment was ticked or a balance updated (D33), are still the phone's own: changing them changes which ticks count inside a balance update, so it's a question for the owner in `PLAN.md`.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/clashes.spec.ts` test 3 (a phone an hour fast, and a phone a day slow: both fail with the correction taken out), `apps/web/test/unit/clash-words.test.ts`, and `supabase/tests/database/16-clashes.test.sql`.

Why: the owner's instruction: a phone with the wrong time can't always win or always lose.

### D367. What the server adds for clashes

Decided 8 Nov 2026 in step 3d. Adds to D330, D331 and D339.

- `plan_changes` gives, for each record, the edit that last saved it, when that edit was made and by whom. A shared record only ever names a shared edit (D291), so a partner never learns anything about the other person's private changes this way. It also gives each person in the plan's nickname, and the person's own notices not read yet.
- A change that wins a clash says which edits it replaces (`overrides`). As it saves, `save_edit` notes each record it replaced that one of those edits saved last, and leaves a notice for that edit's person, naming only those records, which they saved themselves and can read. A notice holds ids and a time, never an amount, a name or a note: their phone puts the words together from its own records. Only an edit made before the winner counts (the newest wins), the same change sent twice tells nobody twice, and nobody can leave a notice outside `save_edit`.
- The phone that made the replaced change shows the message and marks the notice as read. Another phone of the same person leaves it.

Test: `supabase/tests/database/16-clashes.test.sql`, 40 checks with the plan's own row (D375); each new rule was broken on purpose to see them fail.

Why: ARCHITECTURE.md 3.5 ("the other person gets a notice") and CLAUDE.md's privacy rules.

### D368. The words for a clash, and where they appear

Decided 8 Nov 2026 in step 3d. Builds ARCHITECTURE.md 4.4's "Clash notice" sheet.

- A sheet, "Changed on two phones", opens by itself on the app's screens, one at a time, oldest first, and only once no other sheet is open, so it never covers something the person is in the middle of. Under its title: "The same thing was changed on two phones. PrePenny keeps the newest change, and tells you when yours wasn't kept."
- The words, with each person's own names and the payment's date:
  - Someone else in the plan: "Tom changed Rent on Tue 1 Dec to £650 after you changed it to £640. Tom's change is kept." with "Put mine back" and "Keep Tom's change".
  - The person's own other phone: "Your other phone changed Rent on Tue 1 Dec to £650 after you changed it to £640 on this phone. Your other phone's change is kept." with "Put mine back" and "Keep your other phone's change".
  - Moved, removed and ticked say so: "Tom moved Rent on Tue 1 Dec to Fri 4 Dec after you moved it to Wed 2 Dec.", "Tom marked Rent on Tue 1 Dec as not paid after you marked it as paid.", "Tom changed Rent on Tue 1 Dec to £650 after you removed it."
  - The payment removed on the other phone: "Tom removed Rent on Tue 1 Dec, so your change to it can't be made. Make it again if you still want it." with "Got it".
  - A change that can't be made on the newest records for another reason: "... after you changed it to £640. Yours can't be made on the plan as it is now, so it wasn't saved." with "Got it".
- "Tom's change is kept", not "His change is kept" as in the owner's example: PrePenny doesn't know anyone's pronouns, and the name is just as short.
- Amounts in the sentence are whole pounds when there are no pence, "£650", as in other sentences (D166), and "£640.50" when there are.
- Until nicknames can be set (step 3f), someone in the plan with none is "Your partner".
- Once seen, a message isn't shown again, even if the server tells the phone again.

Test: `apps/web/test/unit/clash-words.test.ts`, and `apps/web/e2e/clashes.spec.ts` tests 2. Screenshots are in `docs/screens/3d`.

Why: the owner's instruction: plain words, the real names, one tap to put theirs back.

### D369. A change the account refuses is taken off, and the person told

Decided 8 Nov 2026 in step 3d. Replaces D333's "a change the server refuses stays, and nothing after it is sent" (PLAN.md item 32).

- When the server refuses a change for a reason other than a clash, such as someone who can only view changing something shared (D51), the change is taken off this phone's queue and off the plan on this phone, which goes back to what the account has. The changes made after it are still sent, in order.
- The person sees "A change wasn't saved": "Your account couldn't take a change made on this phone, so it was taken off. Changes made after it are still sent.", then "Your change to Rent on Tue 1 Dec wasn't saved in your account, so it has been taken off this phone." and why, when the server says: "You can look at this plan but not change it.", or otherwise "Make it again if you still want it.", with "Got it".
- No signal, or a server that can't be reached, is never a refusal: those changes wait and are tried again.
- A refusal the privacy rules give for a record the person can read but not change is "not allowed" now; it was "not found" in step 3c.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/clashes.spec.ts` test 5.

Why: the owner's instruction: a refused change mustn't block everything after it for ever.

### D370. The same change sent twice

Decided 8 Nov 2026 in step 3d. Adds to D331.

- Before a change is sent, the phone keeps exactly what it's sending. If no answer comes back, as when the signal drops after the server saved it, the phone sends exactly the same thing again, with the same edit id, and `save_edit` changes nothing the second time (D331).
- Only if the first never arrived, and the records have moved on since, is the change looked at afresh, as a clash would be.
- The phone also notes each change as its own before it's sent, so if the answer is lost, its records still count as this phone's own when they come down, and never as a clash.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/clashes.spec.ts` test 4: the answer to the first save is dropped on the way back; the phone sends it again with the same id; the server has one edit and one Car MOT, at version 1; the other phone has one Car MOT.

Why: the owner's instruction to prove it from the phone's side too.

### D371. More says how many changes are waiting

Decided 8 Nov 2026 in step 3d. Replaces D325's words for More's line.

- Under "Signed in as": "1 change is waiting to be sent to your account. It's saved on this phone, and goes when there's a signal." or "3 changes are waiting to be sent to your account. They're saved on this phone, and go when there's a signal."
- It changes straight after each change. In step 3c it changed only once the phone next tried to send, so it could say "Your plan is saved in your account." just after a change made with no signal.
- Step 3c's line for a refused change is gone: nothing waits behind a refused change now (D369).

Test: `apps/web/e2e/clashes.spec.ts` tests 1 and 6. Screenshot: `docs/screens/3d/more-changes-waiting-360-light.png`.

Why: the owner's instruction.

### D372. PLAN.md item 37: the offline helper missed a page that loaded while it was starting

Decided 8 Nov 2026 in step 3d, from the owner's instruction to find the cause.

- **The cause.** Signing up loads the sign-in page afresh. The offline helper takes over the pages that are open when it starts. A page whose load started just before the helper started, and finished just after, wasn't among them, and nothing took it over later, so it stayed without the helper until it was loaded again. Test 5 waits for the helper on that page, so on a busy machine, where the helper's first start takes longer and lands on the sign-in page's load, it waited 60 seconds and failed. The second try had different timing. A person's phone could do the same: PrePenny wouldn't open with no signal from that page until it was next opened.
- **Found by** making the timing exact on purpose: holding the helper's files while a page starts loading, letting the helper start, then letting the page arrive. The page was never taken over, every time. GitHub's report from the failed run couldn't be read from the computer this was built on, so the cause was shown this way.
- **Now** a page the helper hasn't taken over asks it to, once the helper is ready, and the helper takes it over.
- No retry was added and no wait was loosened.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/offline.spec.ts`, "a page that loads while the offline helper is taking charge is taken over too", which fails without the fix.

Why: the owner's instruction: find the root cause and fix it.

## From the outside review of step 3d

### D375. What the outside review of step 3d changed

Decided 8 Nov 2026 in step 3d, from an outside review of the whole step. Adds to D365 to D374.

- **The phone shows what was sent.** When a change was made again on newer records, as when both phones' changes were kept, the phone sent the merged version but went on showing its own until something else changed. A later change on it could then undo the other phone's without a clash. Now the plan on the phone is worked out again from what the server has after anything is sent.
- **"Already saved" keeps nothing.** A change sent again after a lost answer is "already" saved; the server's records may have moved on since, so the phone no longer keeps what it sent as the server's copy, and brings down what's there instead.
- **A sign-in that ends while sending isn't a refusal.** The server's "not allowed" for a sign-in that has just ended looked like a refused change, so every waiting change would have been taken off. Now only the privacy rules' own refusal of a record counts as "not allowed"; anything else is tried again, and the sign-in is looked at, as before (D326).
- **Signing out stops the sending.** Sending now checks between steps that signing out hasn't begun, and stops, so nothing is written to the phone after it's cleared (D360).
- **The right payment in the words, and an Update balance kept.** The words described the change's first payment, which could be the wrong one. Now they describe the payment that clashed. A tick inside an Update balance on a payment the other phone removed is left out, as a tick already made is, and the balance typed in is still saved: a removed payment moves no money, so no figure changes.
- **A change the phone can't work out doesn't hold up the rest.** If the phone can't work out whether a change clashes, as when it's too slow, it tries again later, and after 3 tries the change is taken off with "Make it again if you still want it."
- **Bank holidays clash too.** The plan's own row now says which change last saved it, so the plan's bank holidays changed on two phones are settled by the newest, like everything else.
- **The clock.** The phone keeps the most precise difference from the server (the quickest answer), and still follows a phone whose clock is put right; times are compared as times, not as text; and a change's time is never later than now when it's sent, so a phone whose clock jumped forward can't make an older change look newer.
- **Accepted:** someone who can edit could name, in `overrides`, the last change to a shared record they then change, and the other person would be told "Tom changed it after you" for an ordinary change. The server can't tell a real clash from that; the notice holds ids and a time only, so nothing leaks, and it's once per change and record.

Test: `apps/web/test/unit/clash.test.ts` (Update balance, bank holidays, rows naming no edit), `apps/web/test/unit/clash-words.test.ts` (the clock), `supabase/tests/database/16-clashes.test.sql` (the plan's row, 40 checks), and `apps/web/e2e/clashes.spec.ts` ("both are kept, on both phones").

Why: each was shown by the review with a case that went wrong.

## Made during step 3e

### D380. The list of signed-in devices

Decided 9 Nov 2026 in step 3e. Builds brief 4.13 and ARCHITECTURE.md 3.7.

- More has a **Security** card, after Your account: App lock, and once signed in, Signed-in devices, Two-step sign-in and Email addresses, each a row that opens its screen (D213).
- **Signed-in devices** lists every sign-in session of the person's, this one first, then the most recently used. Each has a plain name worked out on the server from the browser's own description of itself (its "user agent"), such as "Chrome on an Android phone", "Safari on an iPhone" or, where the browser can't be told, as for an app added to an iPhone's home screen, "An iPhone". Under it: "This device", or "Last used today", "Last used yesterday" or "Last used Tue 3 Nov".
- Never an IP address or a place: the server keeps Supabase's record of the IP address, but `my_devices` gives back only an id, the kind of browser and device as codes, whether it's this one, and when it was last used.
- "Last used" is when the device last asked the server anything (`hello`, at most once a minute), or renewed its sign-in. ARCHITECTURE.md 3.7 said "no last seen", meaning nothing a partner can read (brief 4.14): the list is only ever the person's own, and nobody else can read or end it. The owner asked for when each was last used.

Test: `supabase/tests/database/17-devices.test.sql` (29 checks) and `apps/web/e2e/devices.spec.ts` test 1. Screenshot: `docs/screens/3e/devices-360-light.png`.

### D381. Signing out of a device, and everywhere

Decided 9 Nov 2026 in step 3e. Builds D21, D308, D326 and D348, and PLAN.md item 29.

- **"Sign out of this device"** is on every device. On this one it opens the usual sign-out sheet (D325). On another it asks once, "Sign out of Safari on an iPhone", with "It gets nothing more from your account straight away, and removes your plan within a minute. It's saved in your account, so nothing is lost.", then ends that session on the server (`end_device`). Only the person's own sessions can be ended; anyone else's is refused exactly as one that doesn't exist.
- **"Sign out everywhere"** ends every session of the person's, this one too, as its name says (`end_every_device`). This phone then forgets everything, as signing out does. While anything on this phone hasn't been sent, it says "1 change on this phone hasn't been sent to your account yet. Once it has, you can sign out everywhere." With two-step sign-in on, it asks for the authenticator app's code first (D382).
- **The other devices stop within 1 minute.** An ended session's very next request gets nothing (D21, step 3a). The person's phones are nudged on their own private channel at once, and every signed-in phone also asks the server whether its sign-in stands every minute, even when PrePenny isn't on screen. Told it has ended, a phone removes the plan once it's safely in the account (D326); a phone with a change not sent yet keeps the plan, and More says "Your sign-in has ended. 1 change on this phone hasn't been sent to your account yet: sign in again to send it." Signing in again sends it (D348). Tried end to end, as item 29 asked.
- **Accepted:** Supabase's own sign-in service lets any sign-in, even one that hasn't passed the second step, sign out every device of its person through Supabase's own address, outside PrePenny. Someone who got into the person's email could use it to sign their phones out; they'd see nothing, and the person signs in again. Stopping it would mean blocking Supabase's own sign-out. PrePenny's own "Sign out everywhere" does ask for the second step.
- **Accepted:** Supabase's live updates keep a connection open after its session has ended, until its token runs out. The nudges carry nothing (D339), and the phone's next look finds the sign-in has ended.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/devices.spec.ts` tests 1 and 2, `supabase/tests/database/17-devices.test.sql`.

Why: the owner's instruction and D21; nothing not sent is ever lost (D348).

### D382. Two-step sign-in with an authenticator app

Decided 9 Nov 2026 in step 3e. Builds brief 4.13 and 7.1.

- It's Supabase's own (TOTP), which is free, switched on in `supabase/sign-in-settings.json`.
- **It's real.** Once someone has turned it on, a sign-in that hasn't passed the second step gets nothing from the database and can save nothing: `app.session_ok()`, which every privacy rule and every step the phone calls goes through, needs both Supabase's record of the session and its token to say the second step was passed. A sign-in waiting for it is sent to "Type the code from your authenticator app" on the sign-in page, wherever it is, with "Sign out" there too.
- **Turning it on:** "1. Get an authenticator app...", "2. Add PrePenny to it." with "Add PrePenny to my authenticator app" (a link that opens the app on this phone, since a phone can't scan its own screen), a picture to scan from another phone, and the key to type in groups of 4, then "3. Type the 6-digit code the app now shows for PrePenny." Google Authenticator and Microsoft Authenticator are named as examples; they aren't money products.
- **Supabase signs every other device out** as it's turned on, so each needs the code to sign in again. The screen says so before it happens.
- **Turning it off, changing the email address, signing out everywhere, and adding or removing a backup email** each ask for the app's code just then. The server checks it too: Supabase's sign-in service refuses turning it off or changing the email address without the second step, and two triggers on Supabase's own tables (`fresh_second_step`) also need a code typed in the last 5 minutes; `end_every_device`, `add_backup_email` and `remove_backup_email` need one in the last 5 minutes on that very session.
- **Getting back in with the backup email never gets past it** (D384).
- "If you lose the phone with your authenticator app, you can't sign in." is on the screen once it's on, with what to do. Help for someone who has lost both is support, in Phase 4 (brief 4.16).

Test: `supabase/tests/database/18-two-step.test.sql` (22 checks) and `apps/web/e2e/two-step.spec.ts` test 3, which also asks the database directly with the waiting sign-in's own token: no accounts, no plan members, `plan_changes` and `my_devices` refused. Screenshot: `docs/screens/3e/two-step-turning-on-360-light.png`.

Why: the owner's instruction: "it must be real".

### D383. Changing the email address

Decided 9 Nov 2026 in step 3e.

- Under More, Email addresses, "Change email address": the new address, then "Send the codes". Supabase emails a 6-digit code to the address now and another to the new one ("secure email change", on since step 3b), and both are typed into PrePenny: neither address alone can change it.
- The email is PrePenny's own, laid out like the sign-in email (`supabase/templates/email-change.html`): "The code for your current address is" or "The code for your new address is", the code, then what was asked and "If you didn't ask for this, don't type the code anywhere, and nothing changes."
- These emails have a code and no link: the change was started inside PrePenny, on a phone that's signed in, so that's where the codes go, and a link would open the browser rather than the app on the home screen (D20).
- Once both codes are typed, Supabase starts a new sign-in. The codes are checked on a connection of PrePenny's own that keeps nothing, and that new sign-in is ended straight away, so this phone keeps its own, second step and all, and no extra device appears in the list.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/emails.spec.ts` test 4: the new address's code typed twice changes nothing; both change it; there's still one device; another phone signs in with the new address and gets the same plan.

Why: the owner's instruction: "confirmed from both the old and the new address".

### D384. The backup email, and getting back in with it

Decided 9 Nov 2026 in step 3e, from the owner's instruction to design it securely (D379).

- **Adding it.** Under More, Email addresses: the backup address, then a 6-digit code sent to it, typed into PrePenny. With two-step sign-in on, the authenticator app's code first. It can't be the sign-in address. One each. Once added, the sign-in address is told: "jess.backup@example.com is now the backup email for your PrePenny account." with "If this wasn't you, reply to this email straight away."
- **Never someone else's.** An address that's already someone else's backup, or the address another account signs in with, is refused ("in-use"), and that's checked only after the code from that inbox is typed, so it tells nobody anything they couldn't find out from the inbox itself.
- **Getting back in.** On the sign-in page, "Can't get into your email? Use your backup email" opens `/get-back-in/`, signed out. The backup address gets a code, if it's someone's backup: the answer is the same either way ("If ... is the backup for a PrePenny account, we've sent it a 6-digit code."), and so is the answer to a code typed for an address that isn't, so nobody can find out which addresses are backups. Once the right code is typed: the backup becomes the address the account signs in with, every device signed in to it is signed out (and nudged, D381), the backup is used up, and the old address is told "Your backup email was used to get back into your PrePenny account...". Then the phone signs in as usual, with a sign-in code Supabase sends to that address, and with the authenticator app's code when two-step sign-in is on.
- **The codes.** 6 digits from the database's secure random numbers, kept only scrambled (SHA-256), bound to their person, purpose and address, lasting 10 minutes, 5 tries at most, used once, one a minute and 5 an hour for each address. A new code uses up the one before.
- **The emails** are sent by the database itself (`app.send_email`, with Supabase's pg_net), to Resend on the real project, from hello@prepenny.co.uk, as the sign-in emails are, and to the local test inbox here. Supabase's own sign-in service can only email the address someone signs in with, and PrePenny has no server functions yet; the database is where every other rule already is, and it can be tested here and on GitHub. The Database workflow keeps the Resend key in Supabase's Vault (README.md, part F). pg_net keeps each email's request until it's sent, a moment later, and Resend's answer for 6 hours, both readable only by the database's administrator; Resend keeps email text for 30 days, as for the sign-in emails (ARCHITECTURE.md 1.3). No email holds an amount, a payment name or a note, and no refusal or log line holds an email address: every refusal is a short code.
- **Someone with no backup email** sees "No backup email? Write to hello@prepenny.co.uk from any address, and say which address you signed up with." until support comes in Phase 4 (brief 4.16).
- **Accepted:** someone who gets into the backup inbox can get into the account, as with any backup; two-step sign-in still stops them, and the sign-in address is told. Someone with a few minutes on a phone left signed in, with two-step sign-in off, could add a backup of their own; the sign-in address is told at once.

Test: `supabase/tests/database/19-backup-email.test.sql` (48 checks: nobody reads anyone's backup, Tom can't use Jess's addresses even with their codes, tries, time, the same answers for addresses that aren't backups, the swap, every device signed out, the old address told) and `apps/web/e2e/emails.spec.ts` test 5. Screenshots: `docs/screens/3e/backup-email-360-light.png` and `get-back-in-360-light.png`.

Why: the owner's instruction, kept to email only (D379).

### D385. The app lock: "Open with your PIN"

Decided 9 Nov 2026 in step 3e. Builds brief 4.13 and 7.2, D35 and ARCHITECTURE.md 3.8.

- Under More, Security, App lock: a PIN of 4 to 8 numbers, typed twice. It's for this phone only, and kept only scrambled (PBKDF2 with SHA-256, 310,000 rounds and a salt of its own, from the browser's own crypto), never sent anywhere. Signing out removes it with everything else (D308).
- It's asked for when PrePenny opens, and when it comes back after 5 minutes or more away (brief 4.13), over the app and over setup, which both show the plan. Nothing behind it can be reached by touch, keyboard or screen reader.
- While away with the lock on, a plain cover hides the plan, so the phone's picture of PrePenny in the app switcher shows nothing of it. The phone may still keep an older picture, and the App lock screen says so plainly (ARCHITECTURE.md 7). The phone apps in Phase 6 hide it fully, and add fingerprint and face (D35).
- 5 wrong tries in a row make it wait 30 seconds, and 30 more for each wrong try after that.
- "Forgot your PIN?" says what to do: signed in, sign out and sign in again, and the plan comes back from the account; with a plan on this phone only, "Remove everything from this phone", which says first that the plan goes too.
- Turning it off and changing the PIN need the PIN.
- An update that loads PrePenny afresh straight after the PIN was typed (D386) doesn't ask again.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/app-lock.spec.ts` test 6. Screenshot: `docs/screens/3e/pin-screen-360-light.png`.

Why: the owner's instruction (D35's words), and the brief.

### D386. A phone on an older version never quietly keeps a different plan

Decided 9 Nov 2026 in step 3e, from the owner's instruction after steps 3c and 3d, when a phone still on the version before stayed in setup, and two devices kept different amounts, until each was loaded again by hand.

- **Every build says which version it is:** the database it was built for (the newest migration in `supabase/migrations`) and the commit (`scripts/build-worker.ts`), in the app and in `/version.json`, which the site never lets a browser or the offline helper keep.
- **The database checks the version on every request.** The app names its database version on every request to the Data API (`x-prepenny-version`), and `app.session_ok()` gives nothing to any other version: an app built for an older database ("update-needed"), or for a newer one, in the minutes after an update while the database is still being changed ("server-behind"). So even an old version with a mistake in it can't send a change or bring the plan down.
- **Before sending changes or bringing the plan down,** a signed-in phone asks the server (`hello`) whether its sign-in stands and whether it's on the version expected, and the site which version is live. On an older version, it sends nothing, fetches the new version and loads it at the first safe moment: no sheet open, and nothing typed that hasn't been sent, such as a setup screen half filled in. Every change made meanwhile stays in the queue on the phone, and the new version sends it. "PrePenny has been updated. Tap to reload." (D202) shows meanwhile, and a tap loads it at once. A phone loads afresh for an update no more than once every 2 minutes, so a site part-way through updating can't keep reloading it.
- **When the database is newer than the site, or the other way round,** for the minutes after merging, phones wait, with "Changes can't be sent right now. They're saved on this phone.", and go on once both are updated.
- **The PrePenny live now, from step 3d, sends no version,** so from the moment the database is updated it can't send or bring anything down: its changes wait on the phone, as with no signal, until it's loaded afresh, which the update note asks for, and opening PrePenny again does. Then the new version sends them. Nothing is lost.
- Test 7 plays a phone on the old version with the tests' own setting (D201): it says it was built for the database from before step 3e, and is another build, until it has updated itself.

Test: `supabase/tests/database/20-versions.test.sql` (16 checks) and `apps/web/e2e/versions.spec.ts` test 7: the old phone's change waits, the server gets nothing from it, it doesn't reload while a sheet has something typed, it does once the sheet closes, then sends the change, and both phones show the same balance and the other phone's new payment.

Why: the owner's instruction: "Before sending changes or bringing the plan down, it should know it's on the version the server expects."

### D387. D378 built: the times inside the plan by the server's clock

Decided 9 Nov 2026 in step 3e. Builds D378.

Every time PrePenny writes inside the plan (when a payment was ticked, a balance updated, a spend recorded, a debt updated from a statement, and when a record was made, for the same-day order) is the phone's clock corrected against the server's (`lib/clock-offset.ts`, from `data/clock.ts`), loaded when PrePenny opens. On a phone that has never heard from the server, the difference is 0, as before. It changes no figure for anyone with one phone.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/versions.spec.ts` ("D378"): a phone an hour fast writes its balance update's and its tick's times within minutes of the server's own, not an hour ahead.

Why: the owner's answer to step 3d (D378).

### D389. The sign-in tests are shared between GitHub's three parts

Decided 9 Nov 2026 in step 3e. Adds to D300 and D343.

Step 3e adds about 20 minutes of tests that sign in, which only part 3 ran. Part 3 took about 22 of its 35 minutes already, so it would have run out of time. Now each part starts its own local Supabase in the background and runs a third of them (`--shard`), as it does with the other tests. The minutes billed stay about the same: each part pays a minute or so to start Supabase, while its other tests run.

## From the owner's instructions for step 3e hardening

D391 to D396 are the owner's decisions and answers in the instructions for step 3e hardening, safer recovery, on 9 Nov 2026. D397 and D398 were decided while building it, the same day. Points marked "decided in step 3e hardening" weren't in the owner's words.

### D391. Getting back in with a backup email waits 24 hours, with a stop

The owner's decision, 9 Nov 2026. Replaces D384's "Getting back in" (the backup became the sign-in address as soon as its code was typed).

- **Typing the code sent to the backup email starts a 24-hour wait.** Nothing changes on the account: not its sign-in address, its email sign-in, its devices, its sign-ins' refresh tokens, its authenticator apps, its backup email or its plan.
- **Straight away, the address the account signs in with is told,** in an email with a "Stop it" link, and **every signed-in device shows a notice** on every screen, with a "Stop it" button. The devices hear of it at once from a nudge (D339), and anyway within a minute, when they ask the server whether their sign-in stands (`hello`, D381).
- **The stop link** is single-use, unguessable and can only stop. Decided in step 3e hardening: 32 bytes from the database's secure random numbers, kept only scrambled (SHA-256), after a "#" in the address so it never reaches a server's logs, and taken out of the address bar as soon as the page opens. The page stops nothing until "Stop it" is tapped, so an email service that opens links to check them can't use it, as for the sign-in link (D312). It works once, and not at all once the request has ended. It signs nobody in or out, and the page says nothing about the account.
- **"Stop it" on a signed-in device** stops only the person's own request.
- **When it's stopped,** by either, the backup email is told, and the notice goes from every device.
- **After 24 hours, if nobody stopped it,** the person asks for a fresh code at the backup email, and typing it finishes: the backup becomes the address the account signs in with, every device is signed out, and the old address is told. Then they sign in as usual with a sign-in code sent to the backup address, and **two-step sign-in still applies**: the authenticator app's code is asked for when it's on (D382).
- Decided in step 3e hardening: **the code that finishes it must have been sent after the 24 hours.** A code asked for at 23 hours 59 minutes says to ask for a new one.
- Decided in step 3e hardening: **once the finishing code is accepted, it can no longer be stopped,** for the second or so it takes to finish.
- Decided in step 3e hardening: **a request that's ready can be finished for 7 days,** and then it runs out and has to start again, so one nobody finished can't be used weeks later. One request at a time for each account. **Changing or removing the backup email stops it.**

Test: `supabase/tests/database/21-safer-recovery.test.sql` and `apps/web/e2e/recovery.spec.ts`. Screenshots: `docs/screens/3e-hardening`.

Why: the owner's decision: someone who gets into the backup inbox shouldn't take over the account before its owner can say no.

### D392. Codes are capped and locked, and say the same either way

The owner's decision, 9 Nov 2026. Replaces D384's "one a minute and 5 an hour".

- **3 codes a day for an address,** for "get back in" codes and "add a backup email" codes, each counted on its own. "A day" is the last 24 hours (decided in step 3e hardening). Once a minute still applies.
- **After 10 wrong tries in a day, the address is locked for 24 hours,** for that kind of code: no code is sent to it, and every code typed for it is wrong, the right one too. Every wrong try counts, for any address typed, whoever types it and whether or not a code was ever sent to it. Decided in step 3e hardening: tries made while it's locked don't count, so the lock ends 24 hours after it began, and the tries start again from nothing. Each code still takes 5 tries at most and lasts 10 minutes.
- **A locked address gets the same answers as any other**: "If ... is the backup for a PrePenny account, we've sent it a 6-digit code." and, for a code, the same as a wrong code. So nobody can tell an address is locked, or that it's a backup. For "add a backup email", a capped or locked address gets "We've sent a 6-digit code", as any other, and the screen says 3 codes can be sent a day.
- Decided in step 3e hardening: the tries are kept against the address scrambled (SHA-256), so an address someone typed that isn't anyone's is never kept as typed, and they're cleared after a day.
- **Accepted:** someone who knows a person's backup address can keep it locked by typing 10 wrong codes a day, which stops that person getting back in with it until they stop. The owner chose the lock; support (Phase 4) can help.

Test: `supabase/tests/database/21-safer-recovery.test.sql`.

Why: the owner's decision: a 6-digit code can't be guessed in 10 tries a day.

### D393. The sign-in address changes through Supabase's own admin route

The owner's instruction to check whether changing Supabase's own sign-in tables is supported and safe, 9 Nov 2026, and what was found and decided in step 3e hardening. Replaces D384's change of `auth.users` and `auth.identities` by the database itself.

**What was found** (Supabase's docs and the source code of Supabase's sign-in service, version 2.197.0, which the local Supabase runs, on 9 Nov 2026):

- Supabase doesn't forbid writing to its sign-in tables, but its docs say "Columns, indices, constraints or other database objects managed by Supabase may change at any time". Since 21 Apr 2025 projects can't change those tables' shape, but can still change their rows and put triggers on them.
- **Identities.** The database's own change updated only an existing "email" sign-in. For someone who had only ever used "Continue with Google", it left no email sign-in at all. Supabase's admin route (`PUT /auth/v1/admin/users/{id}`, "update a user") updates the email sign-in, and makes one when there's none (tried here: it does).
- **Email uniqueness.** Supabase keeps one account per sign-in address with an index on its own table, which refuses a duplicate whichever way the change is made. The admin route doesn't check other accounts' Google sign-ins, so the database checks those itself before finishing, and an account never confirmed that's in the way is removed through the admin route.
- **Refresh tokens.** Supabase has no admin route that signs one person's devices out: its own sign-out deletes their rows from `auth.sessions`, and each sign-in's refresh tokens go with it (a database rule, "on delete cascade"). A token whose sign-in row is gone is refused by Supabase's sign-in service ("session_not_found"), and PrePenny's own rules refuse it too (D21).
- The admin route writes the change to Supabase's own log of sign-in events, with the new address, as signing up does.

**So:**

- **Finishing goes through the admin route,** from a small function Supabase runs, `supabase/functions/get-back-in` (an "Edge Function"). The phone sends it the backup address and code; it asks the database what the code does (`get_back_in_with_code`, which only Supabase's own secret key can call); and only when the database says it can finish, it makes the backup the sign-in address with the admin route, then the database signs every device out, uses the backup up and tells the old address (`get_back_in_finished`). If the admin route can't, the request is as it was, still open and still able to be stopped (`get_back_in_not_finished`).
- **Signing devices out still deletes their rows from `auth.sessions`,** as Supabase's own sign-out does, for "Sign out of this device", "Sign out of all other devices" and finishing. The database tests check that a sign-in's refresh tokens go with it (`17-devices`, `21-safer-recovery`), so a Supabase update that changed it would fail GitHub's checks before reaching the real project.
- **What the owner sets up: nothing.** Supabase gives every function the project's own secret key, so no key is copied anywhere. The Database workflow puts the function online after the database changes, with the Supabase key it already has (README.md, part F). The function reads Supabase's newer secret keys first, so it keeps working when Supabase retires its older keys at the end of 2026.
- The function writes nothing to its log, and holds no amount, payment name or note. The function is reached at the same Supabase address as everything else, so the security policy is unchanged (D309).

Test: `supabase/tests/database/21-safer-recovery.test.sql` (the database's part), and `apps/web/e2e/recovery.spec.ts` (the whole of it, through the function and Supabase's admin route, against the local Supabase). Tried by hand here too: an account with only a Google sign-in gets an email sign-in, and a never-confirmed sign-up with the backup address is removed first.

Why: the owner's instruction: "If Supabase's own admin route is safer, use it". It is, for the address: it's Supabase's supported way to change it, and it keeps the email sign-in right for Google accounts, which the database's own change didn't.

### D394. "Sign out everywhere" becomes "Sign out of all other devices"

The owner's decision, 9 Nov 2026. Replaces D381's "Sign out everywhere" (PLAN.md question 3).

- Under More, Signed-in devices: "Sign out of all other devices" keeps this device signed in, and signs out every other one; each removes the plan within a minute, or keeps it with a change not sent yet (D348).
- Signing out of this device stays under "Sign out" (D325), and "Sign out of this device" on this one opens the same sheet.
- It still asks for the authenticator app's code when two-step sign-in is on, and the database checks it was typed in the last 5 minutes (`end_other_devices`, D382). A change on this phone not sent yet no longer stops it, as this phone stays signed in.
- Brief 8.7 test 6's "ends every other session within 1 minute" is what it does.

Test: `supabase/tests/database/17-devices.test.sql`, `18-two-step.test.sql`, and `apps/web/e2e/devices.spec.ts` test 2.

Why: the owner's decision.

## Made during step 3e hardening

### D397. The words for getting back in, the notice and the stop link

Decided 9 Nov 2026 in step 3e hardening, in VOICE.md's style. Times are "2:05pm on Tue 10 Nov", in UK time in emails and by the phone's clock on screen, with the year only when it isn't this year.

- **Get back in** (`/get-back-in/`): the address's hint adds "We can send 3 codes a day to one address."; under the code: "For your safety, getting back in takes 24 hours. Your first code starts the wait, and we tell the address you sign in with now. After 24 hours, a new code finishes it.", with "Use this code".
- **The wait:** "You've asked to get back in", "For your safety, nothing changes on your account for 24 hours.", "You can finish from 2:05pm on Tue 10 Nov.", "Then come back to this page, and we'll send a new code to sam.backup@example.com to finish. From then on, you sign in with sam.backup@example.com.", "We've told the address you sign in with now. If it wasn't you who asked, it can be stopped from there, or on any phone or computer signed in to your account.", with "Back to signing in". After 24 hours: "You can finish now." with "Send me a code to finish". The phone remembers the wait, so the page shows it again when opened again.
- **The notice on a signed-in device:** "Someone asked to get into your account", "They used your backup email. Unless it's stopped, from 2:05pm on Tue 10 Nov they can sign in with it, and every phone and computer will be signed out.", "If it wasn't you, stop it now. If it was you, you don't need to do anything.", with "Stop it". Then: "It's stopped. Nothing on your account changed, and we've told your backup email. If you don't know who has your backup email, change it: More, then Email addresses." with "Got it". It's a card with a brass edge, like the example banner: red and amber are for money only (DESIGN.md).
- **The stop link's page** (`/stop-get-back-in/`): "Stop someone getting into your account", "Someone asked to get into your PrePenny account with your backup email. If it wasn't you, stop it here. Nothing on your account has changed.", "Stop it"; then "It's stopped", "Nobody can use that request to get in, and this link doesn't work any more. We've told your backup email.", "If you don't know who has your backup email, sign in and change it: More, then Email addresses.", "Go to PrePenny". A used link: "This link has been used already, or the request it was for has ended, so there's nothing to stop. If you think someone got into your account, write to hello@prepenny.co.uk."
- **The emails** are in `supabase/migrations/20261111100000_safer_recovery.sql`, laid out as step 3e's: the code to ask to get back in, the code while it waits, the code to finish, "Someone asked to get into your PrePenny account" with the "Stop it" button, "Getting back into PrePenny was stopped", and "Your PrePenny sign-in email has changed".
- **Signed-in devices:** "Sign out of all other devices", "Signs out every phone and computer but this one, and each removes your plan within a minute. This one stays signed in.", the sheet's "Sign them out" and "Keep them signed in", then "2 other phones and computers are signed out."

Why: the owner asked for the wording of each screen and email in VOICE.md's style.

### D398. How safer recovery is built and tested

Decided 9 Nov 2026 in step 3e hardening.

- The database: `supabase/migrations/20261111100000_safer_recovery.sql`. `app.get_back_in` holds each request; `app.email_codes` notes what each "get back in" code was sent to do; `app.code_tries` and `app.code_locks` hold the caps. `hello` adds the open request's times. `use_get_in_code` is gone; `get_back_in_with_code`, `get_back_in_finished` and `get_back_in_not_finished` are for Supabase's secret key only, and each checks it; `stop_get_back_in`, `stop_get_back_in_with_link` and `end_other_devices` are new, and `end_every_device` is gone.
- The function runs in Supabase's own function runner, on the local Supabase as on the real one. GitHub's end-to-end parts now start it too, which adds its download, under a minute, to each part. The database tests leave it out.
- "Let 24 hours pass", in the tests: the database's times for the request and its codes move back 24 hours, as `makeCodeRunOut` moves a code's (step 3b), and the phone's clock moves on 24 hours with Playwright's clock.
- A new test file, `21-safer-recovery.test.sql` (82 checks, 99 after the outside review, D399), proves what the owner listed: nothing changes in the 24 hours (everything about the account compared before and after), a stop from the link or a signed-in device stops it, the link works once and does nothing else, the caps and lock hold for both kinds of code, a locked address or one that isn't a backup gets the same answers, and nobody can stop, finish or use a code for someone else's account. 624 database checks pass in all.
- Each new rule was broken on purpose, one at a time, to see `21-safer-recovery` fail, then put back: the account changing as the wait starts (23 of 82 checks failed), no 24-hour wait (3), a stop link that works twice (10), no daily cap (5), no lock (9), "Stop it" stopping anyone's request (12), anyone able to type a code or finish without Supabase's secret key (3), and a locked address answering differently (37). With nothing broken, all 82 pass.
- Decided in step 3e hardening: if Supabase's admin route can't finish, the request opens again with the same stop link, so the email's link still works (`get_back_in_not_finished`).
- The step 3e tests that used the old way now use the new one: `19-backup-email` (the code starts the wait), `17-devices` and `18-two-step` (signing out of other devices), and `e2e/emails.spec.ts` test 5 (adding a backup, and the same words for an address that isn't one).
- Screenshots are in `docs/screens/3e-hardening`; step 3e's stay as they were, as its record.

Why: each follows from the owner's instructions where they didn't say exactly.

## From the outside review of step 3e hardening

### D399. What the outside review of step 3e hardening changed

Decided 9 Nov 2026 in step 3e hardening, from an outside review of the whole change. Adds to D391 to D398.

- **A finish that stops part-way is never left half done.** If the get-back-in function stopped between the database saying "finish" and the end (Supabase's admin route not answering, or the database's last step lost), the request could stay "finishing" for good: no notice, no stop, never running out. Now the function asks the database to settle it whenever it isn't sure, and the database settles any left after 5 minutes by itself, the next time it's looked at (every signed-in phone looks every minute): finished, if the address did change, or open again as before, with the same stop link, if it didn't. The old address is always the one from before the finish.
- **A stop at the very moment of finishing wins.** The database marks a request as finishing only if it hasn't been stopped meanwhile, and a stop only if it isn't finishing, one at a time, so a stop is never lost and a stopped request can't finish.
- **"Stop it" says when there was nothing to stop:** "There was nothing to stop: it had already ended, or was just finishing. If you're signed out in the next minute, write to hello@prepenny.co.uk."
- **"Add a backup email" no longer shows that an address is locked.** Its once-a-minute answer was worked out from codes actually sent, so asking for a locked address, then another straight away, gave a different answer from an address that wasn't locked. Now it's worked out from the person's own asks, whether or not a code went.
- **The limits hold for requests made at the same moment.** Asking for codes, typing them and counting tries happen one at a time for each address, and a code can only be used once, so sending many requests at once can't get past 3 codes a day, 5 tries a code or 10 tries a day.
- **The stop link's page works when it's loaded again.** The token is kept for that tab only until it's used. Opened without the whole link, the page says "This page needs the whole link from the email. Open the link in the email from PrePenny again." and asks the server nothing. Its first sentence no longer says nothing has changed before anything has been checked.
- **Smaller:** the scrambled addresses use a secret of the database's own (HMAC-SHA-256), so they can't be checked against guesses without it; the stop link works from a page kept from before an update (stopping touches no plan); stopping a request, or removing the backup, uses up its codes; the wait is remembered in the tab only, so nothing of it is left on a shared computer once the tab is closed; the code box says what the finishing code does; and two indexes were added.
- **Tests added:** two-step sign-in still applies after getting back in (`e2e/recovery.spec.ts` test 3); the 7-day run-out; a finish settled both ways; a stopped request can't be finished; the once-a-minute answer the same for a locked address; the stop page loaded again and without its link. 99 checks in `21-safer-recovery`, 641 database checks in all. Three more rules broken on purpose, each caught: a half-done finish never settled (6 checks failed), no run-out (3), and the once-a-minute answer from codes sent (2).
- **For the owner** (PLAN.md): someone who also signed in with Google keeps that Google sign-in after getting back in with the backup email.
- **Accepted:** anyone holding a phone that's signed in to the account can stop any request to get back in, with one tap, as the owner asked ("one plain way to cancel"). The account's owner can sign that phone out with "Sign out of all other devices" from another device that's signed in, or write to hello@prepenny.co.uk.

Test: `supabase/tests/database/21-safer-recovery.test.sql`, `apps/web/e2e/recovery.spec.ts`.

Why: each was shown by the review with a case that went wrong.

### D400. Step 3d's "same change sent twice" test waits for the first save to arrive

Decided 9 Nov 2026 in step 3e hardening, while running every end-to-end test on the computer it was built on.

- `apps/web/e2e/clashes.spec.ts` test 4 failed here at random, on `main` as it was before this step too (1 run in 2 there), so it wasn't caused by this step. The test had two races of its own:
  - it looked in the database as soon as the first save had been sent, so it could look before the server had it;
  - after the first save arrived, the server's nudge (D339) makes the phone send it again within half a second, so a check that exactly one had been sent so far could miss it.
- Now the test waits until the first save has reached the server, then checks the database, and lets the phone send it again either way. Every check of the outcome is as it was: the same edit sent twice, one edit and one Car MOT saved once, at version 1. Nothing in the app changed.
- It passed 12 runs in 12 at 320px and 360px, in light and dark, afterwards.
- Also found while running every test here: "Continue with Google" test 6 can't reach Google from the local Supabase on this computer, whose network re-signs secure connections; once the local sign-in service was given that certificate, it passed at both widths in light and dark. GitHub's machines reach Google directly, as on every earlier step.

Why: the owner's rule that everything passes on this computer before the pull request is ready, and that no expected number is changed to make a test pass.

## From the owner's answers to step 3e hardening

D401 and D402 are the owner's answers of 9 Nov 2026 to the two questions in step 3e hardening's report, with the owner's reviewer's finding about GitHub's Supabase key, recorded in README.md.

### D401. A finished wait stays open for 7 days: confirmed

Agreed 9 Nov 2026. Confirms D391.

After the 24 hours, the person can finish getting back in for 7 days. After that the request runs out, and they start again, with the sign-in address told again. Nothing changes: it's how step 3e hardening built it.

Why: the owner's answer, as suggested.

### D402. Getting back in removes the Google sign-in too

Agreed 9 Nov 2026. Adds to D391 and D393.

- When getting back in with the backup email finishes, every way of signing in other than the email address is removed from the account: "Continue with Google" now, and anything added later, such as Apple in Phase 6. Whoever has the old Google account can no longer use it to get in. The email sign-in, set to the backup by Supabase's admin route, stays, and the account's list of ways to sign in says email only.
- It happens in the same step that signs every device out, so it's never part-way, and never before the 24 hours (`app.complete_get_back_in`).
- Supabase has no admin route that removes one way of signing in (tried here: none answers). Its only way, "unlink", needs the person signed in and an extra setting on. So the database removes it itself, as Supabase's own unlink does: it deletes that sign-in's row and updates the account's list.
- The person is told: the finishing code's email and the code box say "any Google sign-in is removed"; the sign-in page afterwards says "For your safety, any Google sign-in was removed: to use Google again, choose Continue with Google with a Google account that has this address."; and the old address's email adds "and Continue with Google no longer signs in to it" when there was one.
- Adding it back: choosing "Continue with Google" with a Google account that has the account's sign-in address joins it to the same account, as Supabase joins sign-ins with the same confirmed address. That's Supabase's documented behaviour; it can't be tried here, as the local Supabase can't finish a sign-in with Google. A Google account with another address would make a separate account; a "Add Google" button for that would need Supabase's manual linking switched on, and is for later if wanted.

Test: `supabase/tests/database/21-safer-recovery.test.sql` (Jess's Google sign-in removed, only email left, Tom's kept; with the removal stopped on purpose, 2 checks fail), and `apps/web/e2e/recovery.spec.ts` test 1 (Sam's Google sign-in is there through the 24 hours, and gone once it's finished).

Why: the owner's answer: the old email, and the Google account with it, can't be trusted once someone has had to get back in without them.

## From the owner's instructions for step 3e-2

D403 is the owner's instructions for step 3e-2, a preview database, on 9 Nov 2026, with the owner's report from testing the live app (D404). Points marked "decided in step 3e-2" weren't in the owner's words.

### D403. A preview database, its own key, and the live app untouched

The owner's instruction, 9 Nov 2026. Builds D346; replaces D340 once the preview database is set up, which waits for Supabase's paid plan (D411).

- A second, free Supabase project, **prepenny-preview**, in London, used only by pull request previews. It never holds real people's data, and the preview says so on every page: "Test version: don't use real details." (D407).
- When a pull request is ready, its database changes, the sign-in settings and the get-back-in function go to the preview project, and its preview app is built to use it.
- The preview project has its own GitHub key, kept separately from the real project's. The real project's key (`SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN`) is never changed, replaced or widened. Sign-in emails on previews come through Resend with the key GitHub already has.
- The live app and the real database are untouched by all of this.
- From now on, anything that needs a new permission, secret, variable or setting goes under "What you need to set up" in the step's report, with exact steps, checked against Supabase's and GitHub's own lists.
- Decided in step 3e-2:
  - The Deploy workflow does it, in a job of its own after the preview is online, "Get the preview database ready, and try the preview". It's given the preview project's three secrets (`PREVIEW_SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN`, `PREVIEW_SUPABASE_PROJECT_REF`, `PREVIEW_SUPABASE_DB_PASSWORD`), each only in the steps that use it, and never the real project's key, password or Project ID. The preview app is built with two variables, `PREVIEW_SUPABASE_URL` and `PREVIEW_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`; a preview never falls back to the real project's, and isn't built at all if they name the real one.
  - It asks nothing of a project until its address is the preview app's and isn't the real project's, and changes nothing on it unless Supabase says it's called `prepenny-preview`, so a mistyped Project ID can't empty the real database (D410).
  - The preview's sign-in settings are the real project's (`supabase/sign-in-settings.json`), except that the sign-in email's link opens the preview put online last, only preview addresses may be sent back to, emails come from "PrePenny (test version)" and Google is off: Google's sign-in is set up for the real project only. The database's own emails (backup email, getting back in) go through Resend from "PrePenny (test version)" too, with their links to the preview put online last. Signing in by typing the code works on every preview; the link comment and README.md say to type it.
  - Until the preview project is set up, previews go online with signing in off, as before step 3b.

Test: `.github/workflows/deploy.yml`, tried on this step's own pull request (D408). README.md, "The preview database", has the owner's steps.

Why: the owner's instruction. Steps 3c, 3d and 3e could only be tried on the live app after merging, and two problems were found that way.

## Made during step 3e-2

D404 to D409 were decided on 9 Nov 2026 while building step 3e-2.

### D404. Why "Someone asked to get into your account" needed a refresh, and the fix

The owner's report from testing the live app, 9 Nov 2026: the notice appeared on their signed-in phone only after the page was refreshed. It should appear by itself within a minute, on every signed-in device. Adds to D381 and D391.

**What was found.** Every situation a phone is in was tried here against the local Supabase, timing how long the notice took with no reload: on screen with live updates (under a second), on screen with no live updates (57 seconds, the look every minute), in the background or frozen as Chrome freezes a background tab (seconds after coming back), and with the sign-in's hour-long token run out on screen, in the background and frozen (seconds after coming back). All showed it. Two things didn't:

- **One request with no answer stopped everything after it.** A phone that sleeps, or moves between Wi-Fi and mobile data, part-way through a request can be left waiting for an answer that never comes, for many minutes. Supabase's sign-in code shares one request to renew the sign-in between every request that needs it, and PrePenny's looks at the account run one at a time. So one request to renew the sign-in, or to bring the plan down, left with no answer held up every look after it, the notice included, until the page was loaded again. Played here by leaving one request unanswered: the notice never came in 2.5 minutes, and nothing more was asked of the server. A reload starts afresh, which is why refreshing showed it. This is the only way found that gives exactly what the owner saw; their phone can't be seen from here.
- **A phone on the previous version got no notice.** `hello` gave the request only to an app built for the very same database, so for a while after every update, until a phone loaded the new version at a safe moment (D386), it was told nothing.

**The fix.**

- Every request PrePenny sends has a time limit of 30 seconds (`lib/time-limit.ts`): one with no answer by then is given up, as with no signal, and the next look asks again. Supabase's own code uses it too, renewing the sign-in included, and so do the get-back-in function and the check of which version is live.
- The notice is asked for on its own (`lookForGetBackIn` in `data/sync.ts`) every minute, whether PrePenny is on screen or not, when it comes back on screen or gets a signal, and on every live nudge, so a look at the account under way can never hold it up.
- `hello` tells any version whose sign-in stands about an open request (migration `20261112100000_notice_for_every_version.sql`): it's about the person's own account and holds no plan data. Plan data still goes only to the version the database is (D386).
- So the notice shows within a minute of the request; after a request with no answer, the look waits for that request's 30 seconds at most first.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/recovery.spec.ts` test 4: the phone's live updates are cut off and one of its requests is left with no answer, then someone starts getting back in on another phone, and the notice shows without the page loading again, within a minute and the time the look takes, at 320px and 360px in light and dark. It fails without the fix. `supabase/tests/database/22-notice-for-every-version.test.sql`, 12 checks, 4 of which fail with the old `hello`; `apps/web/test/unit/time-limit.test.ts`.

Why: the owner's report, and their instruction to find the cause.

### D405. Two open pull requests and one preview database

Decided 9 Nov 2026 in step 3e-2, from the owner's instruction to decide how two open pull requests don't get in each other's way.

- **One at a time.** Only one pull request's preview changes the preview database, and is tried on it, at a time; the next waits its turn (a GitHub concurrency group, `preview-database`).
- **It follows the pull request whose preview went online last.** Each database change is known by its version and a fingerprint of its words (SHA-256), kept in the preview database itself, in a schema of its own the Data API never shows (`preview`). Then (`scripts/preview-database.ts`):
  - it already has exactly the pull request's database changes: nothing changes, and anyone signed up on it stays signed up. Most pull requests don't change the database, so they all share it;
  - it has every one but the pull request's newest: those are added (`supabase db push`);
  - anything else, such as another pull request's change it doesn't have, or a change written differently in a later commit: it starts again, empty, with exactly this pull request's changes (`supabase db reset --linked --no-seed`), never the tests' made-up people. Tried here on the local database by its other address, the way a hosted one is reset: it removed every account and plan and put back all 14 database changes in about a second.
- **The other pull requests' previews** then have a different database from the one they were built for, so they say "Changes can't be sent right now. They're saved on this phone." and lose nothing (D386), until theirs are put online again. The preview database notes every pull request sharing it (`preview.pull_requests`), and GitHub comments on each still open to say so, and how to re-run it.
- **GitHub keeps only one waiting in line.** A third preview put online while one changes the preview database and another waits cancels the one waiting. Its link is posted already, by the job that put it online, and README.md says to re-run it.
- Chosen over a database for each pull request, which needs Supabase's paid branching, and over starting again at every push, which would sign the owner out of every preview after each small fix.

Test: `scripts/preview-database.test.ts`; the workflow, on this step's own pull request.

Why: the owner's instruction.

### D406. The key check

Decided 9 Nov 2026 in step 3e-2, from the owner's instruction: the owner had to make the Supabase key four times, one missing permission at a time.

- At the start of the Database workflow, and of the preview's job, before anything changes, `scripts/check-supabase-key.ts` tries each of the eleven permissions on Supabase's token page: Project Settings, Auth Config, Database, Migrations and Edge Functions: Read-write; API Keys, API Key Secrets, Data API Config, Database Config, Connection Pooling and Storage Config: Read. Missing any, it stops with one message listing all of them, as the token page names them, and says what to do.
- Supabase has no way to ask a key what it may do, so each permission is tried with a call that needs it and changes nothing: a read; the project's name set to what it is already; a table that lasts only for its own query ("on commit drop"), which a read-only query can't make; or a change to something that can't exist (a sign-in provider with the nil id, which Supabase's description allows; a migration from 1970; a function never made), which Supabase answers "not found" only once the key may make it. Where Supabase might let a call through without the permission and answer in a weaker way, the answer must show it: the table made, and a secret key revealed in full (never kept or printed). On a brand-new project, the list of database changes may not exist yet, so any answer but a refusal counts there. Which call needs which permission is from Supabase's own description of its API ("x-fga-permissions", from github.com/supabase/supabase on 9 Nov 2026). It shows, for example, that changing the sign-in settings needs Project Settings: Read-write as well as Auth Config: Read-write. The revealed API keys are never kept or printed, and nor is the key.
- What Supabase's command line asks for was watched here against a pretend Supabase: connecting asks for the project, its API keys with their secrets, its storage settings and its connection pooling; putting the function online, Edge Functions: Read-write. Its database changes go over the database connection itself, which couldn't be watched here, so the list is the owner's own, which works on the real project.
- A key Supabase doesn't accept, a project it can't find, and a Supabase that doesn't answer clearly each say so instead, never "missing".
- README.md, "The database", step 5, lists the eleven exactly as the token page names them, and "The preview database" the same for the preview key.

Test: `scripts/check-supabase-key.test.ts`, against a pretend Supabase that refuses each call the key lacks a permission for, as the description says: a key with every permission passes and nothing changes; keys missing two (Migrations: Read only and no Edge Functions; Project Settings: Read only and no API Key Secrets) stop with both listed in one message and nothing changed; a key with none lists all eleven; and run as a command it fails the step and never prints the key. The real calls are tried on this step's own pull request, with the preview key.

Why: the owner's instruction.
