Never red or amber, which are for money only (DESIGN.md). The live app and the tests' own copy never have it.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/preview.spec.ts` on the real preview; here, every screen check (`checks.spec.ts`, `screens.spec.ts`, 179 checks with axe at 320px and 360px) passed on a preview copy, with no sideways scroll. Screenshots: `docs/screens/3e-2`.

Why: the owner's instruction, in the owner's words.

### D408. A preview is tried on two phones

Decided 9 Nov 2026 in step 3e-2, from the owner's test: a pull request preview signs in, saves a plan and syncs between two devices on the preview project, and the live app and real database don't change.

- After the preview database is ready, `apps/web/e2e/preview.spec.ts` runs against the preview just put online, on two phones, one 320px wide in light and one 360px in dark: the first makes a plan and signs in, the plan goes up, the second signs in and brings it down, and a balance updated on the second shows on the first.
- Every address each phone asks is noted: the only Supabase project either asks is the preview's, never the real one's. The live app says the same version (`/version.json` at prepenny.co.uk) before and after. And the job is never given the real project's key, password or Project ID, so it couldn't change the real database if it tried.
- Signing in uses links the preview project makes with its own secret key, read by the job when it needs it and never printed, so no email is sent. The test's accounts and their plans are removed from the preview database afterwards, by their address: removing a person with a plan isn't built yet (step 3h).
- It adds about 3 to 4 minutes to each push of a ready pull request (D300).

Test: the workflow, on this step's own pull request. Here, the same test passed against a preview copy served on this computer with the local Supabase, and a stand-in for the live site, which this computer can't reach.

Why: the owner's test.

### D409. Smaller choices in step 3e-2

- A pull request's preview is never put online as the live app. Until step 3e-2, the very first preview was, because Cloudflare needs a live app to make previews of; now that would be a test version with the preview database, so the job stops instead and says to put `main` online first.
- The preview database gets the Resend key as a value beside its query, never written into the query's words, so no log of them holds it. The Database workflow's own step is left as it is: it changes the real project.

Decided 9 Nov 2026 in step 3e-2.

- The preview's Data API is checked as the real one's is, but never changed: the key reads those settings only (Data API Config: Read).
- The preview job's link comment says what happened to the preview database ("nothing changes and test accounts stay", "the new one is added", or that it started again) and whether the try on two phones passed.
- The database tests ran here first through pgTAP inside the local database, file by file, as `supabase test db` does, while Docker Hub refused to send its test runner's image (too many requests), then with `supabase test db` itself on a fresh database: 656 checks either way.
- Screenshots of step 3e-2 are in `docs/screens/3e-2`.

Why: each follows from the owner's instructions where they didn't say exactly.

## From the outside review of step 3e-2

### D410. What the outside review of step 3e-2 changed

Decided 9 Nov 2026 in step 3e-2, from an outside review of the whole change. Adds to D403 to D409.

- **Nothing is asked of a project before it's known to be the preview one.** The key check's calls, which change nothing but do write, ran before the project's name was checked, and removing the try's test accounts ran even when the checks had failed. Now the preview's address is checked against its Project ID and against the real project's address first, with no call to Supabase; the key check reads the project's name first and stops before anything else if it isn't `prepenny-preview` (`EXPECT_PROJECT_NAME`); and the test accounts are removed only after the key check passed.
- **The key check can't be fooled by a weaker answer.** If Supabase ran a query read-only, or hid the secret keys, rather than refusing, the check would have passed a key without Database: Read-write or API Key Secrets: Read. Now the answer must show the permission (D406). And a brand-new project, whose list of database changes may fail before its first change, no longer stops the very first run.
- **A preview waiting its turn keeps its link.** GitHub cancels all but one waiting job, which took the link with it; now the link is posted when the preview goes online, and the next job updates it (D405).
- **Every pull request sharing the preview database is told,** not just the last one (D405).
- **The sign-in email's link opens the preview put online last,** so the comment and README.md say to type the code (D403).
- **The preview can't ever go live,** even the first time (D409).
- **Smaller:** the look at the preview database shows its own errors; the try's report and traces are kept when it fails; the key check points the preview key to "The preview database", steps 6 to 8; the set-up message says what's true when only some secrets are there; a merge to `main` during the try no longer fails it, as long as the live app isn't the preview; and the notice's own look asks again when a nudge comes while it's asking, and leaves out an answer that comes back after "Stop it".
- **For the owner:** the real project's key must have all eleven permissions, or the Database run after merging stops before changing anything, and phones wait to send changes until it has run (README.md, "The database"). The owner's list is the one the real key was made with.
- **Accepted:** the real project's secrets are repository secrets, so keeping them from the preview job relies on the workflow, which every pull request's review can see. Moving them to a GitHub environment for `main` only would make GitHub enforce it; it's for later, as it changes how the real key is kept.

Test: `scripts/check-supabase-key.test.ts` (11 checks: a weaker answer, a brand-new project and a wrong project added), `scripts/preview-database.test.ts`.

Why: each was shown by the review with a case that went wrong.

### D411. The preview database waits for Supabase's paid plan

The owner's decision, 10 Nov 2026. Puts D403's preview database off until the paid plan; keeps D404, D406 and D410.

- Supabase's free plan allows only two active projects, and both are in use, so prepenny-preview can't be made now. While PrePenny has no real users, the owner tests each step on the live app after merging, with a spare email address. The preview database waits until the move to Supabase's paid plan before launch: making prepenny-preview and its key (README.md, "The preview database") is on the launch checklist beside that move.
- Kept and switched on: the key check at the start of the Database workflow (D406, D410), README.md's list of the eleven permissions as Supabase's token page names them, and the notice that appears within a minute without a refresh (D404).
- Kept but switched off: the preview database's code. Decided in step 3e-2:
  - The preview database's job runs only once the preview project's two variables, `PREVIEW_SUPABASE_URL` and `PREVIEW_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY`, are there. Until then GitHub skips it, so it shows as skipped, never red, and nothing is asked of any Supabase project.
  - A preview is still built with signing in off, never with the real project's address or key, and without a warning, since that's the plan for now. It still says "Test version: don't use real details." at the top.
  - Its link comment says plainly what a preview can and can't do: everything kept on the phone works (the welcome, setup, the example plan, and every screen and change); signing in, saving the plan in an account and keeping two devices in step are tried on the live app after merging.
  - Once the three secrets and two variables are added, it all switches on by itself, with nothing else to change. Its first run is its real test: the parts that talk to a hosted Supabase project couldn't be tried from here.
- D340 holds again until then: a step's database changes can only be tried once it's merged.

Test: the Deploy workflow on this step's pull request, with no preview secrets or variables: the preview is put online with signing in off, the preview database's job is skipped, and every other check passes.

Why: the owner's decision.

## From the owner's instructions for step 3f

D412 to D416 come from the owner's instructions for step 3f, shared plans: invite and join, on 10 Nov 2026. Points marked "decided in step 3f" weren't in the owner's words. D417 to D425 were decided while building it, the same day.

### D412. Invites: by link or by email, 7 days, once, and nobody joins without "Allow"

The owner's instruction, 10 Nov 2026. Builds brief 4.14 and ARCHITECTURE.md 3.2.

- "Share with my partner", under More, makes an invite by a link the person sends themselves, or by an email from PrePenny. It says what it gives: "Can edit" (the default) or "Can view only".
- An invite lasts 7 days and works once. A link can be forwarded, so opening it lets nobody in: the person who opens it asks to join, and the inviter sees "Jess wants to join your plan" with "Allow" and "Don't allow". "Don't allow" ends the invite for good.
- The server checks every role (D51): a person with "Can view only" can't change anything shared, apart from their own pay (D311).
- Decided in step 3f:
  - **Only the plan's owner invites.** Someone with "Can edit" can't, and nobody can invite themselves into someone else's plan: making, cancelling and answering an invite are the server's own steps, each checking who's asking, and nobody can write an invite or a membership straight through the Data API.
  - **The first person who asks uses it up.** Anyone else opening the same link, then or later, is told "This invite has been used already." The person who asked sees whether they were let in.
  - **One invite at a time.** Making a new one cancels any not answered yet, and "Cancel this invite" cancels it. A plan with two people in it can't make one (brief 4.14's limit, kept as `max_members`).
  - **The link's secret** is 32 random bytes, kept only scrambled (SHA-256), after a "#" in the link so it never reaches PrePenny's site or its logs, and taken out of the address as soon as the join page has read it. While the person signs in or signs up it waits on that phone only, and goes once used, or at signing out.
  - **By email:** the database sends it through Resend, as step 3e's emails, with the link as a button. It holds the inviter's name and no amount, payment name or note. At most 3 invite emails a day for a plan, so PrePenny can't be used to send its emails to strangers; a link still works after that.
  - **Run out:** an invite asked with but not answered in its 7 days can't let anyone in either.

Test: `supabase/tests/database/23-invite-and-join.test.sql` (77 checks), `apps/web/e2e/sharing.spec.ts` tests 1 to 3.

Why: the owner's instruction.

### D413. Sharing an account is the server's own step

The owner's instruction, 10 Nov 2026 (PLAN.md items 26 and 34). Replaces D290's "sharing-comes-later".

- A private account becomes shared only when its own person chooses it, on "Choose shared accounts", and everything on it changes privacy in one step of the server's own (`share_accounts`), or nothing does.
- Decided in step 3f:
  - **What moves with it:** its balance updates, budgets and their spends, payments with their one-off changes and ticks, and overtime on pay in it, as amounts locked to their person (D303). Each is saved again through the same checks, which fill its privacy in from the account, so nothing can be left private on a shared account, or shared by mistake.
  - **What stays private:** savings goals, debts and jobs, which are chosen on their own (D65, D303), and the person's other accounts. A payment in the account that names one of them keeps the link privately, in the person's own private links (D291), which the server makes with the same ids the phone works out (`app.derived_id`, from `lib/sync-ids.ts`), so the phone and the server agree on every row.
  - **Money between two of the person's accounts can't cross from shared to private.** Sharing one of them alone is refused, naming the other: "Money moves between these and Savings, which you're keeping private. Tick Savings too, or leave them all private." Money kept as "From Jess" in a shared account (D16) is refused too, naming it, until it's removed and added again after sharing (D425).
  - **Only someone who can edit shares an account**, and only their own private ones. With "Can view only", sharing would make their own records shared ones they could no longer change.
  - **A shared account can't be made private again** for now. The sheet that asks first says so. Unsharing is with leaving, in step 3g (D425).
  - **A shared account names no one person** (`belongs_to` empty), as every phone keeps it. Found by the two-phone tests: the server first kept the person's name on it, so the partner's phone, which keeps it empty, sent a change to say so with its next change, and with "Can view only" the whole change was refused.
  - It needs a signal and nothing waiting to be sent, because it's made on what the account has now: "Some changes on this phone haven't been sent to your account yet. Try again once they have."

Test: `supabase/tests/database/24-sharing-accounts.test.sql` (43 checks), `apps/web/e2e/sharing.spec.ts` tests 1 to 3.

Why: the owner's instruction, and D290's reason: one forgotten field would put Jess's private payment where Tom can read it.

### D414. What was removed before sharing stays private

Decided 10 Nov 2026 in step 3f. Adds to D413 and changes D299's family check.

- Records removed before an account is shared (kept 90 days so every phone hears they've gone, ARCHITECTURE.md 3.1) stay private: a partner never gets what was removed, such as "Gift for Tom", even as a removed record.
- So a family can hold a removed private series beside shared ones. The family check (`app.family_problem`) now leaves out a removed series' privacy, lock and pay when comparing; it must still be on the same account, and a series still joins only a family it was split into. Overtime takes its privacy from a payment still in its family.

Test: `24-sharing-accounts.test.sql` ("What she removed before sharing stays private", and Tom changing a shared payment whose family has a removed private one).

Why: the owner's rule, "a partner never receives anything private, before or after an account is shared".

### D415. "From Jess, £600": money between a private account and a shared one

The owner's instruction, 10 Nov 2026 (D16, D52, D94).

- Decided in step 3f:
  - **Add a payment** between two accounts, one the person's own private account and the other shared, makes the two linked halves of D16 in one change with one undo: money going out of (or into) their own account, with the name and note they typed, and "From Jess" arriving in (or "To Jess" leaving) the shared account, which carries the date, the amount, how often and whether it's an estimate, and nothing else. The engine's "Add a payment" takes the other half (`otherHalf`); the app decides when, from which accounts are shared.
  - "Jess" is the name she gave (D420). With none yet, "From your partner".
  - Only Jess can change it, as before (D293). On Tom's phone, tapping it says "Only Jess can change this. It's money between Jess's own account and this one."
  - Removing it before its first payment removes both halves (the engine's `delete-payment` now reaches the other half).
  - A savings goal stays on her half only when it's kept in her own account.
  - With "Can view only", adding it is refused like any other new shared record (D51, D292): "From Jess" lands in the shared account. Her own half alone, in her own account, is hers to add.

Test: `packages/engine/test/unit/add-private-transfer.test.ts`, `apps/web/test/unit/sharing.test.ts`, `apps/web/e2e/sharing.spec.ts` test 1. Screenshot: `docs/screens/3f/from-jess-360-light.png`.

Why: D16, D52 and D94 decided the rule; nothing could make the two halves until now.

### D418. Shared pay someone else added can be claimed: "This is my pay" (D314)

Decided 10 Nov 2026 in step 3f, as the owner asked: decide and explain plainly. Replaces D349.

- **What it means.** When Tom types Jess's pay into the joint account before she joins, PrePenny records it as Tom's pay, because he added it (D303). Then Jess, with "Can view only", can't type its real amount (D311), and only Tom could add overtime to it. Until now she had to remove it and add it again, which "Can view only" can't do.
- **Decided: yes.** On a shared payment that's Pay or Benefits and someone else's, the edit sheet says "Tom added this as their pay." with "This is my pay". One tap makes it hers, for every payment of it, past and future. Then she can change it, even with "Can view only", and add her overtime or a missed shift to it.
- **When it can't:** while the other person has overtime, a missed shift or their job linked to it, which only they can take off ("Tom has overtime or job details on it, so it can't be made yours. Ask Tom to take them off first."). Never "From Jess", rent or anything that isn't pay, and never anything private.
- **Anyone in the plan can claim it, with either role,** because the person whose pay it is should be able to look after it. A claim made by mistake can be claimed back the same way. It's an edit like any other, so it's in the history for Activity (step 3g) and reaches the other phone.

Test: `24-sharing-accounts.test.sql` (claiming, its refusals, and claiming back), `apps/web/e2e/sharing.spec.ts` test 2.

Why: without it, a partner with "Can view only" could never look after pay typed in for them, which D311 is for.

## From the owner's answers and instructions for step 3f-2

D426 to D434 come from the owner's message of 10 Nov 2026, after step 3f was merged: the answers to step 3f's four questions (D426, D432 to D434), the security tests for bringing a plan (D427), and four fixes from testing the live app (D428 to D431). Points marked "decided in step 3f-2" weren't in the owner's words. D435 to D437 were decided while building it, the same day, and D438 comes from its outside review.

### D426. Joining with a plan of your own brings it with you

The owner's answer (a) to step 3f's question 2, 10 Nov 2026. Replaces D416.

- When Sam joins Jess's plan, his own plan's accounts and everything on them move into her plan, private to him until he chooses to share them. Nothing is lost or retyped. If his plan uses other bank holidays (his nation), he's told plainly before joining what that changes. It's built on the server in one step: all of it moves, or nothing does. His old plan is gone afterwards.
- Decided in step 3f-2:
  - **Who moves it.** Only Sam's own request ever moves his plan. Jess's "Allow" moves nothing: for someone with a plan of their own, it marks the invite allowed (`invites.allowed_at`, state "allowed") and nudges his phones. His phone then sends everything it has to the account, and asks the server to bring the plan in (`public.join_with_my_plan`). Someone with no plan joins at "Allow", as in step 3f.
  - **What moves.** Every record of his plan, live and removed, keeps its own id and everything in it, and is saved again in her plan through the same checks as every record, so the database fills in each one's privacy from what it's on: all of it private to him. His settings (his main pay) and his change history come too, private to him. The move is one edit of his, private to him (`join-with-plan`). Rows whose id is worked out from their plan (a one-off change, a tick, overtime's details, his settings, `lib/sync-ids.ts`) get the id his phone works out for her plan, so the phone and the server agree on every row.
  - **An account he'd shared before anyone joined** becomes private to him like the rest. Links kept apart because a shared payment named something private (D291) are joined back on the payment, as everything is his now. Money kept as "From Sam" in that account (D16) becomes a payment of his own: its two halves are no longer kept in step, and every amount and date stays the same.
  - **Bank holidays.** Ticks and one-off changes are kept by a payment's date before any bank holiday move, so every one stays on its payment; only the day a payment set to move for a bank holiday lands on can change. Once Jess has let him in, the join page says: "Bank holidays: Jess's plan uses Scotland, and yours uses England and Wales. Once you join, a payment set to move for a bank holiday follows Scotland, so it may move to a different day." and he chooses "Bring my plan and join"; with the same bank holidays, his plan comes straight away. Before she lets him in, nothing about her plan is shown, not even its bank holidays (D439, replacing "before he asks"). If Jess changed hers in between, the server refuses ("nation-changed") and he's told again.
  - **What the join page says** before he asks: "You have a plan of your own, and it comes with you: your accounts and everything on them move into Jess's plan, private to you." and "Jess sees none of it until you choose to share an account. Nothing has to be typed again, and you'll have one plan: Jess's, with yours in it." Once allowed: "Jess let you in." then, as it moves, "Jess let you in. Bringing your plan into Jess's plan." If something on his phone hasn't been sent: "Some changes on this phone haven't been sent to your account yet. Stay online, then try again: your plan comes with you once they have." If the server couldn't move it: "Your plan couldn't be moved, so nothing has changed: you're still in your own plan. Write to hello@prepenny.co.uk and we'll look into it." A plan on the phone that the account hasn't been given yet is saved there first ("Saving your plan in your account first.").
  - **"Finish joining".** Someone who closed the join page before it was allowed sees, on every screen of the app, "Jess has let you into their plan", "Finish joining to bring your plan into it. It moves in one go, private to you." and "Finish joining", which opens the join page for that invite (`/join/#invite=…`, `public.look_at_my_invite`), so the move happens on a page where nothing else is changing.
  - **What Jess sees.** After "Allow": "Sam can join now. It finishes on Sam's phone, as soon as PrePenny is open there." The same line shows on Share with my partner until he has joined, and his place is kept: she can't invite someone else meanwhile, and "Cancel this invite" ends it. Then "Who's in this plan" lists him by the name he gave. Nothing of his plan reaches her phone.
  - **The invite** still runs out 7 days after it was made, allowed or not. Once someone joins a plan, by "Allow" or by bringing theirs, any other ask of theirs, waiting or allowed, is cancelled: they can't join two plans, so no other plan keeps a place for them (D438).
  - **Who can bring a plan:** someone whose own plan has nobody else in it. Someone in someone else's plan, or whose own plan is shared with someone, is told "You're already in a shared plan in PrePenny, so you can't join Tom's plan as well. PrePenny keeps one plan for each person." Leaving comes in step 3g.

Test: `supabase/tests/database/25-bring-your-plan.test.sql` (54 checks), `apps/web/e2e/sharing.spec.ts` test 4, `apps/web/test/unit/sharing.test.ts`. Screenshots: `docs/screens/3f-2`.

Why: the owner's answer.

### D428. Invite links always use prepenny.co.uk, and the old address sends people there

The owner's fix B, 10 Nov 2026: an invite link used https://prepenny.prepenny.workers.dev/join because the phone had the old address open.

- Invite links always use https://prepenny.co.uk. Anyone opening the old address, prepenny.prepenny.workers.dev, is sent to the same page on prepenny.co.uk. Pull request previews (pr-N-prepenny.prepenny.workers.dev) keep working as they are.
- Decided in step 3f-2:
  - **The link** is made from `siteOrigin()` (`lib/site.ts`): prepenny.co.uk wherever the live app was opened; a preview's own address on a preview, and this computer's in the tests, so their links come back to them. The invite email's link already came from the database's own setting, prepenny.co.uk on the live project.
  - **On Cloudflare**, a few lines now run in front of the app's pages (`apps/web/cloudflare/worker.ts`, `main` and `assets.run_worker_first` in `wrangler.jsonc`): a page opened at the old address gets "moved permanently" (301) to the same page on prepenny.co.uk, with its query; a browser keeps what follows a "#", such as an invite's secret. Only a page the browser is opening moves (`Sec-Fetch-Mode: navigate`, or for an older browser a request for a web page); the app's other files are still served at the old address, so a phone that installed PrePenny there can update its offline helper. Every other address is served exactly as before, with the same security headers (`public/_headers`) and redirects (`public/_redirects`), checked by the tests. The app's files skip the script altogether: everything under `/_next/`, `/version.json` (which a signed-in phone checks every minute), the offline helper, the icons, the engine's file and the app's details (D438).
  - **A phone whose offline helper serves the old address** never asks Cloudflare for the page, so the app moves itself there (`components/OldAddress.tsx`) once the helper has the new version. A browser keeps each address's things apart, so a phone holding something there that isn't saved in an account yet (a plan never saved, part of one still going up, or changes not sent) stays where it is and says why: "PrePenny's address is now prepenny.co.uk. This phone has changes that aren't saved in your account yet, so they stay here for now. Sign in and stay online until they're sent, then open prepenny.co.uk." Nothing is lost by moving.
  - **What was checked, and how.** Cloudflare's own documentation couldn't be reached from the computer this was built on (its address doesn't resolve there), as in D322. Instead: Wrangler 4.151.3's own settings description allows `run_worker_first` as a list with "!" for paths that skip the script; its own code uploads a script with files through the same address as files alone (`/accounts/…/workers/scripts/prepenny`, with its `assets-upload-session`), so the Cloudflare key needs nothing new (D322's "Account, Workers Scripts, Edit"); a dry-run deploy accepts the settings, with a script of 1.1 KB; and Wrangler run on this computer, the way the tests serve the app, applies the security headers and the Coming up redirect to what the script passes on.
  - **Cloudflare's free plan.** The script runs only when a page is opened, never for the app's files. On Workers' free plan each run counts towards a daily allowance; from memory, as the page couldn't be opened, it's 100,000 a day, far more than PrePenny needs before launch. Launch checklist item 5 already has the owner check Cloudflare's plan before launch.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/old-address.spec.ts`, against a second copy of the app served by Wrangler exactly as Cloudflare serves it, with no custom domain listed, because Wrangler run locally gives every request the listed domain's address (Miniflare's "upstream"), so the main copy can't show the old address. The browser is told the old address and a preview's are this computer. Chromium can't hand a test the second step of a move made by a server, and prepenny.co.uk can't be reached from here, so the test checks the browser asks for the same page on prepenny.co.uk straight after the old address answers; the app's own move is followed to the end. Turning the move off makes 3 of its checks fail. `test/unit/sharing.test.ts` checks the link's address and the page kept with its "#".

Why: the owner's instruction.

### D430. Home's big figure: "About £X now"

The line under the figure says "Bank: £267.54 today" or "Bank: £500.00 on Sun 8 Nov" since D450.

In Sam's example, the line under the figure is replaced by D440: "Example plan, as on Sun 25 Oct 2026", and the bank's figure without "today". A person's own plan keeps these words.

The owner's fix D, 10 Nov 2026. Replaces brief 4.9's "Balance and when it was checked" as Home's big figure, keeping both in the line under it. Adds to D168.

- Home's big figure is "About £X now", worked out with brief 5.5 from the last balance check and every payment up to and including today, Not ticked yet included, with a line under it such as "Your bank showed £500 on Sun 8 Nov". When nothing has happened since the check, it stays simple, for example "£500, checked today".
- Decided in step 3f-2:
  - The figure is the engine's own balance at the end of today, the same "expected" figure Update balance shows (D46), so the two screens always agree.
  - "Something has happened" means any payment, budget or spend that moves money in or out of the account between the check and the end of today, a tick after the check included. A £0.00 payment doesn't count.
  - **The words, case by case** (Sam's example on Sun 25 Oct, and a plan checked at £500.00 on Sun 8 Nov with a £20.00 phone bill on Tue 10 Nov):
    - checked today, nothing since: "£500.00" and "Checked today, Sun 8 Nov" (as before);
    - checked on an earlier day, nothing since: "£500.00" and "Checked Sun 8 Nov" (as before);
    - money moved since a check today: "About £237.54 now" and "Your bank showed £267.54 today" (Sam's example, after the £30.00 spending money due today);
    - money moved since a check on an earlier day: "About £480.00 now" and "Your bank showed £500.00 on Sun 8 Nov", on the bill's day and every day after it while it isn't ticked.
  - "About" and "now" sit smaller beside the figure, in the muted words-on-navy colour, and the figure is sized so all three fit on one line where they can; with very large text the words go on to the next line, never the figure itself. Screen readers hear "About £237.54 now".
  - Amounts keep their pence (£500.00), as every balance does; only a warning level in a sentence drops ".00" (D166).
  - Three older end-to-end checks of the old "Checked …" line change with it, each because money had moved since the check: `home.spec.ts` (Sam's example on Sun 25 Oct now shows "About £237.54 now" and "Your bank showed £267.54 today") and `actions.spec.ts` test 2, before Update balance and after its Undo, on Fri 30 Oct ("About £604.53 now", the £604.53 that Update balance expects in the same test, and "Your bank showed £267.54 on Sun 25 Oct"). The unit test of Home's account gains its two new fields (`now` £237.54, `movedSinceCheck`). Every other figure is unchanged.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/answers-3f-2.spec.ts` (fix D: before, on and after a payment's day, and Sam's example), `apps/web/test/unit/home.test.ts` (the figure is Update balance's expected figure on every day of a month of Sam's example). Decided 10 Nov 2026 in step 3f-2.

## From the outside review of step 3f-2

### D438. What the outside review of step 3f-2 changed

Decided 10 Nov 2026 in step 3f-2, from an outside review of the whole step. Adds to D426 to D430.

- **Making a plan and "Allow" at the same moment could put someone in two plans.** "Allow" for someone with no plan checked they were in none without waiting for anything else they were doing, so Tom making a plan of his own while Jess let him in left him in both. Reproduced on this computer with two database connections at once: without the fix, Tom was in 2 plans; with it, 1. Now "Allow" takes the asker's own row first, as making a plan and bringing one do, so each waits for the other: Jess's "Allow" then sees Tom's new plan, and lets him bring it.
- **Bringing a plan checks again, once it holds the plan, that nobody was let into it meanwhile** from another of Sam's phones, who would have gone with it.
- **Joining withdraws the person's other asks.** Let in by two plans, Sam joining one left the other's invite allowed: "Finish joining" stayed on every screen for up to 7 days, leading only to "already in a shared plan", and the other owner couldn't invite anyone. Now joining cancels every other ask of theirs, waiting or allowed, and a place is kept, and "Finish joining" shown, only for someone who can still join. Someone who has let a person into their own plan, while that person is bringing theirs, can't bring their own plan anywhere meanwhile, as the place is kept (D436).
- **Anything that stops a move is "bad-record"**, so the join page always says "Your plan couldn't be moved, so nothing has changed: you're still in your own plan…", never "couldn't reach its server".
- **"Cancel this invite" says what the invite is** when it's no longer open, such as joined; the screen then says "This has been answered already." rather than "The invite is cancelled."
- **The join page:** "Try again" really tries to save the phone's plan in the account again; a phone whose plan isn't the account's goes to "Choose which plan to keep" first (D324); when the bank holidays changed meanwhile it looks again before anything could move on the old ones; once the plan has moved, it shows "joined" with "Go to the plan" if the plan can't come down at once; and at the old address it reads the link after the move to prepenny.co.uk has taken the whole address.
- **A plan with nothing in it never holds up joining** with "haven't been sent".
- **Cloudflare's few lines run only for pages.** `/version.json`, which a signed-in phone checks every minute, ran them on prepenny.co.uk too; with Workers' free daily allowance, many phones left open could have used it up, and then the live site itself. Now the app's files skip them (D428), checked on this computer path by path.
- **Smaller:** an index for each person's own asks, which every phone looks at every minute; the old address's words no longer say "your account" to someone who never made one.
- **Accepted:** Sam's unread clash notices and the invites he made for his old plan go with it. A database rule that refuses two plans for one person outright, beside the row locks, would refuse the accounts with two plans from before D352, so it isn't added.

Test: `25-bring-your-plan.test.sql` (59 checks, 5 new: no "Finish joining" left behind, the other owner's invite cancelled, "Cancel this invite" saying "joined", only its owner cancelling, and no plan to bring while someone is bringing theirs in), `e2e/old-address.spec.ts` (a join link the offline helper serves at the old address arrives with its secret; the app's files skip the script).

Why: each was shown by the review with a case that went wrong.

## Before merging step 3f-2

### D439. GitHub's failed phone tests, and the bank holidays only after "Allow"

Decided 11 Nov 2026, from the owner's review of the ready pull request. Adds to D426 and D428; replaces D436's first point.

- **What failed on GitHub.** On the ready run, end-to-end parts 2 and 3 failed in one file only, `e2e/old-address.spec.ts`: its 3 tests that open an address in the browser failed in light and dark at both widths (12 failures), each with "ERR_SSL_PROTOCOL_ERROR at http://pr-31-prepenny.prepenny.workers.dev:4174/" or waiting for a page that never came. Every sign-in and sharing test passed.
- **Why.** Chrome only ever opens an address ending `.dev` over https: Google, which runs `.dev`, has every browser treat the whole ending as https-only. The browser GitHub installs, the one this project's version of Playwright asks for, applies that to an http address too and asks for https instead. The test copy of the app (port 4174) only spoke http, so it couldn't answer, and the page never opened. The building computer can't download that browser and uses an older one already there, which let http through, so the tests passed there. The app and the live site weren't affected: the live site is https already. Only the test copy was.
- **Fix.** The test copy now runs over https (`wrangler dev --local-protocol https`, with Wrangler's own certificate for this computer), and the tests open https addresses, as Cloudflare serves them. The test browser accepts that one local certificate (`ignoreHTTPSErrors`, `--ignore-certificate-errors`), and starts without the computer's proxy settings, so it only ever talks to this computer. What each test checks is unchanged: none is skipped, loosened or retried. With the move turned off on purpose, 3 tests fail, so they still catch a broken move.
- **What that run showed next.** With the https fix, two of the three tests passed on GitHub; "a page opened at the old address goes on to the same page on prepenny.co.uk" still failed at both widths: it expected the move to `https://prepenny.co.uk/app/coming-up/?from=home` and saw a request for `https://prepenny.co.uk/sign-in/?to=sign-up&from=join`. Why: a browser can't be stopped part-way through a move the server sends, so that test relies on prepenny.co.uk being out of reach and only checks where the browser was sent. On the building computer it is out of reach; GitHub's computers can reach the real live site. So the browser opened live prepenny.co.uk's join page, which, for someone signed out, opens "sign in" by itself, and the test's next step took that request for the move it was waiting for. Reproduced on the building computer with a copy of the app standing in for prepenny.co.uk: the same failure. Fix: the test browser is told prepenny.co.uk doesn't exist (`--host-resolver-rules … MAP prepenny.co.uk ~NOTFOUND`), so no test ever opens the live site, from any computer. The checks are unchanged, and with the move turned off on purpose they still fail.
- **The bank holidays only after "Allow".** The owner's instruction: someone with a plan of their own was told the inviter's bank holidays (England and Wales, Scotland or Northern Ireland) on the join page before being let in, so a stranger with a forwarded link learned something about the plan before the owner tapped Allow. Now the server sends the bank holidays of both plans only once the invite is allowed, and only to the person who asked (`app.invite_look`: `nation` and `own_nation` only in state `allowed`; `brings` still before, as it's about the person's own plan). Before "Allow" the join page says only that their plan comes with them, private to them. Once allowed, with different bank holidays: "Jess let you in.", the bank holidays line and **Bring my plan and join**; with the same ones, the plan comes straight away, as before. The phone also ignores bank holidays for an invite not yet allowed (`lookOf`). Bringing a plan is refused for an invite not yet allowed before its bank holidays are checked, so "nation-changed" can't be used to guess them either.

Test: `25-bring-your-plan.test.sql` (62 checks, 3 new: Mo, a stranger with a forwarded link and a plan of her own, told nothing about the plan before or after "Allow"; Jess told nothing while she waits; and told both plans' bank holidays only once allowed), `23-invite-and-join.test.sql` (Priya's look, now without them), `e2e/sharing.spec.ts` test 4 (no bank holidays on the page or in the server's answer before asking or while waiting; the line with **Bring my plan and join** once allowed), `test/unit/sharing.test.ts` (3 new). Put back on the old server for a moment, 4 database checks and test 4 failed.

Why: the owner's instruction, and the root cause in GitHub's logs.

### D440. Sam's example says "Example plan, as on Sun 25 Oct 2026"

The owner's decision 1, 12 Nov 2026. Replaces, in Sam's example only, the line under Home's figure from D430. D133 stays as it is: the example still always shows Sun 25 Oct 2026 as today.

- In Sam's example, "Checked today, Sun 25 Oct" becomes "Example plan, as on Sun 25 Oct 2026".
- Decided in the polish step:
  - **The year always shows**, as the owner wrote it, because the example's day stays the same whatever the real date is.

## Made during step 3g

### D455. The history: every record a change saves, before and after

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3g. Builds D50, D87 and ARCHITECTURE.md 3.5; replaces D331's "It keeps no before-copies or summary yet".

- Every record a change saves is kept as it was before the change (nothing, when the change made it) and after it, with who could read each copy at that moment: shared, or private to one person. The database keeps them itself, as each record is saved, for every change through `save_edit` and through its own steps (sharing an account, claiming pay, making something private). The phone sends nothing extra, so nothing can be left out or made up.
- They're in the database's own part (`app.edit_records`), which the Data API never shows. Only the database's own steps read them, each for the person asking.
- A plan's first copy, and a plan brought into another (D426), save the whole plan at once and change nothing anyone made: they keep no history, so Activity doesn't list every record of a plan as "added".
- Like the changes themselves, they go with the plan when it's removed. Changes older than 12 months aren't shown (D50); clearing them out of the database waits for a scheduled job (PLAN.md item 61).

Test: `supabase/tests/database/26-living-together.test.sql` ("The history keeps each record an edit saves", "with how it was before", "and how it was after").

Why: Activity needs what each change did (brief 4.12), and undo for older changes needs the "before" copies (D87), on any of the person's phones.

### D456. Activity: who changed what, and when

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3g. Builds brief 4.12 and 4.14.

- Under More, after Share with my partner: **Activity**, "Every change, and undo for your own". Share with my partner links to it too.
- It starts with "Every change to your plan in the last 12 months, newest first. You can undo your own." or, in a shared plan, "Every change you and Tom made in the last 12 months, newest first. You can undo your own changes, but not Tom's." Since D469, without "in the last 12 months", and each change says from what to what (D474).
- Changes are in cards by day ("Today", "Yesterday", "Tue 10 Nov"), newest first, 50 at a time with **Show older changes**. Each is one sentence with who made it and what it did, and "At 2:05pm" under it: "Tom changed Rent on Sun 1 Nov to £560.00.", "You marked Pay on Fri 27 Nov as arrived.", "Jess added Car MOT, −£54.85 on Fri 13 Nov.", "You updated the Joint balance to £1,234.56, and ticked 3 payments.", "Tom spent £12.50 on Food.", "Jess shared Current account.", "Jess changed what you can do to Can view only.", "You undid a change to Rent." Every kind of change has its words (`lib/activity-words.ts`); anything else says which records it changed ("Tom changed Rent and Gym.") or "Tom made a change." Someone with no name yet is "Your partner" (D377).
- A change undone says **Undone** beside its time.
- The words are put together on the phone from what the server gives it (D458), so they can name nothing private, and none of them is in a log.
- Activity is the account's history, so it needs signing in and a signal: "You're offline. Connect to the internet to see Activity." Signed out: "Activity shows the changes saved in your account. Sign in first, so your plan is kept there." The example plan has none.
- A change from the other phone appears as soon as the plan comes down, with the screen left open.

Test: `apps/web/test/unit/living-together.test.ts` (Activity's words), `apps/web/e2e/together.spec.ts` test 1. Screenshots: `docs/screens/3g/activity-tom-360-light.png` and `activity-jess-360-light.png`.

Why: the owner's instruction: "who changed what and when, in plain words".

### D457. Undo for your own changes only

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3g. Builds D87 and D186 ("Older changes will be in Activity").

- Each of the person's own changes has **Undo this change** while nothing it changed has been changed again since, by anyone. Their partner's never do.
- Undo is the server's own step (`undo_change`): it puts every record the change saved back as it was before, as a new change of the person's, through `save_edit`, so every privacy rule and role applies as to any change (D51: "Can view only" can't undo a shared change). Undoing an undo puts the change back (D87).
- The server checks whose change it is: anyone else's, private or shared, is refused exactly as a change that isn't there ("not-found"), so nobody can even learn a change exists. A change someone has changed since is refused ("changed-since"), so a later change is never lost: "This can't be undone, because something it changed has been changed again since." (D186's words).
- Not undone from Activity: a plan's first copy, joining with a plan, sharing an account, claiming pay, making something private or shared, and changing a role. Each has its own way back.
- It needs a signal and nothing waiting to be sent, as sharing an account does (D413): "Some changes on this phone haven't been sent to your account yet. Try again once they have." The undo bar still undoes the last change on the phone, with or without a signal (D186).

Test: `26-living-together.test.sql` ("Nobody can undo someone else's change", 13 checks, and "Undoing one's own change", 13), `apps/web/e2e/together.spec.ts` tests 1 and 4.

Why: brief 4.14's "with undo for your own changes", and the owner's security test "nobody can undo someone else's change".

### D458. Nothing private in a partner's Activity

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3g. Builds D16, D291, D299, D414.

- Each person sees their own changes, private ones included, and only the other person's shared changes, as before (D296, D331).
- Of each change, they get only the copies of records they could read when it was made **and** can still read now. So a change Jess made to an account before she shared it never shows on Tom's phone, even once the account is shared; sharing it shows only how each record is now, never how it was while private; and once an account, debt or goal is made private again, nothing of it stays in Tom's Activity, even changes from while it was shared. A change with nothing left that Tom may see isn't shown at all.
- One exception, so nothing is taken away unseen (brief 4.14's safety): "Jess made a shared account private, so you no longer see it." It names nothing: not the account, nor anything in it.
- A partner's own records stay theirs to see: a payment Tom added to Jess's account and took out again before she made it private stays in his Activity as his.

Test: `26-living-together.test.sql` ("Activity never shows a partner anything private", 16 checks), `apps/web/e2e/together.spec.ts` tests 1 and 4. Each rule was broken on purpose to see the checks fail: a copy shown whoever could read it then (1 failed), whoever can read it now (3 failed).

Why: the owner's rule: "Activity never shows a partner anything private, including from before an account was shared".

### D459. "Changed by Tom, 2 hours ago"

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3g. Builds brief 4.14 and D420.

- In a shared plan, a payment's row says "Changed by Tom, 2 hours ago" under its date when the other person changed it last, in the last 7 days. The edit sheet always says who changed it last: "Changed by Tom, just now", "Changed by you, yesterday", "Added by Jess, Tue 3 Nov". A plan with one person in it shows nothing.
- "Last" is the newest change to the payment's series, its one-off change or its tick, by the server's record of who made each change and when. A series that "this and all later ones" only gave a new end hasn't changed its earlier payments, so they don't say so.
- Sharing an account saves everything on it again, but changes no payment: it shows as "Shared by Jess" in the sheet and nothing on the rows. Making something private or shared, and joining, show nothing.
- When: "just now", "5 minutes ago", "2 hours ago", "yesterday", or the date, by the phone's clock corrected against the server's.
- The rows show only the other person's recent changes so the plan stays calm (DESIGN.md) and their change stands out; the sheet says it whenever someone asks.

Test: `living-together.test.ts`, `apps/web/e2e/together.spec.ts` test 1. Screenshots: `docs/screens/3g/changed-by-on-home-360-light.png`, `changed-by-in-the-sheet-360-light.png`.

Why: brief 4.14 ("Each payment shows who added or last changed it") and 8.7 test 1.

### D461. Changing a partner's role

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3g. Builds brief 4.14, D51, D292.

- On Share with my partner, the plan's owner has **Change what Tom can do** under Tom's name. A sheet: "Tom's phone follows the change straight away. Either of you can still keep your own pay up to date.", "What can Tom do?" with **Can edit** and **Can view only** (with the invite's own lines under each), and **Save**. Then "Tom can view only now." or "Tom can edit now."
- Only the owner (D412, D434), never their own role, and only "Can edit" or "Can view only". Through the server's own step, `change_role`.
- It takes effect straight away on the server: every privacy rule reads the role on every request (`app.my_edit_plans`), so the next change Tom's phone sends is refused, however long ago he made it, with "You can look at this plan but not change it." (D369).
- Tom's phone hears at once (a nudge) and shows under "Who's in this plan": "You can see everything shared, but change nothing. Only Jess can change that." or "You can see and change everything shared."
- It's in Activity for both: "Jess changed what you can do to Can view only."

Test: `26-living-together.test.sql` ("Changing a role takes effect straight away on the server", 11 checks), `apps/web/e2e/together.spec.ts` test 3. Screenshot: `docs/screens/3g/change-what-tom-can-do-360-light.png`.

Why: brief 4.14 ("Either can be changed later") and 8.7 test 3, which step 3f left for now (D425).

### D462. Making a shared account private again

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3g. Replaces D413's "A shared account can't be made private again".

- On Choose shared accounts, the shared ones are in a card of their own, "Shared accounts", "Tom sees these, and everything in them." Each the person shared has **Make Joint private again**; one the other person shared says "Only Jess can make this private again, as they shared it." A sheet asks first: "Make Joint private?", "Tom will no longer see Joint or anything in it, and its changes leave Tom's Activity. You can share it again later.", **Yes, make it private** and **No, keep it shared**. Then "Joint is private now. Only you see it."
- **Who:** only the person who shared it (who added it), whatever their role: it was theirs to share and is theirs to take back. Nobody can take a joint account away from the other person this way.
- **Not while the other person has things of their own in it:** payments or budgets they added, their pay, or money kept as theirs ("From Tom"): "Tom has payments of their own in Joint: Gym. While they're there, it stays shared. Tom can remove them or move them to an account of their own." Those are Tom's to see, so it stays shared. A goal of Tom's own kept in it isn't named: "Tom has things of their own in Joint, so it stays shared for now."
- **Also refused, in plain words:** a shared savings goal kept in it ("Make the goal private first"); money moving between it and another shared account; money from your own account kept as "From Jess" in it (as D413).
- **What happens:** the account and everything on it become private to its person in one step of the server's own (`make_private`), or nothing does. Ticks, one-off changes and spends Tom made on her payments go with them. What she removed from it before goes private too (D467); only what Tom added and removed himself stays his to see. Links kept apart (D291) go back on the payments, exactly as the phone keeps them.
- Tom's phone removes the account within seconds, and his Activity keeps only "Jess made a shared account private, so you no longer see it." (D458).

Test: `26-living-together.test.sql` ("Making a shared account private again", 11 checks; breaking "only the person who shared it" fails 2), `apps/web/e2e/together.spec.ts` test 4. Screenshot: `docs/screens/3g/make-private-360-light.png`.

Why: the owner's instruction, and brief 4.14's safety: nobody is cut off from their own things.

### D463. Each debt and goal: shared, or one person's

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3g. Builds D65, brief 4.14.

- In a shared plan, the Debts list says under each debt "Shared with Tom" or "Only you see this". Its page has **Who sees it**, with **Share this debt with Tom**, or **Make it private** for one the person shared, or "Shared by Jess. Only Jess can make it private." Each goal on Savings goals says the same, with **Share this goal with Tom**.
- A new debt is the person's own until they share it (D65). A new goal kept in a shared account is shared, as before; one kept in a private account is private and says "It's kept in Current account, which only you see, so it stays private."
- Sharing asks first: "Share Car loan with Tom?", "Tom will see Car loan, what's left on it and when it's paid off. You can make it private again later." (for a goal: "…what's saved and when it's reached, and can put money into it."), **Yes, share it**, **No, keep it private**. Making it private: "Tom will no longer see Car loan or what's left on it. Payments to it from shared accounts still show, without the debt."
- Both are the server's own steps (`share_record`, `make_private`), on what the account has now. Sharing needs "Can edit", as it makes something shared. Making private is for whoever shared it, and refused while the other person puts money towards it ("Tom puts money towards Car loan, so it stays shared."). Links on shared payments move into or out of the person's private links (D291), exactly as the phone keeps them.

Why: the owner's instruction (D65).

The shared half still carries nothing else: no note, debt, job or money set aside, and never a goal of hers alone ("link-to-private").

## From the outside review of step 3g

### D467. What the outside review of step 3g changed

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3g, from an outside review of its database changes, which tried each hole on the local database. Adds to D455 to D465.

- **A phone could send a change as one of the server's own kinds**, such as "first-copy", which keeps no history: Tom's change to Rent then showed nowhere in Activity, and not as "Changed by Tom". Now `save_edit` refuses the kinds only the server's own steps make (joining with a plan, sharing an account, claiming pay, making something private or shared, changing a role), and a first copy while anyone else is in the plan ("bad-edit").
- **Making a debt or goal private could take over the other person's own link.** A link kept apart (D291) has an id worked out from the plan and the payment's family, not the person (`lib/sync-ids.ts`, D413), so Tom's own link from shared Rent to his own loan has the same id Jess's link to her card would. Making her card private then rewrote Tom's link, or was refused with "not-found". Now it's refused plainly while the other person's link holds that id ("partner-things", naming nothing), and never writes a link that isn't the person's own. Putting the person into those ids is PLAN.md item 64.
- **Putting links back could write a payment private to the other person**, through a stale link of one's own. Now it writes only shared payments and one's own, and making an account private is refused while the other person has a private link of their own on a payment in it ("partner-things", naming nothing), so no stale link is left behind.
- **What Jess removed from an account stayed shared once she made it private**, so its name and amount still reached Tom, in Activity too, against D458. Now her removed records go private with the account (D462). A removed transfer to another shared account, or a removed "From Jess", stays as it was, since it's about an account that's still shared.
- **Two at once:** making something private now holds it for the whole step, so nothing the other person adds at that moment can be missed by its checks and made theirs.
- **A time that isn't one** is refused with "bad-edit" in Activity and the server's own steps, never the database's own message.
- **Accepted:** a change already on its way when a role changes is saved, as it was made before; the very next one is refused (D461). A refusal with nothing named still tells a person their partner has something of their own there, as D462 says on purpose.

Test: `supabase/tests/database/27-living-together-review.test.sql` (20 checks). With the code from before the review put back on the local database for a moment, 9 of them failed, and the plan count with them.

Why: each was shown by the review with a case that went wrong.

## The owner's answers to step 3g

### D468. Leaving a shared plan: your own goes with you, and the shared plan stays

Decided by the owner 12 Nov 2026, answering step 3g's question (D454): option A. Builds D53 and brief 4.14, 8.7 test 5.

- When Tom leaves, everything only Tom sees (his own accounts with everything on them, his own debts and goals, his job details) goes with him into a new plan of his own, with its history. Jess never saw them and still doesn't.
- Every shared account stays in the shared plan with Jess, as it is. Payments Tom added in them stay and become Jess's to change. "From Tom" stays as money arriving, no longer locked to him, and his pay into a shared account stays as an amount Jess can change.
- Tom chooses whether to take a **copy** of the shared accounts. The copy is his alone and never updates. Without it, only his own things go with him.
- Jess's Activity keeps the shared changes with Tom's name on them. His private history goes with him.
- Nobody has to agree. From that moment Tom gets nothing more from the plan, and nothing private of his stays in it.
- If the owner leaves, the person who stays becomes the owner (D53).
- The owner asked to see every leaving screen's words: they're in the step's report and `apps/web/strings/en-GB.ts` (`together.leave`).

Why: the owner's choice. It's what brief 4.14 and 8.7 test 5 describe ("The other person keeps the shared plan", "Jess's plan is unchanged").

### D469. A lasting record: History on every payment, kept for as long as the plan exists

Decided by the owner 12 Nov 2026, answering step 3g's question. **Replaces D50's 12 months.**

- Each payment's edit sheet has a **History** list: every change to that payment, who made it, when, and from what to what, for example "Tom changed it from £550.00 to £560.00, Tue 10 Nov, 2:05pm".
- **Activity** says from what to what too: "Tom changed Rent on Tue 1 Dec from £550.00 to £560.00."
- History is kept for **as long as the plan exists**, not 12 months. Activity shows it all, 50 changes at a time, with "Show older changes".
- The same privacy rule as Activity (D458): nobody ever sees history of anything they couldn't see at the time and can't see now.
- What happens to someone's history when they delete their account is for step 3h (PLAN.md).

Why: "a lasting record, in case of arguments", in the owner's words.

### D470. "Can view only" can always make their own shared account private again

Decided by the owner 12 Nov 2026, answering step 3g's question. Confirms D462 as built.

Whoever shared an account can make it private again, whatever their role.

Why: the owner can't keep someone's own account by changing their role.

### D471. "Changed by Tom" on the rows for 7 days

Decided by the owner 12 Nov 2026, answering step 3g's question. Confirms D459 as built.

A partner's change shows on the payment's row for 7 days, and always in its edit sheet.

Why: the owner's choice.

## Built with the owner's answers to step 3g

### D472. A goal of your own that saves into a shared account brings a copy of that account

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3g. Adds to D468.

- A savings goal only Tom sees can be kept in a shared account (D463). When Tom leaves without a copy, the goal would have no account to be kept in. So a copy of just that account comes with it either way: the account and its balance, without its payments.
- The leaving screen says so: "Ring saves into Joint, so a copy of Joint comes with you either way."

Why: otherwise leaving without a copy would lose a goal of Tom's own, which option A says goes with him. A copy of the account is the least that keeps it. The owner may prefer another way; this is easy to change.

### D473. How leaving is built

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3g. Builds D468.

- **Where:** Share with my partner has a **Leave this plan** card for anyone in a plan with someone else in it, whatever they can do. It opens a screen saying what comes with them and what stays, asks "Take a copy of the shared accounts?" with no answer chosen for them, and a sheet asks first: "Leave this plan?". If the owner is leaving, it says the other person will own the plan.
- **One step on the server** (`leave_plan`): all of it, or nothing. It's refused for someone not in the plan ("not-found") and for someone alone in it ("alone"). Sent twice, it changes nothing the second time.
- **The new plan** has the id made on the phone, as every plan's (D324), and the same bank holidays as the shared plan. Tom's own records keep their ids. Copies get new ids worked out from the new plan, and everything in them is private to Tom. His own links from shared payments to his own debts, goals and job (D291) go back on the copies. Removed payments come with a copy only where their payment's family still has a live one. If the copy can't be made, he leaves all the same with his own things, and Home says "The copy of the shared accounts couldn't be made, so it didn't come with you."
- **The shared plan:** "From Tom" and his pay into a shared account are no longer locked to him, as they are. Overtime on his pay there stays as an amount; its details (hours, rate) are his and go. Every record private to Tom goes, with his notices. Changes only Tom could see go with him. In shared changes that stay, every copy of something private to him, and every copy at all of a record that went, is removed or emptied, so Jess keeps nothing of his. If Tom owned the plan, the other person owns it now, and any invite still open is cancelled.
- **Names:** Activity and History keep the name of everyone who has been in the plan, so "Tom left the plan." and his older changes still say "Tom".
- **His phones:** they find the plan they had is no longer in the account and bring down his new one in its place, as for any plan no longer in the account. Home then says "You've left the plan." with what came, until he taps "Got it". His Activity starts with "You left the shared plan, and took a copy of the shared accounts."
- **Joining again later** works: his settings row in the shared plan, whose id comes from the plan and the person, is gone (PLAN.md item 60).

Test: `supabase/tests/database/28-leaving.test.sql` (63 checks): the owner's two security tests (after leaving, Tom can read, bring down, save, hear and see nothing more of the plan, by every way the Data API offers; Jess's plan, its changes and its history name nothing of Tom's own), the shared accounts unchanged, what goes with him and its copy, "Can view only" leaving, nobody leaving a plan they're alone in, the owner leaving, and D472. Breaking four of its rules on purpose, one at a time, fails the checks each time. `apps/web/e2e/together.spec.ts` test 5. Screenshots: `docs/screens/3g/leave-360-light.png`, `leave-confirm-360-light.png`, `left-360-light.png`, `activity-tom-left-360-light.png`.

Why: D468, and brief 8.7 test 5.

### D474. How History and "from what to what" are built

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3g. Builds D469.

- **History** is at the foot of a payment's edit sheet, with "Show its history", for a plan kept in an account: it's the account's history, so it isn't looked up until asked for. Each line: who, what, the day and the time, newest first, and "Undone since" on a change undone later.
- The server's own step `payment_history` gives the changes to the payment's series and to its own one-off change and tick, with Activity's rule (D458). The phone keeps a change to the series only when it changed this payment: the engine works the payment out from the series as it was before and after (brief 5.3). So "this and all later ones" from a later date, or sharing the account, isn't in an earlier payment's History.
- **"From" amounts:** for a one-off change, its earlier one-off amount, or else what the payment's series gave for it then. That's worked out by the engine from a copy of the series kept with each one-off change and tick when it's saved (new in this step). Overtime and missed shifts aren't in it: they're changes of their own.
- **Activity** says from what to what for one-off changes, "this and all later ones" ("Tom changed Rent from £550.00 to £560.00, starting Tue 1 Dec."), changes to a spend and to a budget's amount. Where nothing was there before, it says what it is now, as before.
- **Space:** measured on the test database, a change takes about 2.5 KB in all: the records it saved, before and after (1.7 a change on average, about 1 to 1.5 KB each with the database's own lists), the change itself and the phone it came from. A plan used every day by two people makes about 3,000 to 6,000 changes a year (8 to 16 a day: ticks, balance updates, spends, changes): roughly 5 to 15 MB of history a year. Supabase's free plan has 500 MB of database in all, so it holds a year of history for about 30 to 100 such plans; the paid plan has 8 GB.

Test: `supabase/tests/database/29-payment-history.test.sql` (12 checks: what Tom sees of a payment shared later, nothing of a payment only Jess sees or one made private again, each payment's own changes only, and a change more than 12 months old). Breaking its privacy checks or its filter fails them. `apps/web/test/unit/lasting-history.test.ts` (12 tests). `apps/web/e2e/together.spec.ts` test 5. Screenshot: `docs/screens/3g/history-360-light.png`.

Why: D469.
