## Made during step 3h

### D476. Download my data: two files, made on the phone

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3h. Builds brief 4.12, 4.13 ("download my data") and 7.2 ("a working data export").

- Under More, a **Your data** card: "Keep a copy of your plan, bring one back, or delete everything.", with **Download my data**, **Import a backup** and **Delete my account and all my data**.
- **Download my data** has two buttons: **Download the spreadsheet** (CSV) and **Download the backup** (JSON). Both files are made on the phone and saved straight to its downloads; nothing is sent anywhere. The screen says what's in them and "The files aren't locked. Keep them somewhere only you can open, as you would a bank statement."
- **What's in them**: the plan as the phone has it, which only ever holds what the person may see, so never anything only their partner sees (the database's tests since step 3a prove the server never sends it); for someone signed in, their account from the server's own step for them alone (`my_account_data`: the address they sign in with, when they signed up, the name their partner sees, their backup email, whether two-step sign-in is on and Google signs them in, their signed-in devices by plain name, and the plans they've been in, with roles and dates); who's in the plan, by name; and the plan's history in Activity's own words, with Activity's own rule (D458). Signed out, the plan only. If the account or its history can't be fetched, the file has the plan and the screen says so.
- **The spreadsheet** is one CSV file with a part for each kind of thing under its own heading: Your account, Signed-in devices, Who's in the plan, Accounts, Coming up in the next 12 months (every payment with Left after this, as the plan works it out), Payments, Changes to one payment, Ticked payments, Balance updates, Debts, Budgets, What you spent, Savings goals, Your job, Overtime and missed shifts, History. Decided in step 3h: as it's for a spreadsheet, amounts are plain numbers in pounds ("-30.00") and dates "2026-11-12", which a spreadsheet can sort and add up, and times are the UK's clock ("2026-11-12 14:05"); the words people read (headings and columns) follow VOICE.md. Anything a person typed that a spreadsheet could take for a formula starts with an apostrophe, so opening the file can't run it. It starts with a mark saying it's UTF-8, so "£" shows as it is.
- **The backup** is JSON a person can read: `"app": "PrePenny"`, `"kind": "backup"`, `"version": 1`, a sentence saying what it is and how to use it, when it was made, the plan (its bank holidays and the engine's own records, D87), and the account, people and history as above. Who a debt or goal belongs to is left out: it names a person by id, and on import the database decides it.
- File names: `prepenny-2026-11-12.csv` and `prepenny-backup-2026-11-12.json`.
- Sam's example isn't the person's own, so with no plan of their own: "You haven't made a plan of your own yet, so there's nothing to download."

Test: `apps/web/test/unit/backup-csv.test.ts` (7), `supabase/tests/database/30-download-import-delete.test.sql` (part 1), `apps/web/e2e/your-data.spec.ts` test 1. Screenshot: `docs/screens/3h/download-360-light.png`.

Why: the owner's instruction: "everything the person can see, and never a partner's private records".

### D477. Import a backup: checked before it's trusted, and only ever into a plan of one's own

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3h. Builds brief 4.12 ("import a JSON backup").

- **The checks**, on the phone, before anything is saved (`lib/backup.ts`), each refusing in plain words with nothing changed:
  - the file's size, at most 20 MB, before it's read;
  - that it's JSON, and says it's a PrePenny backup of a version this app reads ("This backup is from a newer version of PrePenny. Update PrePenny, then try again.");
  - **every record and every field**, with Zod (ARCHITECTURE.md 1.1), against the engine's own types and the database's limits: no field left over; kinds from their fixed lists; dates that exist; times in UTC; amounts in whole pence, exact (`Number.isSafeInteger`), never below zero where the database says so; decimals as text; names up to 500 characters and notes up to 5,000, with no hidden control characters; ids of up to 100 letters, numbers, dashes and underscores;
  - how many of each kind: at most 50 accounts, 5,000 payments, 20,000 one-off changes, 50,000 ticks and so on, far more than any real plan;
  - every link between records, which must name a record in the same file, with no id used twice and no payment ticked twice;
  - then the engine works the plan, its debts and its goals out, as Home, Debts and Savings goals do, and refuses anything it can't use.
- **Nothing is taken on trust**: every id is made new on the phone, so nothing in the file can name or replace a record anywhere; who a record belongs to and who may see it are never read (the database decides each record's privacy as it saves it, as for a plan made on the phone, and refuses a record private to or owned by anyone else); a "From Jess" half comes in as two payments of the importer's own (D426, D437). A file only ever changes the person's own plan, and only after they say yes: "Import this backup?", "It was made on Thu 12 Nov. It has 1 account, 18 payments, 6 debts and no savings goals. Your plan now will be replaced. It can't be brought back.", **Yes, replace my plan** and **No, keep my plan**.
- **What happens** (decided in step 3h):
  - With a plan of one's own, signed in: the backup's plan takes its place, in the account and on the phone. The server swaps the plans in one step (`replace_plan`: the old plan goes and an empty new one starts, or neither), then the phone sends the backup up as the new plan's first copy, through save_edit, so every privacy rule and check applies to each record; a first copy cut off part-way is sent again from where it stopped (step 3c). The old plan's Activity goes with it, and the new plan's starts again, so the screen says "Its Activity starts again." and "Download your data first if you want to keep your plan as it is now."
  - Signed out: the backup's plan takes the place of the plan on this phone.
  - With no plan of one's own yet: it becomes their plan, finished setup and all.
  - **In a shared plan: never.** "You share a plan with Tom, so a backup can't go in. A backup only ever goes into a plan of your own, so nothing in it can reach Tom. To use it, leave the shared plan first." The server refuses it too ("shared-plan"), for the owner and the partner alike, and refuses a first copy into a plan anyone else is in (D467). Putting a backup's accounts into a shared plan as the person's own private accounts would be possible later, if the owner wants it.
- After importing, the page loads afresh: "Your backup is in. Your plan is now the one from the backup." with **Go to Home**.

Test: `apps/web/test/unit/backup.test.ts` (35: every one of the seven test people's plans comes back with every account's balance at the end of every day for a year the same, every id new, and each kind of broken file refused), `30-download-import-delete.test.sql` (part 2: nobody imports into someone else's plan or a shared one, and a first copy can't make a record private to or owned by anyone else, take another record's id or name a record in another plan; what a record says about its plan, its maker, its version and whether it's removed is the database's), `apps/web/e2e/your-data.spec.ts` tests 2 and 3. Screenshots: `docs/screens/3h/import-360-light.png`, `import-broken-file-360-light.png`.

Why: the owner's instruction: "checked before it's trusted: size limits, every field checked, nothing taken on trust from the file (ids, owners, privacy)", and "say what happens if the person already has a plan or is in a shared one".

### D478. Delete my account and all my data

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3h. Builds brief 4.12, 4.13 and 7.2; changes D17's "account deletion runs in Edge Functions".

- **The screen** says what's deleted ("Your plan, with every account, payment, debt, budget and savings goal, and its history." and "Your signed-in devices, your backup email, your two-step sign-in and your sign-in itself."), "It can't be undone. Download your data first if you want a copy.", and when it's gone (D479). Then **Check it's you**: **Send me a code** emails a 6-digit code to the address the person signs in with; they type it, the authenticator app's code when two-step sign-in is on, and **Type delete to confirm**, then tap **Delete my account and all my data**, or **Keep my account**.
- **The checks, on the server** (`delete_my_account`), each refusing with nothing changed: a sign-in session made in the last 10 minutes on this device ("sign-in-again": the code signs the phone in again, and the sign-in it replaces is ended so it doesn't stay in Signed-in devices); with two-step sign-in on, the app's code in the last 5 minutes (as for signing other devices out, D382); "delete" typed, in any case and with spaces around it; and nobody who is or has been in a shared plan, until the owner chooses (D475). It names nobody: it only ever deletes the person asking.
- **What goes, in one step of the database's own, or nothing** (`app.delete_person`): their plan with every record, change and copy in its history, members, invites, notices and counts; their name and address on invites to someone else's plan they asked to join (no longer waiting); their codes, wrong tries and locks, by address too; their devices, backup email and getting-back-in requests; the nudges sent to their phones; Supabase's own log of their sign-ins where it's kept in the database; and their sign-in, which takes their identities (Google included), sessions, refresh tokens, authenticator app and one-time codes with it. Decided in step 3h: in the database rather than through Supabase's admin route from an Edge Function, so it's all or nothing, needs no key and nothing new to set up; and the admin route writes the deleted person's address into Supabase's own log.
- **Kept**: a scrambled id and the time, for 30 days, so if a backup from before is ever put back, the deletion can be made again (`app.delete_again_after_restore()`, from the SQL Editor; README.md part K). It's scrambled with the database's own secret, so it can't be checked against anyone without it, and holds nothing else.
- **The email** "Your PrePenny account has been deleted": "As you asked, at 2:05pm on Thu 12 Nov we deleted your account and everything in it: your plan and its history, your signed-in devices, your backup email and your sign-in.", "Nothing of it is left in PrePenny. Copies in PrePenny's backups, and in the records of the services that run it, are gone within 30 days.", "You can make a new account with this email address at any time.", and "If you didn't ask for this, write to hello@prepenny.co.uk." No amount, payment or note. With emails switched off, the account is deleted all the same.
- **The phone** forgets everything, a change not sent included, and opens the welcome: "Your account and all your data have been deleted, and nothing of yours is left on this phone. An email is on its way to say so."
- **Their other phones get nothing more**: every rule needs the sign-in session, which went with the account, so every request is refused and the live channel names a plan that's gone. While their sign-in token lasts (up to an hour), `hello` says "deleted", so each removes everything at its next look, within a minute, a change not sent included, with "This account has been deleted, so nothing of it is left on this phone." After that, Supabase's client can't renew the sign-in, and the phone does what it does for a sign-in that ended (D326).
- **Not signed in**: the plan is only on the phone, so the screen says "You're not signed in, so your plan is kept only on this phone." with **Remove everything from this phone** (a sheet asks first), then "Everything PrePenny kept on this phone has been removed.", and "Have an account? Sign in first to delete it."

Test: `30-download-import-delete.test.sql` (parts 3 to 6, 66 checks in all with D481's: every refusal, nothing anywhere in the database naming the person's id, address or backup address afterwards, in any table of any part, the email queued with no amount in it, Jess's plan untouched, his phones' token reading, saving, starting, hearing and downloading nothing, and the deletion made again after a backup is put back), `apps/web/e2e/your-data.spec.ts` tests 4 to 6. Each new rule was broken on purpose to see the checks fail: without the 10-minute check, the sign-in-again check failed and the run stopped; without the log and address clean-up, 3 failed; without the shared-plan refusal, 6 failed. Screenshots: `docs/screens/3h/delete-360-light.png`, `delete-check-360-light.png`, `deleted-360-light.png`.

Why: the owner's instruction: "really deletes it: their records, history, devices, backup email, sign-in and everything else", "a fresh sign-in, and the two-step code when that's on", "the person types a confirmation, and an email confirms it's done", and "a deleted person's devices get nothing more".

### D479. When deleted data is gone from backups and logs

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3h, from Supabase's own documentation and pricing pages (read from Supabase's public repository, as supabase.com couldn't be opened from the computer this was built on) and Resend's pricing page as search engines quote it.

- **PrePenny's database**: everything goes at once (D478).
- **Supabase's backups**: the free plan, which PrePenny is on now, takes **none**. The paid plan, before launch (launch checklist, item 3), takes one a day and keeps the last **7 days**; none can be deleted early, so a deleted account is in the backups for up to 7 days after, then gone. Point-in-Time Recovery is an add-on keeping 7, 14 or 28 days of changes; PrePenny doesn't plan to use it, and if it ever did, these words would need checking.
- **Supabase's logs** of requests and sign-ins (which can hold an email address, a device and an IP address, never an amount, name or note): 1 day on the free plan, 7 on the paid plan.
- **Resend**, which sends PrePenny's emails, keeps each one it sent, the deletion email included, for **30 days** on its free and paid plans. Not confirmed on Resend's own site, which couldn't be opened from here: worth checking at resend.com/pricing.
- So the screen and the email say: "It's deleted from PrePenny straight away. Copies in PrePenny's backups, and in the records of the services that run it, are gone within 30 days.", inside brief 7.2's 30 days. The privacy notice (Phase 5) should say the same, service by service.

Why: the owner's instruction: "Find out how long Supabase keeps backups on the free and paid plans, and say in plain words when the data is gone from those too."

Download, Import a backup and Delete my account join the every-screen checks (`checks.spec.ts`: axe, keyboard, 200% text, reduced motion, offline and nothing sent), as the example plan sees them: 12 runs, all passing.

After the outside review's database changes (D481), on a fresh database: 1,088 database checks pass, and the new tests, your plan, sharing, living together and bringing your plan, at both widths: 110 passed, 4 skipped by design, and `your-plan.spec.ts` test 9 failed twice three at a time (PLAN.md item 65), then passed 4 runs in 4 on its own.

## From the outside review of step 3h

### D481. What the outside review of step 3h changed

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3h, from an outside review of its database steps and the import's checks, which tried each hole on the local database and forged files against `readBackup`. It found no way for anyone to download, import into or delete someone else's account, or to make an imported record someone else's. Adds to D477 and D478.

- **Deleting, or importing, at the very moment a partner joins** could have removed the joiner's newly brought plan: the check of who's in the plan came before the plan was held. Now deleting holds the person's plans, and removing a plan (for an import, or "Keep the plan on this phone") holds that plan, before checking who's in it, so a join under way finishes first and is then seen, and the step is refused. Worked out from the code; a test needs two sessions at once, which the database tests can't do. Accepted: a join that starts at the very moment its plan is being deleted fails with the database's own error, and nothing changes.
- **"Has shared a plan" could be got round**: once Tom had left, Jess could import a backup in place of the plan he'd been in, which took away the only sign that she'd shared it, and then delete her account. Now the database keeps a mark for everyone who has ever been in a plan with someone else (`app.shared_once`, filled in as anyone joins, and for everyone already so), which removing a plan doesn't take away. **Replaced by D482**: nobody is refused for having shared a plan any more, so the mark is gone.
- **Nudges for a person's plan** (live updates naming the plan, not the person) stayed after deleting. Now deleting removes the nudges for the person and their plans, and any for a plan that's no longer there, such as one a backup replaced.
- **Supabase's own sign-in log in the database** (`auth.audit_log_entries`) has no index the database may add, so deleting reads all of it: about 2.3 seconds for a million rows, against the 8 seconds a request may take. Supabase adds a row at every sign-in and every hourly renewal, so with many people it could grow past what deleting can read. Supabase lets the database copy be switched off (Authentication, Audit Logs, "Write audit logs to the database"), keeping the log in its own log storage, which it keeps 1 or 7 days (D479). It's a new setting for the owner (README.md part K): with it off, the table stops growing, and deleting stays quick.
- **Putting a backup of the database back** now skips anyone in a plan with someone else, and anyone the database won't remove, and says how many it left, rather than stopping.
- **Any problem sending the deletion email** leaves the deletion done, not just "emails switched off".
- **Accepted**: a phone that stays offline for longer than its sign-in token lasts (an hour) never hears "deleted"; if it has changes not sent, it keeps the plan as a phone whose sign-in ended does (D326, D478), with nothing left on the server to send them to. The test that searches every table for the person can't read pg_net's queue of emails waiting to go, which holds the deletion email itself until it's sent; that email is checked on its own.

Test: `30-download-import-delete.test.sql` (4 new checks, 66 in all: importing in place of a plan someone left still leaves the person waiting for the owner's choice, and the mark can't be read). Taking the mark out fails 2 of them.

Why: each was shown by the review with a case that went wrong, or by reading the code.

## The owner's answers to step 3h

D482 to D486 are the owner's answers to step 3h's question and their instructions, on 12 Nov 2026. Points marked "decided in step 3h" weren't in the owner's words.

### D482. Deleting an account in a shared plan: option B, "Someone who has left"

Decided by the owner 12 Nov 2026, answering step 3h's question (D475). Replaces D475's refusal and D481's lasting mark, which only kept that refusal; builds PLAN.md item 66.

- When Tom deletes his account while he shares Jess's plan, or after having been in it, his changes stay in Jess's Activity, History and "Changed by", with **"Someone who has left"** in place of his name: "Someone who has left changed Rent on Sun 1 Nov from £550.00 to £560.00.", "Someone who has left changed it from £550.00 to £560.00, Tue 10 Nov, 2:05pm", "Changed by someone who has left, 2 hours ago". His leaving says "Someone left the plan.", and a role he was given, "You changed what someone who has left can do to Can view only." Amounts on shared payments stay, as they're Jess's records too. His name, email address, account and id appear nowhere afterwards.
- **How it's built** (decided in step 3h):
  - Deleting leaves the shared plan first, exactly as Leave this plan does without a copy (D468, D473): every shared account stays with Jess as it is, she owns the plan if Tom did, and everything only Tom sees goes into a plan of his own, which is deleted with the rest.
  - Then he's **forgotten** in every plan he's been in that isn't his own (`app.forget_person`): every record he made or changed keeps everything but his id (made, changed, ticked and recorded by nobody), and so does every copy of a record in the history of changes, the server's own words for its steps ("Tom left the plan"), Jess's notices about his changes (with the device he made them on taken off), and invites. His changes are made by nobody now (`edits.person_id` empty). The database's own stamps, which would otherwise mark each record as changed just now by the person deleting, keep everything as it was while someone is forgotten (`app.forgetting`), only giving each record a new change number so Jess's phones bring it down again.
  - All of it, leaving, forgetting and deleting, is one step, or nothing ("not-deleted").
  - Jess's phone keeps a row it's sent again when who made or changed it is different, not only at a new version, so nothing she's changing at that moment clashes with it.
  - "Changed by" on a payment shows for a change by someone whose account was deleted even once Jess is alone in the plan; otherwise a plan with one person in it still shows none (D459).
- The delete screen, in a shared plan, under **Your shared plan**: "You share a plan with Jess. Deleting your account takes you out of it first, as Leave this plan does: every shared account stays with Jess as it is, and everything only you see is deleted." and "Your changes stay in Jess's Activity and History, as made by "Someone who has left". Your name, email address and account go."
- Putting a backup of the database back (D478) still leaves anyone who has been in a plan with someone else for a person to look at, as leaving needs their own sign-in.

Test: `supabase/tests/database/30-download-import-delete.test.sql` (85 checks: Tom deletes while in the plan, Sam after having left it, then Jess; after each, nothing in any table of any part of the database names their id, address, backup address or name, and Jess's plan, Activity, History, "Changed by" and notices keep the changes by nobody). Taking out any one of the three clean-ups (the changes, the history's copies, the notices) fails the checks, or the deletion refuses as a whole. `apps/web/test/unit/someone-who-left.test.ts` (5). `apps/web/e2e/together.spec.ts` test 6: on two phones, Tom (named "Tomasz", to look for it afterwards) changes Rent and deletes his account; Jess's phone, left open, shows "Changed by someone who has left", History and Activity keep the change, and neither the database nor anything on her phone holds his id, address or name. Screenshots: `docs/screens/3h/delete-shared-360-light.png`, `activity-after-delete-360-light.png`.

Why: the owner's choice: "Their changes stay in the other person's Activity, History and "Changed by", with "Someone who has left" in place of their name", with tests proving the deleted person's name, email and nickname appear nowhere afterwards.

### D483. Importing a backup into a shared plan: not now

Decided by the owner 12 Nov 2026. Confirms D477.

A backup only ever replaces a plan of one's own. Bringing a backup's accounts into a shared plan as the person's own private accounts stays an idea (PLAN.md item 69).

Why: the owner's choice.

The owner's instruction, 12 Nov 2026.

Since D482, the two test accounts of parts H and J, which share a plan, are removed with Delete my account on each phone, in either order (README.md). The SQL query of part H is kept only as a fallback, for an account that can't be signed in to any more.

Why: the owner's instruction.

The owner's instruction, 12 Nov 2026. Builds D479 and PLAN.md item 68.

Launch checklist item 9: check on resend.com how long Resend keeps the emails it has sent, and if it's longer than 30 days, change "within 30 days" in the app and the deletion email.

Why: D479 couldn't confirm Resend's 30 days from Resend's own site.

### D486. Supabase's sign-in log is no longer written to the database

The owner, 12 Nov 2026, as README.md part K asked (D481).

The owner turned off **Write audit logs to the database** in Supabase (Authentication, Audit Logs). Supabase keeps the log in its own log storage, 1 day on the free plan and 7 on the paid plan (D479), and deleting an account no longer has a growing table to read. Rows written before stay until someone they name deletes their account, which removes theirs.

Why: the owner's action.

## Made during step 3i

D487 to D503 were decided on 12 and 13 Nov 2026 in step 3i, Phase 3 checks. D488 to D494 come from its outside review, D501 and D502 from its full end-to-end run, and D503 from its checks on GitHub. Points for the owner are in `PLAN.md`, "Waiting for the owner".

### D487. A form shows only once it works

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3i, from the trace of `your-plan.spec.ts` test 9 (PLAN.md item 65).

- Every page is built ahead of time, so a phone shows its words before the code behind them has started. A form shown in that moment looked ready but wasn't: on a slow phone, someone who typed their address and tapped "Send me a code" straight away had the browser send the form itself, which loaded the page again, empty, with what they'd typed gone. The test's trace showed exactly that: the sign-in page loaded a second time as "/sign-in/?".
- Three pages showed a form in that moment: sign-in, "Get back in with your backup email" and App lock. Each now shows its heading and first line straight away, and its form once its code has started (`lib/started.ts`). On a phone that's ready, nothing looks different.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/started.spec.ts` holds each page's code back, as a slow phone does: no form shows until it arrives, then the form does. Without the fix it fails on all three pages.

Why: nothing a person types should be lost, and a test that loses a race with the page is showing a race a person could lose too.

## From the outside review of step 3i

D488 to D494 come from an outside review of everything Phase 3 added: the database rules, `save_edit`, sharing, leaving, getting back in, deleting, import and the GitHub workflows. The reviewer had no part in the code, and tried each problem on the local database. It found no way for one person to read another person's private records, over the Data API, GraphQL or live updates. It found seven problems, all fixed, each with a test that fails without its fix: `supabase/tests/database/31-phase-3-review.test.sql` (16 checks), `32-phase-3-review-joining.test.sql` (13), `apps/web/test/unit/when-it-reached.test.ts` and `scripts/workflows.test.ts`. The database changes are in `supabase/migrations/20261117100000_phase_3_checks.sql`.

### D488. Nothing a partner types can stop someone deleting their account

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3i. Changes D482's `app.without_person`.

- Deleting an account in a shared plan takes the person's id off every record they made or changed (D482). It did this by finding their id in the text of the whole record. So Tom could type text that looks like Jess's id into a payment's name or note, even with "Can view only" on his own pay, and Jess could then never delete her account: the deletion broke, and she was told "not-deleted". Renaming it back didn't help, as the history keeps a copy.
- Now the id comes off only where it's a whole value, and never out of the words people type (names and notes). What Tom typed stays exactly as he typed it.

Test: `31-phase-3-review.test.sql` part 1: Tom plants three kinds of text, and Jess still deletes her account; the same for someone who can only view.

Why: someone's right to delete their account can't depend on what someone else typed.

### D489. Joining with a plan brings its history

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3i. Builds D426 and D469.

- Someone bringing their plan into another (D426) kept their list of changes, but not the copies of what each change did, which went with the old plan. So their Activity and every payment's History lost everything from before they joined, against D469's "kept for as long as the plan exists".
- Now the copies come too, private to the person, as everything they brought is. A tick's, a one-off change's, an overtime's details' and the settings' ids are worked out from the plan they're in (D413), so their copies take the ids they have in the new plan, and History still finds them.
- Changes from before joining can't be undone from Activity afterwards: joining saved every record again. They're kept and shown, as before.

Test: `32-phase-3-review-joining.test.sql` part 1.

Why: D469.

### D490. A plan brought in names nobody who was never in it

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3i. Adds to D426 and D482.

- When Alex brings in a plan Tom had been in and left, Tom's changes in it came too, still naming Tom, in Jess's plan, where Tom never was. Tom could then never delete his account, as his deletion only looked in plans he'd been in. And Alex's Activity could have taken Jess for the one who made them.
- Now they come as made by "Someone who has left" (D482). Deleting an account also looks in any plan that has a change of the person's. Plans brought in before this are put right by the same change.

Test: `32-phase-3-review-joining.test.sql` part 2.

Why: the review showed both with a case that went wrong.

### D491. No copy in the history names someone whose account is gone

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3i. Adds to D482.

- The history notes who could read each copy at the time (D455). After Jess deleted her account, copies from when she shared an account still named her there, against D482's "her id appears nowhere afterwards". Nobody was shown it.
- Now deleting takes it off too. A copy only she could read is emptied first, so nothing private to her becomes anyone else's to see.

Test: `32-phase-3-review-joining.test.sql` part 3: after Jess shares an account and deletes hers, no copy in the history names her.

Why: D482.

### D492. Activity, History and "Changed by" go by when a change reached the account

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3i. Changes D456, D459 and D474's times; D365 and D366 stay as they are.

- A phone says when it made each change. That decides which of two changes to the same payment is newer when they clash (D365), and the server never takes a time later than its own (D366). But a phone could say a change was made as far back as it liked: Tom could change Rent and say he did it in 2001, so Jess's row wouldn't say "Changed by Tom" and the change would sit at the very bottom of her Activity.
- Now Activity, a payment's History and "Changed by" show and sort each change by when it reached the account, by the server's own clock, which no phone can set. The time the phone gave is used only for clashes.
- A change made with no signal shows the time it was sent, not the time it was made. For a change made offline and sent hours later, that's a few hours out; it's what can be trusted "in case of arguments" (D469).

Test: `31-phase-3-review.test.sql` part 2, `apps/web/test/unit/when-it-reached.test.ts`.

Why: the lasting record has to be one nobody can rearrange.

### D493. A name is checked wherever it's saved

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3i. Adds to D420.

- The name a partner sees was checked (1 to 30 characters) when someone asks to join or invites, but could also be set straight through the Data API, past the checks: the review saved one 100,009 characters long, with a character that turns words round.
- Now the database checks every name it saves, however it arrives, and the check also refuses the invisible characters that change which way words run or hide letters, so "Changed by Tom" always says what it seems to. Nothing on the phone wrote names that way, so that way in is closed.

Test: `31-phase-3-review.test.sql` part 3.

Why: a name is shown on the other person's phone, so it must be what it looks like.

### D494. The email key goes to the live database as a parameter

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3i.

- The Database workflow gave the live database Resend's key by writing it into the text of a query. If that query ever failed, the key could be written into Supabase's database logs. The preview database's step already passed it as a parameter (D408); now the live one does too.
- Nothing to do for the owner: the same secret, sent a safer way.

Test: `scripts/workflows.test.ts`: no workflow writes any of its secrets into the text of a query. It fails on the workflow as it was.

Why: a key should only ever be where it's used.

## Made during step 3i, continued

### D495. The security test: every request Tom could make

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3i. Builds brief 8.7 test 2 and ARCHITECTURE.md 7 ("brief 8.7 test 2 tries every request Tom could make").

- `apps/web/e2e/security.spec.ts`. Jess and Tom on two phones, as in brief 8.7. Jess has a private account with a payment ticked in an Update balance, a debt, a savings goal, and private history. Their real ids are read from the database.
- Four people try every way in: Tom ("Can edit"), Alex (signed in, in no plan), someone not signed in, and Tom again once Jess has made him "Can view only". With each of Jess's ids: every table through the Data API (read, change, remove, add over it), and the database's own parts, which it doesn't show; every table through GraphQL (read, change, remove); every database step the person may call, with her ids and again with the shared plan; every live-update channel (hers, the plan's, and channels named after her records' ids, private and public, listening and sending) while she changes things on her phone; the get-back-in function with her address; and Supabase's administrator routes.
- Each must give nothing of Jess's private records and change nothing of them, nor of her account. Alex and someone signed out get nothing of the plan at all, and "Can view only" changes nothing shared. Each kind of request is first seen to work on something the person may see, such as shared Rent, so nothing can pass by trying nothing.
- GraphQL isn't switched on in the local database; the test switches it on, as it can be on Supabase's own projects, so every table is tried through it too. PrePenny never uses GraphQL (a question for the owner in PLAN.md).
- Two things the test allows, on purpose: a live nudge carries its own message id, which Supabase adds to every message and which names nothing; and a wrong code typed at the get-back-in function counts towards locking that address for a day (D392).
- It runs once, at 360px: it's about the server, not the screen. In one test, the four people took 7.7 minutes on their own and ran out of 15 minutes three at a time, so each person has a test of their own, with its own set-up.

Breaking a rule on purpose, the test fails at once and names what leaked: payments readable by every member fails it at "Tom: GET series", listing Jess's ids and payment names; letting anyone hear any plan's channel fails it at Jess's own channel.

Why: brief 8.7 test 2, and the owner's list for step 3i.

### D496. Brief 8.7 tests 1 to 6, on two phones

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3i.

- `apps/web/e2e/brief-8-7.spec.ts`: one test for each of brief 8.7's first six, in the brief's own words, with Jess and Tom on two phones, at 320px and 360px in light and dark, with axe: 24 runs.
- Test 2 checks every screen of Tom's that lists accounts or payments, his Activity, everything kept on his phone, and what the server gives him; the full proof is D495's test.
- Test 3 sends one change from Tom's phone and one straight to the server, past his screens: both refused.
- Test 4: Tom saves Rent, then Jess, within a second: Jess's, the newest, is on both phones and the server, and Tom is told by name.
- Test 5: Tom leaves with a copy; Jess's Home and Coming up are word for word the same, and her payments unchanged; then her change to Rent never reaches his copy.
- Test 6: "Sign out everywhere" is "Sign out of all other devices" since D394: the server refuses the laptop's next request at once, and the laptop removes the plan within a minute.
- Brief 8.7 tests 7 to 9 are about subscriptions, in Phase 5.

Why: the owner's list for step 3i.

### D497. The six people's journeys, signed in, on two phones

Decided 12 Nov 2026 in step 3i. Builds brief Part 10 and D268.

- `apps/web/e2e/journeys-signed-in.spec.ts`. Each of the six people sets up through the screens exactly as in `people.spec.ts` and signs up. A second phone signs in and brings the plan down; Jess shares their three accounts and Tom joins on his own phone. The first phone loses its signal and adds a payment, which waits, then reaches the other phone once the signal is back; a payment added on the other phone reaches the first. Every day's balance a year ahead, on each phone, is checked against the person's fixture after each step.
- Each person runs twice, at 320px in light and at 360px in dark: both widths and both colours are covered, in half the minutes of all four. The full journeys of step 2h still run all four.
- It found a question for the owner. Each person's main pay is their own (D289), and budget periods follow it (D29). Tom has no main pay, so on his phone the joint account's £300 food month runs from the 1st, while on Jess's it runs from her payday: from 28 to 31 Oct the joint account shows £300 more on Tom's phone. Tom's phone is checked against the rule as built (it matches every day of the year exactly), and the choice is in PLAN.md.

Why: the owner's list for step 3i.

### D498. Tests that needed a second try: the causes, and no second tries

Decided 13 Nov 2026 in step 3i. Builds the owner's instruction: find the root cause, and don't skip, loosen or retry.

- **Item 47, the offline test.** Measured: the offline helper downloads all 227 of the app's files before it takes charge, about 2 seconds with one test running and 6 to 8.5 with three. The test gave it 5 seconds, Playwright's own limit for a repeated look, and failed 4 runs in 4 three at a time. Every test now waits for the helper's own "ready" and "took charge" signals (`e2e/offline-helper.ts`): 30 runs in 30 three at a time, and item 37's test still fails without its fix.
- **Item 65, your-plan test 9.** Two causes. Its trace showed the form race of D487, fixed in the app. And it did two tests' work, four phones and two setups, in one test's 30 seconds: 31 to 35 seconds three at a time, measured. Its second half is test 9b now, every check kept: 24 runs in 24 three at a time.
- **Item 35, the layout check at 200% text.** Holding the font back shows it exactly: until Figtree's files arrive the browser uses a stand-in font, wider on Linux, and at 320px with 200% text the Pay sheet and the tab bar measured wider than the screen. The check now measures once the page's font has loaded; `e2e/layout-check.spec.ts` delays the font 3 seconds and fails without it.
- **Item 41, database checks after the end-to-end tests.** Five checks counted every shared record, or compared with every change number, in the whole database. They count only their own test plan now.
- **Two-step test 3 and recovery test 4** (step 3g's "ran past its time limit", item 71): two-step test 3 spent 58 of its 66 seconds waiting for the laptop to find out it was signed out. Once two-step sign-in is on, the laptop's older sign-in gets nothing, live nudges included, so only its once-a-minute look tells it (D21, D381). Its 90 seconds left 32 for the rest, not enough three at a time. Recovery test 4 waits the minute on purpose. Both have the minute added to their overall limit; their "within a minute" checks are exactly as they were. The other "within a minute" tests hear a nudge and finish in seconds, measured.
- **The pay calculator's test 3** failed once in step 3h's full run, with no record kept of which check. It passed 156 runs in 156 here, every test in its file at both widths, four at a time, and nothing in its code waits on a race. It's left as it is; if it fails again, its trace is kept (below).
- **No second tries on GitHub.** Playwright gave every failing test a second try there, which hid the ones above. Now it gives none: a test that would need one fails, with its trace and report kept, so its cause is found.
- Item 56 ("Continue with Google" can't run on the building computer) is about that computer's network, not the test, and runs on GitHub as before.

Why: the owner's instruction.

Both were the owner's choices.

### D501. Two phones making the same change at the same moment: the newest still wins

Decided 13 Nov 2026 in step 3i, found by brief 8.7 test 4 (D496) in the full end-to-end run, once in four runs three at a time. Fixes D365 for changes that meet inside the server.

- When Tom and Jess both change Rent on the same day "just this one", both phones make the same new record: one-off changes have an id worked out from the payment (D413). `save_edit` checks a new record isn't there yet, then adds it. When the two saves met inside the server, both checked before either had finished, and the later one then hit the database's own rule that an id is used once. `save_edit` answered that as "not found", so Jess's phone took her change off as refused: her newer change was lost, and Tom's older one kept, against D365.
- Now the later save waits for the other, and when it was saved, saves nothing itself, so it's a clash, exactly as when the other save had finished first: Jess's phone works her change out again on the newest records, and as it's the newest, it's kept, and Tom is told. Someone else's private record with the same id is still answered as "not found" (D290, D299).

Test: `apps/web/e2e/brief-8-7.spec.ts`, "4, at the very same moment": Tom's save is held inside the server for 5 seconds, after it has added the change, and Jess saves meanwhile, every time. Without the fix the server keeps Tom's £571.00 and Jess's £581.00 is lost; with it, £581.00 on both phones and the server, and Tom is told by name.

Why: brief 8.7 test 4: "The newest change wins and the other person is told". Never lose a change silently (brief 4.14).

### D502. Bringing the plan down reads the notices first

Decided 13 Nov 2026 in step 3i, found by D501's test once D501 was fixed. Adds to D339 and D367.

- `plan_changes` reads every kind of record, then the person's notices. Each read sees the newest the database has at that instant, so when Jess's change was saved at the very moment Tom's phone was asking, Tom's phone could get the notice "your change was replaced" without the change that replaced it. It then wrote "Jess changed Rent on Sun 1 Nov to £571 after you changed it to £571.": Jess's amount was missing, and his own stood in for it.
- Now it reads the notices first. Every notice it gives was saved before the records were read, so the change it's about is always among them; a notice saved meanwhile comes next time, with its change.
- First built by marking `plan_changes` as reading one moment ("stable"). The database's own check refused that on GitHub, rightly, as it gives the server's clock time, which changes from moment to moment, and it was a check the building computer hadn't run. Reading the notices first needs no such mark.

Test: `supabase/tests/database/31-phase-3-review.test.sql` part 4 (the notices are read before the records), and `apps/web/e2e/brief-8-7.spec.ts`, "4, at the very same moment", which checks Tom's words name Jess's £581.

Why: a person is told exactly what happened (brief 4.14), with the right amounts.

### D503. The local Supabase's ports are kept for it alone on GitHub's machines

Decided 13 Nov 2026 in step 3i, from its pull request's checks once it was marked ready.

- "Lint, type checks, tests and the database" failed on GitHub with lint, type checks and every test passed: the local database couldn't start, as port 54322 was "already in use" on GitHub's machine.
- The cause: the database starts in the background while the install and the tests run (D300). Linux hands every outgoing connection, and every test server that asks for any free port, a number from 32768 to 60999, and the local Supabase's ports, 54320 to 54329, are in that range. So once in a while one is taken at the very moment the database starts. The earlier pushes of this pull request didn't meet it; this one did.
- Now both workflows that start the local Supabase first tell the machine to hand out only 60000 to 65000, so its ports can never be taken. Nothing is tried a second time, and nothing on a phone or in the live database changes.

Test: `scripts/workflows.test.ts`: every workflow that starts the local Supabase sets that range first, leaving 54320 to 54329 out. It fails on the workflow as it was.

Why: a check that fails because of what else the machine happens to be doing can stop the live app updating after a merge (D297).

### D504. Deleting an account on a phone says "deleted", and only that, whenever the phone looks

Decided 13 Nov 2026 in step 3i, found by shared-plan test 6 (D482) in a local run three at a time, once. A fault from step 3h, not from step 3i's changes.

- Tom deleted his account, and his phone said "Your sign-in has ended, so your plan was removed from this phone" instead of "Your account and all your data have been deleted". The account was deleted; only the words were wrong.
- The cause, from the test's recording: while the delete screen waited for the server's answer, the phone's own look at the account (every minute, and when the signal comes back) was told "deleted" and cleared the phone itself, as it does when the account is deleted on another phone. So two clear-ups ran at once, each signing the phone out. The first to finish lowered the phone's "signing out on purpose" mark while the other's sign-out was still under way, and that one then looked like a sign-in that had ended by itself. Three different pages were asked for, one after another; the last won.
- Now, while a phone asks for its own account to be deleted, nothing else on it acts on the account being deleted or the sign-in having ended: the delete screen clears the phone and says so, once. Refused, all goes on as before. Once a phone is clearing itself on purpose (signing out, or deleting), an ended sign-in is part of that and changes nothing. And the mark counts the sign-outs under way, so two at once can't clear each other's.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/your-data.spec.ts`, "4b": the account is deleted on the server and the answer held back until the phone has looked at its account twice and been told "deleted", every time. Without the fix the phone says "This account has been deleted" (the words for another phone) and stops looking; with it, "Your account and all your data have been deleted", and nothing is left on the phone. Once, at 360px in light, as it's about timing.

Why: the person is told exactly what happened (brief 4.14), and only once.

### D505. Each copy of shared-plan test 6 gives Tom its own name

Decided 13 Nov 2026 in step 3i, found with D504.

- Shared-plan test 6 runs four times, at both widths in light and dark, and ends by looking for Tom's name in every table of the database. Every copy called Tom "Tomasz", so when two copies ran at once, one found the other's Tom, still signed up. It failed once in a run of the delete tests three at a time.
- Now each copy has its own name, none inside another: Tomasz (360px light, the copy that takes the screenshots, so they don't change), Tomislav, Tomaso and Tomos. What's checked is unchanged. The other whole-database searches already look for an id or an address made for that run.

Test: the delete tests, every copy twice, three at a time: 26 passed.

Why: a test that fails because of what else is running at the same time hides real failures (D498).

### D506. On GitHub, no test runs while the local Supabase is starting

Decided 13 Nov 2026 in step 3i, from its pull request's checks after D504. Changes D300's overlap for the end-to-end parts; the database check's overlap with lint and the unit tests stays.

- Part 1 failed on GitHub: "I did overtime" (actions test 5, 320px, light) opened Home and its balance wasn't there within 5 seconds. It was the fifth test, 25 seconds into the tests, and took 5.9 seconds, where the tests around it took 2 to 4. The same third of the tests passed here, 222 in 222, and that test 48 times in 48.
- At that moment the machine was still downloading and starting the local Supabase in the background (D300): the log shows its downloads refused for a while ("Rate exceeded") and tried again. GitHub's recording of the test can't be fetched from the building computer, so what exactly held Home up isn't known. Adding and taking away networks as Docker does, every 0.3 seconds, didn't break the test here.
- What is known: three tests that failed once on GitHub, and never here, ran in those first minutes beside the starting Supabase: the browser's "network changed" note and the Pay sheet's layout (D344, PLAN.md item 35), and this one. Before step 3i each got a second try; since D498, none does, so this overlap now fails a run.
- Now each part waits until the local Supabase has started before any test. It still starts first and overlaps with installing and building the app, so the cost is the rest of its start, about a minute a part. The tests that don't use it still run if it couldn't start.

Test: `scripts/workflows.test.ts`: every workflow that starts the local Supabase in the background waits for it before its first `playwright test`. It fails on the workflow as it was.

Why: a test that fails because of what else the machine is doing hides real failures, and can stop the live app updating after a merge (D297, D498).

### D508. A shared budget's money leaves on the payday of the person who added it

Agreed 13 Nov 2026, the owner's answer to step 3i's question (option B, as recommended). Adds to D29 and D108 for budgets in a shared plan. Built in step 4a.

- In a shared plan, a budget's weeks and months follow the main pay of the person who added it (D29, D108), on every phone in the plan. Jess added the joint account's £300 food month, so it leaves on Jess's payday, Wed 28 Oct, on Jess's phone and on Tom's alike, and the joint account shows the same balance on both phones every day. Before this, Tom's phone, with no main pay of his own, took it from the 1st, so from 28 to 31 Oct the joint account showed £300 more on his phone than on hers (D497).
- Someone with no main pay who adds a budget gets D29's usual weeks and months, Monday to Sunday and from the 1st, on every phone.
- A budget in a plan of one's own is unchanged: it follows that person's own main pay, as now.
- Two details are asked before step 4a (`PLAN.md`, "Waiting for the owner"): whether a partner's phone may know the other person's paydays, the dates only, when their pay goes into a private account (D289 keeps each person's main pay private), and whose payday a budget follows once the person who added it leaves the plan or deletes their account.

Why: the owner's answer. Two people sharing an account should see the same balance in it on the same day (brief Part 3, principle 1, always true to the bank), and it needs no new setting.

### D509. GraphQL is switched off on the live database, and the Database workflow keeps it off

Agreed 13 Nov 2026, the owner's answer to step 3i's question, as recommended. Adds to D341 and D495. Built in step 4a.

- PrePenny never uses GraphQL, a second way into the database (Supabase's pg_graphql). Step 4a switches it off on the live project with a database change, which the Database workflow makes at the merge, as it makes every database change (D297).
- From then on, every run of the Database workflow checks GraphQL is still off. If it has been switched back on, from Supabase's own pages for example, the run switches it off again and says so. The preview database's job does the same, once the preview database is switched on (D411).
- The security test (D495) still switches GraphQL on in the test database and tries every table through it, so if it's ever on again, it still gives nothing private.

Why: the owner's answer. The privacy rules already apply to GraphQL (D495), but a way in that's never used is one door fewer.

## From the Phase 4 plan's checks

### D510. Tests that failed now and then: What if keeps its list before showing it, a change is counted as waiting before it shows, and a plan brought down is always rebuilt

Decided 13 and 14 Nov 2026, from the checks of the Phase 4 plan's pull request, which changes no app code. The owner chose to fix all three failures before Phase 4 starts; the fixes' own checks found a fourth.

- **What if** (D264). The practice changes were shown on screen first and kept on the phone a moment later. Closed or reloaded in that moment, PrePenny lost a practice change just made, or brought back ones just taken back or thrown away. After "Keep these changes" it could bring back changes already in the real plan, and keeping them again would have made them twice. Now each new list is kept on the phone before the screen shows it: a practice change, taking one back, throwing them away. Keep removes them from the phone first, then makes them in the real plan, and puts them back on the phone if that's refused: closed part-way, PrePenny loses a practice change at worst, never makes one twice. The words saying what happened take focus once any sheet has closed and given focus back (WCAG 2.4.3).
- **Changes waiting to be sent** (D371). After a change, the phone counted it as waiting a moment after the change showed, so for that moment More could say "Your plan is saved in your account." with the change not sent. The clock test (D378) opened More in that moment and asked the server too early, on `main`. Now a change is counted as waiting before it shows anywhere.
- **Bringing changes down** (step 3c). A phone kept the records it brought down from the account, noted them as seen, then rebuilt the plan it shows from them, in three separate saves. Closed or reloaded before the rebuild, it showed the plan without them, and as it had noted them as seen it never asked for them again: it stayed behind until some other change arrived. The shared-goal test met it once on GitHub and once on the building computer, when Jess's page reloaded 86 milliseconds after her phone brought Tom's £50.00 down. Now the records and "seen" are kept in one save with "rebuild due", which only the rebuilt plan's own save takes off, so the next look at the account rebuilds, though nothing new arrives. The same for a whole plan brought down from the account.
- **Kofi's setup.** Screen 3 stayed after Next once on GitHub, in dark mode at 360px, and once in 130 runs on the building computer: alone, beside other tests, three at a time and with recordings on. Its cause isn't known, so nothing is guessed at. If it fails again, its message in GitHub's log lists the whole screen, whether Next was busy and what had focus. GitHub's log can be read from the building computer, and its recordings can't (D506).

Tests:

- `apps/web/e2e/what-if.spec.ts`, two new tests. The first keeps the phone's storage busy for 2 seconds, as a slow phone can, then reloads straight after a practice change, a take-back and throwing them away. The second checks that Keep removes the practice changes from the phone before the undo bar says they're in the plan, and that a reload then has none, with the real plan showing the change once. Both failed 3 runs in 3 without the fix.
- `apps/web/test/unit/sync-count.test.ts` fails without the fix: once `syncSoon` is done, the count includes the change just queued.
- `apps/web/e2e/your-plan.spec.ts` test 16: a second phone's rebuild is cut off, as closing PrePenny would, just after it brought a change down; opened again, it shows the change. It failed 3 runs in 3 without the fix, stuck on the old balance, and passes 6 in 6 with it at both widths.

Why: a test that fails now and then hides real failures and can stop the live app updating after a merge (D297, D498, D506), and two of the three were small faults in the app itself.

## The owner's instructions for step 4a

### D514. Step 4a: how it's built and tried

Agreed 14 Nov 2026, the owner's instructions for step 4a. Adds to D508, D509, D512 and D513, which the owner confirmed as written.

- The Phase 4 plan is approved as written (D511). The answers to Phase 4's questions 2 and 3 are D512 and D513, and they're off the list waiting for the owner.

If switching it off needs a permission the key doesn't have, the pull request says so under "What you need to set up", checked against Supabase's own list, before it's merged.
- The security tests prove that a partner never receives anything about the other's pay but its dates, that both phones show the same balance for every shared account, every day, a year ahead, and that GraphQL gives nothing once it's off. Nothing about money, and no email address, goes into a log or an error.
- The end-to-end tests run at 320px and 360px, in light and dark, with axe, against a local Supabase, with two people on two phones: a shared budget added by each partner, the balances matching on both phones, the "your paydays" message, and the person who added it leaving. Database tests cover everything new.

## Made during step 4a

### D515. A phone looks again as soon as it's listening for nudges

Decided 14 Nov 2026 in step 4a, found by its two-phone test. A fault from step 3c (D339), not from step 4a's changes.

- When PrePenny opens on a signed-in phone, it looks at the account once, then starts listening for the live nudges. A change the other phone saved in between sent its nudge before this phone could hear it, so it showed here only at the next once-a-minute look. In step 4a's test, Jess's page loaded again just as Tom's meals out budget went, and her phone showed the joint account £60.00 different for up to a minute: four runs in eight missed a 30-second window.
- Now, as soon as the phone is listening, it looks once more, and again whenever it starts listening anew, as after a lost signal. A change saved before that look is brought down by it; one saved after sends a nudge the phone hears.

Test: `apps/web/e2e/shared-budgets.spec.ts`, whose two phones must agree within 30 seconds, well inside the minute: eight runs in eight with the fix.

Why: a change on one phone should reach the other within seconds (brief 4.14), and a check that waits on a minute's chance hides real failures (D498).

### D516. How a partner's phone gets the paydays a shared budget follows

Decided 14 Nov 2026 in step 4a. Builds D508, D512 and D514.

- **The database works them out, and nobody else writes them.** A new table, `paydays`, holds one row for each part of a person's main pay that changes its dates (D14, D49, D522), while that person is in the plan, has a main pay, and a shared budget there follows their paydays. Each row has when the pay starts, how often it comes, when it ends, its weekend and bank holiday rule, the payment number it starts at after a split, and the paydays moved (from and to) and removed. It has no column for an amount, a name, a note, an account, a job or a link, and its id is worked out from the plan and the pay's own id, which it never shows. A schedule written with anything else in it gives the dates only.
- **Kept up to date by itself.** Whenever a budget, a person's settings, their membership, their main pay's series or its one-off changes are saved, by anyone or any server step (sharing, leaving, joining, undo, deleting an account), the database works out that person's paydays again once the change is complete, saves only what changed, with a new change number, and nudges the plan's phones, so a partner's phone hears at once even when the change was to private pay.
- **Only while needed, as D29 says.** A weekly budget follows the weekday of pay that comes every week or every few weeks; a monthly one, pay that comes every month. A pay-later card follows its bill, and irregular pay gives Monday weeks (D14), so neither needs paydays. When nothing needs them any more, each row is left with nothing in it, not even whose it was, so phones take it off and nothing names a person who has gone (D482).
- **Who reads them:** everyone in the plan, with "Can view only" too, while their sign-in session exists. Nobody outside the plan, and nobody signed out. No phone can add, change or remove them, through the Data API or `save_edit`.
- **The phone** reads them with the plan's other shared records, keeps them with the plan in its own storage, one for each person, and gives them to the engine with the budgets that name that person (`followsPayOf`, `otherPaydays`). The engine works out that person's paydays exactly as their own phone does. The phone never sends them back, and a backup leaves them out, with whose paydays each budget follows: an imported plan is the importer's own (D477).
- **One rare case.** The database looks at every part of a person's main pay, and the engine at the part in force today, as D108 says. Someone whose pay changes from monthly to weekly, say, with a weekly shared budget, gives a partner's phone their paydays before that budget starts following them, on the day the pay changes. The person is told when they add it, by the database's own rule (D522).

Tests: `supabase/tests/database/33-shared-budget-paydays.test.sql` (77 checks, twelve rules each broken on purpose to see them fail); `packages/engine/test/unit/shared-budget-paydays.test.ts`; `apps/web/test/unit/shared-budget-paydays.test.ts`; `apps/web/e2e/security.spec.ts`, "step 4a".

Why: the owner's answer (D512): the dates only, and both phones the same. Working them out in the database, rather than on the phone of the person they're about, means they're right whoever changed what, and no phone can send more than dates.

### D517. Whose paydays a budget follows, and when they leave

Decided 14 Nov 2026 in step 4a. Builds D508 and D513.

- Each budget has `follows_pay_of`, filled in by the database: the person who saved it first. No phone can change it. Every budget saved before step 4a was given the person who added it, or for one on a private account that account's person, or, when the person who added it has left, the plan's owner, who stayed; and a new change number, so every phone brings it down again, with nothing else about it changed.
- When the person who added a shared budget leaves the plan, or deletes their account, which leaves first (D482), the leaving step makes it follow the person who stays, in the same change as "Tom left the plan.", and marks it `passed_on`, which no phone can change either. The person who stays sees it on the Budgets screen (D518). With no main pay, its months start on the 1st and its weeks on Monday (D29).
- A budget in a copy someone takes when they leave, or brings when they join, follows that person, as it's theirs.

Tests: `33-shared-budget-paydays.test.sql`; `apps/web/e2e/shared-budgets.spec.ts`.

Why: the owner's answers (D508, D513). The shared account and its budget stay with the person who stays (D468).

### D518. What people are told about whose paydays a budget follows

Decided 14 Nov 2026 in step 4a. Builds D512, D513 and D514. Every sentence is in `apps/web/strings/en-GB.ts`.

- **Before a budget is added to a shared account**, when it would leave on the person's own paydays, the sheet says above "Add budget": "Tom will see this money leave on your paydays." (with no partner yet: "Your partner will see…"). Not for a pay-later card, or a weekly budget when the pay is monthly, which don't follow paydays, and not for a private account. Exactly the database's rule (D516, D522), so the person is told whenever their paydays will reach their partner's phone.
- **Before an account is shared**, when budgets on it leave on the person's paydays, the sheet that asks first names them: "Tom will also see Food leave on your paydays."
- **Before an invite**, when shared budgets leave on the person's paydays, as for someone who stays after their partner left: "Whoever joins will see Food leave on your paydays."
- **On the Budgets screen**, for a shared budget that follows paydays: on Tom's phone, "Leaves on Jess's paydays."; on Jess's, "Tom sees this leave on your paydays.", which comes before the note below once someone new is in the plan.
- **Once the person who added it has left**, on the phone of the person who stays: "The person who added this has left the plan, so it now leaves on your paydays.", or "…so each month of it now starts on the 1st." or "…so each week of it now starts on Monday." It names nobody, as the person who left may have deleted their account (D482).

Tests: `apps/web/e2e/shared-budgets.spec.ts`, at 320px and 360px in light and dark, with axe. Screenshots: `docs/screens/4a`.

Why: the owner's instruction: the person adding such a budget is told plainly first (D514), and the note when it's passed on says so plainly (D513).

### D519. GraphQL is switched off, and the Database workflow keeps it off

Decided 14 Nov 2026 in step 4a. Builds D509.

- **How:** as Supabase's own guide to pg_graphql gives it, `drop extension if exists pg_graphql;`, read on 14 Nov 2026 from Supabase's documentation in its own repository (`apps/docs/content/guides/database/extensions/pg_graphql.mdx`), as supabase.com itself couldn't be opened from the building computer. The database's own `app.keep_graphql_off()` does it, and says `switched-off` or `off`; only the database's own roles can run it. Step 4a's database change runs it once, through the Database workflow, as every database change goes.
- **Kept off:** every run of the Database workflow, after the database changes, runs `app.keep_graphql_off()` through Supabase's database query route. If GraphQL was on again, it's switched off, and the run says "GraphQL was on". If the check itself fails, the run fails, and README.md says how to switch it off by hand. The preview database's job does the same, once it's switched on (D411).
- **The key needs nothing new.** Supabase's own description of its API (`api_v1_openapi.json`, read on 14 Nov 2026) says the database query route needs `database_read` and `database_write`, which is "Database: Read-write" on the token page, one of the eleven the key already has (D406). Switching it off through the Data API's settings, by taking `graphql_public` off its list, would need "Data API Config: Read-write", which the key doesn't have, so it isn't done that way. `graphql_public` stays on the list: with pg_graphql gone, Supabase's own placeholder answers only "pg_graphql extension is not enabled."
- **Tests:** the security test (D495) still switches GraphQL on in the test database and tries every table through it, then switches it off with `app.keep_graphql_off()` and checks every table, read and changed, gives nothing of the plan at all, shared Rent included, for each of the four people, only "pg_graphql extension is not enabled.". The security tests take turns at this (`e2e/graphql-turn.ts`), so one switching it off never meets another trying it on. `33-shared-budget-paydays.test.sql` checks the function, and `scripts/workflows.test.ts` that both workflows run it after the database changes.

Why: the owner's answer (D509) and instruction (D514).

### D520. Step 4a's checks of the six people, the security test and the new tests

Decided 14 Nov 2026 in step 4a.

- **The six people signed in** (D497): Tom's phone, with no main pay, is now checked against exactly the balances Jess's phone shows, every account, every day for a year, where step 3i checked it against the rule as built then.
- **The security test** has a test of its own for step 4a: Jess's pay, £2,147.63 into her own account, with a payday moved and another's amount and note changed, and her food month on the joint account. Every table through the Data API and GraphQL, bringing the plan down, her pay's history and everything kept on Tom's phone give the dates of her paydays and nothing else: no amount, name, note, account or id. Giving her pay's own id as a payday's id fails it at once, naming the id.
- **Two phones** (`e2e/shared-budgets.spec.ts`), at 320px and 360px in light and dark, with axe: each partner adds a budget to the joint account and is told first; both phones agree every day for a year; sharing an account with a budget on it says so first; and when Jess leaves, her food month follows Tom's paydays, or the 1st with no main pay of his own, and his phone says so.

Why: the owner's list for step 4a (D514).

### D521. Bringing the plan down reads every record at one moment

Decided 14 Nov 2026 in step 4a, found by its two-phone test once in about 30 runs three at a time. A fault from step 3c, which step 4a made likelier.

- `plan_changes` read each kind of record in a query of its own, and each query sees the newest the database has at its own moment. A change saved between two of them came half: the records of kinds read later, without those read earlier. The phone then counted past the change numbers of what it hadn't got, and never asked for them again. Here, Jess's food month (a budget) and the paydays saved with it (read last) came as the paydays alone, and Tom's phone showed the joint account without the food month for good. It could happen before step 4a too, to any change saving two kinds of record, such as a new account and its first balance, though the moment was shorter.
- Now one query reads every kind of record and the paydays, so a change comes whole or not yet, and the next look brings it. The notices are still read first, before it (D502).

Test: `apps/web/e2e/shared-budgets.spec.ts` test 3 holds the server for 2 seconds while it reads Tom's budgets, and saves Jess's food month in that moment, every time. Read in one go, Tom gets neither the budget nor the paydays, then both; with the old reading he got the paydays alone, and the test fails.

Why: a change on one phone must reach the other whole (brief 4.14), and never be lost without anyone being told.

### D522. From the outside review of step 4a

Decided 14 Nov 2026 in step 4a. An outside review of the step found eight things to change, all fixed, each with a check that fails without its fix.

- **Budgets from before step 4a reach every phone.** The database filled in whose paydays each budget follows with a new change number but the same version, and a phone keeps a row it brings down only at a new version, so phones that had the plan kept the old budget, and counted past it. Now each gets a new version too (`app.fill_in_budget_follows()`).
- **Two people with the same main pay**, such as pay into the joint account, each have paydays of their own: a row's id is worked out from the person as well as the pay, so one stopping never takes away the other's.
- **A split that changes only an amount, a name or a note shows nothing.** Parts of a person's main pay that carry on each other's dates exactly (the same schedule and weekend rule, the next payment numbers, the same days of the week or months) are given as one row, so a partner's phone never learns that something else changed, or when. And the paydays are worked out once a change is complete, when it's saved, so a change made in several steps shows nothing of its half-way states, not even a new change number. A split that changes the dates shows the new dates.
- **A budget that ended over two months ago**, whose last month is over, no longer gives a partner's phone its person's paydays; one still in its last month does.
- **The sentence before a budget is shared follows exactly the database's rule**, any part of the person's main pay, not only the part in force today, so they're told whenever their paydays will reach their partner's phone (D518).
- **Two saves at the same moment wait in turn.** Working out paydays takes the plan's shared change counter first, as every save of a shared record does, before any payday, so a change of Tom's to a shared budget and a change of Jess's to her own pay can't each wait for the other. And a plan being deleted, whose rows go one by one, is passed over at once.
- **The Budgets screen** says "Sam sees this leave on your paydays." once someone new is in the plan, before the note that it was passed on.
- **The tests:** a check that a schedule's other words never reach Tom now also checks its dates do; test 3 waits until the server is really held, rather than a fixed 0.7 seconds; and a database index finds a person's main pay by its family.

One thing the review raised needs the owner: when Tom changes Jess's pay-later card budget into one that leaves at the start of each month, it starts following Jess's paydays, so Tom's phone gets them, and Jess isn't asked first (`PLAN.md`, "Waiting for the owner", question 23).

Tests: `33-shared-budget-paydays.test.sql` (77 checks, the four new rules each broken on purpose to see them fail); `apps/web/test/unit/shared-budget-paydays.test.ts`; `apps/web/e2e/shared-budgets.spec.ts`.

Why: a strict outside review before every stop (CLAUDE.md), and the owner's rule: the dates only (D512).
